#1. Man Utd board sympathise with Michael Carrick amid worrying slump

Without a vote of confidence and knowing when the next win will come for Manchester United, Michael Carrick’s job at Manchester United is uncertain.

Michael Carrick suffered his heaviest defeat as Manchester United head coach on Wednesday night as the Reds collapsed to a 4-1 defeat in the Champions League against Como. It was a dire display, with Joshua Zirkzee scoring a mere consolation goal in the first minute of stoppage time.

Most of the away end left early as the rain belted down in Como. Carrick acknowledged those who stayed until the end with an apologetic gesture. To make matters worse, Bruno Fernandes spoke out against the tactics instructed by Carrick’s staff and, therefore, threw the head coach under the bus.

There has been no shortage of reports on Thursday morning, with the biggest publications eager to get the scoop at Old Trafford, where there is no shortage of scrutiny at the moment.

Who do you want to see as Manchester United manager?

Last week, Lisandro Martinez called out the board, including Jason Wilcox, but now the suits have appeared to have had their say – at least through briefing the press in the hours following United’s humiliating defeat to Como.

The result means United have just two wins from their first 11 games across all competitions this season. That form isn’t sustainable, and Carrick knows it.

According to The Times, United’s hierarchy has been left shocked by the poor standards shown by the players on the pitch in Italy. It is also noted that the board feel they should take a portion of the blame for the slump in form that has left Carrick fighting to save his job.

The report doesn’t clarify whether Carrick is certain to be in charge when United host Bournemouth in the Premier League on Sunday. However, it does state that senior officials have a “certain amount of sympathy” for the United head coach.

Chief executive Omar Berrada, the director of football, Jason Wilcox, and other senior figures, including co-owners Jim Ratcliffe, will decide whether Carrick should remain in charge of the team for the next match in three games.

However, sympathy isn’t enough when you are managing United. It doesn’t change the fact that the buck stops with Carrick.

Como breezed past United on the night, made light work of Carrick’s side, and the players are being accused of not matching their opponents’ intensity each week. For instance, the Italian side ran 4.2km more than United. Their progressive coach, Cesc Fabregas, even told his players to stop running in the final 10 minutes.

#2. Youri Tielemans exposes painful Man United reality after embarrassing Como defeat

Manchester United midfielder Youri Tielemans has admitted that he and his teammates simply could not compete with Como during Wednesday’s 4-1 Champions League hammering.

United’s woes went from bad to worse as they were put to the sword by Como at the Giuseppe Sinigaglia Stadium.

Nico Paz gave the hosts the lead inside two minutes when he cut through the defence to curl the ball home. Como doubled their advantage shortly after the break through Anastasios Douvikas.

Paz all but put the game to bed for Como, snatching the ball from Carlos Baleba and charging up the pitch to tuck it beyond Senne Lammens. Defender Jacobo Ramon made it four before Joshua Zirkzee scored a consolation goal for the Red Devils in injury time.

The finger-pointing began after the final whistle: Michael Carrick criticised his players for a lack of effort, while Bruno Fernandes took aim at the manager’s tactics.

Carrick later hit back at Fernandes in his post-match press conference.

Tielemans spoke to MUTV and conceded that United were lacking in every single department, as he called on everyone to do better.

The Belgian told club media, “We played very poor and conceded after two minutes and then it puts you on the back foot straight away. We want to be positive and go forward but this wasn’t the way.”

“There was more said at half-time about us trying to get back into the game, but they score just after half-time, so it wasn’t a great atmosphere in the changing rooms.”

“We need to move on as we have games coming. Everything was missing tonight. We were disjointed as a team, we didn’t play well together, our press was off and when we were on the ball, we could not make the right decisions, so you get frustrated with the game.”

“They [Como] played around us too easily, it was a poor night.”

Tielemans issued an apology to the United faithful.

“They travel all the way and don’t want to see this; the only thing we can do now is apologise. We can apologise as much as we want but we need to put the performances out on the pitch and, at the moment, we aren’t doing that. It’s tough times for them supporting the club and we have to fight as players.”

United are next in action on Sunday when they host Bournemouth.

#3. ‘Angry’ Carrick on the brink as Fernandes hits out at Manchester United tactics

Manager apologises to fans after 4-1 defeat in Como

Captain criticises ‘decision from the staff’ not to press

Michael Carrick’s future as Manchester United manager is in the balance after a humiliating 4-1 Champions League defeat by Como on Wednesday night.

Before Bournemouth’s visit to Old Trafford on Sunday his future may be discussed by Jason Wilcox, the director of football, Omar Berrada, the chief executive, and Sir Jim Ratcliffe, the co-owner who controls sporting policy.

On a torrid night an “angry” ­Carrick insisted afterwards that he had not given his position any thought and was forced to defend himself against Bruno Fernandes after his captain questioned the manager’s tactics of not pressing against Cesc Fàbregas’s team. Carrick also criticised his side for not running. The defeat by Como in the third Champions League group game leaves United with only two wins all season from 11 games. ­Carrick’s side are in 13th place in the Premier League and were eliminated from the Carabao Cup last month.

The 45-year-old was asked if he fears for his job. “Listen, I’ve not given my own position any thought,” he said. “[My thoughts are] for the supporters – I was one of them watching that and I wasn’t happy at all.”

Carrick was then asked if he could walk away following the latest defeat in Europe. “No. It’s not about me right now and my feelings in that regard,” the manager said. “It’s more of as a team and as a football club, what we want to look like, what we should look like. And as I said, the results can come and go at times, but you got to do it in a certain way. And that wasn’t it.”

700 United sup­porters travelled. At the end around 200 remained and Carrick went to ­apologise to them, noticeably ­ignoring his players. After United conceded Nico Paz’s second-minute goal, Carrick’s men were unable to mount any resistance. By 57 minutes Como led 3-0, due to Tasos Douvikas’s strike and Paz’s second, before Jacobo Ramón completed the scoring for the Italian side on 76 minutes. Joshua Zirkzee netted in added time for the visitors.

Fernandes openly challenged ­Carrick’s strategy during the match. “It’s a decision from the staff to not press man-to-man and sometimes leave space behind and try to get the ball, wait for them, but sometimes when you give space to these teams they get confidence,” he said.

The captain was asked if Carrick’s tactics were wrong. “That’s on the manager and staff to see, not me,” he said. “Sometimes you have to be more brave under pressure, take the risk of pressing high and making them uncomfortable. As you saw with them, the centre-back was coming to mark me in my own half. We need to do that as a team but we were unable to.”

Fernandes’s criticism was put to his manager after the match. “We actually half-pressed man-to-man the second half and then conceded a goal from that,” Carrick said.

Kick off your evenings with the Guardian's take on the world of football

“Whatever system you play, whatever tactics you play, if you don’t run, and you don’t run forward, and you don’t run back, you ain’t going to win.”

Carrick was asked how he can raise the mood before Bournemouth’s visit. “I’m bitterly disappointed, I’m frustrated; more than frustrated, I’m really angry. But you’ve got to move on. You got to deal with what’s next and see what that looks like.

“At this moment in time, there’s a lot to do before that next game to get the team ready because that was a difficult night for everyone. I have to apologise to the supporters.”

#4. Cesc Fabregas revelation humiliates Manchester United even further after rout

Cesc Fabregas says he regrets telling his Como players to ease up after they conceded a late consolation goal in their 4-1 Champions League victory over Manchester United.

Como dismantled Man Utd in embarrassing fashion on Wednesday night, taking a four-goal lead after 76 minutes thanks to efforts from Tasos Douvikas and Jacobo Ramon, as well as a brace from Nico Paz.

The Serie A side made the perfect start when Paz netted after just 118 seconds, and United were unable to respond, though they grabbed a goal back in second-half stoppage time through Joshua Zirkzee.

Paz’s opening goal meant United were the first English team in history to concede goals in the first two minutes of consecutive major European matches, following last week’s listless 2-0 loss at Atletico Madrid, and manager Michael Carrick is now under severe pressure.

Fabregas, meanwhile, further underlined his credentials as one of Europe’s brightest young managers, but admitted he was not happy with himself for telling his players to conserve energy after Ramon’s fourth goal.

“I believe that tactically speaking the team played a rather good game,” he said.

“I regret maybe the last 10 minutes when I was the one telling the team to slow down the pace because there was another game and maybe with a 4-0 lead they could have managed something.

“I told Tasos Douvikas not to run anymore, to stay in the middle and maybe that is what I regret, because I’m the first one who always pushes the fight until the end. That goal hurt me.”

Fabregas has been linked as a potential replacement for Carrick at Old Trafford, but he dismissed those questions ahead of the United game.

"I won't answer that question because I think that's not a very nice question," the former Arsenal, Chelsea and Barcelona midfielder said at his pre-match press conference on Tuesday.

"I don't think that shows enough respect to a great footballer and manager like Michael Carrick at the helm of a wonderful Manchester United side."

United now sit 20th in the Champions League table after their thrashing by Como, and Carrick went over to United’s 700-strong away support at the Stadio Giuseppe Sinigaglia - many of whom had already left - at full-time to apologise.

"It's a decision from the staff to not press man to man and sometimes leave space behind and try to get the ball, but sometimes when you give too much the ball to these teams, they get confidence," Fernandes said.

#5. Cesc Fabregas admits telling Como to ‘slow down’ in thrashing of Man United

Please refresh the page or navigate to another page on the site to be automatically logged inPlease refresh your browser to be logged in

Michael Carrick apologised to the Manchester United fans for the 4-1 defeat but Fabregas wanted more

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

Cesc Fabregas has admitted to telling his Como team to “slow down” during their thrashing of Michael Carrick’s Manchester United in the Champions League.

Como led 4-0 after 76 minutes in Naples after a sublime double from Nico Paz and efforts from Anastasios Douvikas and Jacobo Ramon punished a woeful display from the visitors.

But Fabregas revealed he regretted ordering his players to take their foot off the gas, with Joshua Zirkzee then scoring a late consolation for Carrick’s beleaguered team.

It remained United’s joint-heaviest Champions League defeat, which placed further pressure on Carrick amid a seven-game winless run in all competitions.

“I believe that tactically speaking the team played a rather good game,” said Fabregas, who led Como to the club’s first ever Champions League qualification last season.

“I regret maybe the last 10 minutes when I was the one telling the team to slow down the pace because there was another game and maybe with a 4-0 lead they could have managed something.

“I told Tasos Douvikas not to run anymore, to stay in the middle and maybe that is what I regret, because I’m the first one who always pushes the fight until the end. That [Zirkzee] goal hurt me.”

It remained a chastening night for United and Carrick, however, with the head coach coming under criticism from his captain Bruno Fernandes for his tactics in northern Italy.

“I’ve not given my position any thought,” Carrick told a press conference. “It’s [about] the supporters, I was one of them, and I didn’t like what I saw.

"I'm really angry but you've got to move on and deal with what's next and see what that looks like. At this moment in time, there's a lot to do before the next game to get the team ready, because that was a difficult night for everybody.”

#6. Matthijs De Ligt is already a big fan of 39-year-old manager Man Utd could now hire

It may not be long before Manchester United’s search for a new manager begins.

Michael Carrick hasn’t got to grips with the job since being made the permanent manager of Manchester United, and, sadly, it feels like he could soon be served his P45.

As you can imagine, United are already being linked with a number of different managers, and one interesting name that has re-emerged is Julian Nagelsmann.

Nagelsmann has been linked to United before, and with the 39-year-old currently out of work, now could be an opportune time to finally make the move to Old Trafford.

Interestingly, Nagelsmann already has two supporters within the United dressing room.

Who do you want to see as Manchester United manager?

Both Matthijs De Ligt and Noussair Mazraoui have played under Julian Nagelsmann before at Bayern Munich.

Nagelsmann was actually the coach who signed both players, and upon joining the German giants, both De Ligt and Mazraoui had good things to say about the manager.

“I was very impressed that the entire Bayern management made me feel from the very beginning that the club really wanted me. Everything just clicked. Julian Nagelsmann is also a coach who has proven that he understands how to develop young players well,” said de Ligt, via Sport1.“FC Bayern’s attacking style of play definitely suits me and was indeed one of the reasons for my decision to move to Munich. Bayern plays a technically advanced style of football with extreme pressing. That matches my strengths and my understanding of the game.”

Meanwhile, Mazraoui noted that the German’s ideas really resonated with him, and that he had a fantastic feeling when he joined his team.

“I was impressed by the way Julian Nagelsmann, Hasan Salihamidžić, Marco Neppe and Oliver Kahn spoke to me. I could immediately see something in their ideas. When the coach told me what he expected from me and how he imagined me in the team, it was a kind of visualisation for me. It was the best feeling you can have,” he explained, via Bavarian Football Works.

Unfortunately, in the end, things didn’t work out for Nagelsmann at Bayern Munich as he was sacked less than a year after signing Mazraoui and De Ligt, but De Ligt did have a fantastic record under the former Bayern boss.

Unfortunately, the pressure at Bayern Munich is so high that a handful of defeats can cost you your job, and Nagelsmann unfortunately suffered for that.

De Ligt could well reunite with his former manager at Old Trafford, and they will be hoping to continue what was an impressive winning record at Bayern.

#7. Ruud Nijstad: Man United make transfer call on FC Twente’s highly-rated defensive prodigy

Manchester United are reportedly eyeing a move for FC Twente defender Ruud Nijstad, a new report has claimed.

Having focused their attention on bolstering the attacking department during the summer, United are expected to prioritise other positions in 2027.

United chose not to sign defenders, even with a glaring need for a left-back and a centre-back. It already looks a costly mishap, and internally there is an acceptance that it was a massive mistake given the club’s woeful form this term.

The Red Devils are on the hunt for a long-term successor to Luke Shaw. Harry Maguire and Lisandro Martinez’s respective deals are also set to expire next summer and it’s unclear whether United will extend their stays.

Maguire is in visible decline, while Martinez is believed to be eager to leave Old Trafford as a free agent.

Then there is Matthijs de Ligt, who just returned from a long-term injury and endured a nightmare outing against Como in the Champions League.

According to The Athletic, United are keeping tabs on Nijstad.

The newspaper reveals that Nijstad is on the radar of several top European teams, including Michael Carrick’s side.

The 18-year-old has made eight Eredivisie appearances so far this term, scoring one goal. He has played the entire 90 minutes in every game.

Towering at around 1.95 metres, Nijstad blends an imposing physical presence with the technical class demanded of a modern left-sided centre-back.

The left-footed defender is under contract until 2029, meaning Twente are in an advantageous position should United initiate talks.

United will also have to deal with former manager Erik ten Hag, who is currently Twente’s the technical director of the Dutch outfit.

Carrick and his players are next in action on Sunday when they host Bournemouth at Old Trafford.

The Peoples Person Reliability Assessment Overall credibility: 8/10

✅ Reported by a very reliable source. ✅ The player fits United’s tactical and age profile.

We believe United are indeed keeping tabs on Nijstad and the situation is one to keep an eye on.

#8. From Amorim to Carrick: Man Utd keep using one player as a bandage, they must stop

Manchester United were humiliated by Como, suffering a crushing 4-1 defeat at the Stadio Giuseppe Sinigaglia in the UEFA Champions League on Wednesday evening.

The Red Devils showed virtually no fight or resilience, appearing to surrender after conceding just two minutes into the contest.

Their body language was alarming, their work rate was questionable, and their performances suggested a team struggling to understand what was expected of each player.

The players must shoulder their share of the blame, but head coach Michael Carrick cannot escape scrutiny for a tactical approach that left United looking completely lost.

The 45-year-old made one particularly baffling decision by deploying Bruno Fernandes as a number nine against Como, taking the club captain away from the position where he is most effective.

Fernandes endured a dreadful outing, looking isolated and struggling to influence proceedings whenever the ball came his way.

Carrick appears to be repeating one of the biggest mistakes of his predecessor, Ruben Amorim.

The Portuguese manager frequently deployed Fernandes in central midfield during the 2025-26 campaign rather than allowing him to operate in his natural attacking midfield role.

Although the 32-year-old continued to contribute goals and assists, the overall balance of the team remained a persistent problem.

The Red Devils looked vulnerable defensively and were repeatedly exposed through the middle, highlighting the consequences of a midfield that failed to provide adequate protection.

Amorim effectively used Fernandes as a bandage to cover midfield deficiencies after INEOS failed to sign a midfielder in the summer of 2025.

The result was a team that struggled to build any sustained winning run, with Amorim eventually paying the price for the disappointing performances and being relieved of his duties.

Carrick appears to be making a similar mistake by misprofiling Fernandes and asking him to operate in a role that fails to maximise his strengths.

Benjamin Sesko is out injured and Joshua Zirkzee is simply not good enough. However, turning Fernandes into a makeshift striker is hardly a convincing solution.

The humiliating defeat to Como has intensified the pressure on Carrick, leaving his position increasingly precarious as questions mount over his tactical decisions.

Unless the former midfielder can mastermind a remarkable turnaround, his time in the dugout could be running out.

Carrick may have left it too late to restore Fernandes to his natural position.

#9. Manchester United next manager: New top candidate emerges with Michael Carrick on the brink

Julian Nagelsmann has emerged as the leading candidate to replace Michael Carrick should Manchester United opt to dispense with their under-fire head coach.

Carrick appears to be on the brink of dismissal after United were subject to a galling 4-1 Champions League defeat away at Como on Wednesday night, leaving them winless in their last seven matches across all competitions.

He seems to have lost the dressing room, too, with Bruno Fernandes publicly questioning his tactics at full-time in Como, barely a week after Lisandro Martinez’s remarkable outburst in Madrid.

While some at United are still said to be backing United, reports have emerged suggesting they are lining up candidates to replace Carrick in the event he is relieved of his duties.

The Telegraph reported on Thursday morning the former Germany and Bayern Munich boss Nagelsmann is open to replacing Carrick, while TalkSport say he has already been sounded out about the role.

Nagelsmann has been out of work since the summer, when he stepped down as head coach of the German national team, having been dumped out of the World Cup last 32 by Paraguay.

Plenty of candidates are queueing up for the job, with the managerial market in rude health. Antonio Conte, a Premier League winner with Chelsea most recently employed by Napoli, has reportedly been offered to United, but the Telegraph say he does not figure on the club’s shortlist.

United are known to be keen on Brighton’s Fabian Hurzeler, but the club acknowledge he would likely prove an expensive appointment given he agreed a new long-term contract last season.

Eddie Howe is out of work, however, and is reportedly the subject of Sir Jim Ratcliffe’s admiration. It remains to be seen, though, how much sway he will have over the decision-making process. Howe also said upon leaving Newcastle this summer that he would like to spend some time away from the game.

Aston Villa’s Unai Emery has also been tipped by The i Paper as a ‘dream candidate’ after helping Aston Villa to Europa League glory last season, though it is thought he would be tricky to pry away from Birmingham.

Como’s Cesc Fabregas had previously been mentioned as a left-field option, but the chances of him wanting to take over United after beating them 4-1 are profoundly slim.

#10. Nico Paz double sinks United 4-1 at Como as Carrick’s winless run reaches seven

Nico Paz scored twice and set up another as Como beat Manchester United 4-1 in the Champions League. Carrick's United, missing several players and with Yoro suspended, have now gone seven games without a win in all competitions.

Manchester United slipped to a heavy Champions League defeat in Italy on Wednesday evening. Como won 4-1, with Nico Paz at the centre of almost everything.

Paz struck in the second minute from a Lucas Da Cunha pass, and Como led 1-0 at the break. Anastasios Douvikas doubled the lead in the 49th minute after Paz set him up. Paz added his second in the 57th minute. Jacobo Ramón made it four in the 76th minute, assisted by Kaiki Bruno. Joshua Zirkzee replied in stoppage time from an Amad Diallo pass.

Paz is 22 years and 43 days old. No younger player has scored and assisted in the same Champions League game against United since Alan Dzagoev, according to Goal. The CSKA Moscow player did so in November 2009. Paz played 80 minutes.

Most of United’s attempts were speculative. Eight came from outside the box, against three for Como. Como’s expected goals figure was 2.12, against 1.11 for the visitors. The hosts also had 55% of the ball and completed 597 accurate passes to United’s 474.

The numbers are consistent with a side that created the better chances while United shot from distance. United collected three yellow cards, for Andrey Santos, Carlos Baleba and Shea Lacey. Marc-Oliver Kempf was Como’s only booking.

United arrived without Dorgu (hamstring), Heaven (illness), Mainoo, Martínez (thigh), Šeško (shin), Shaw and Rashford. Yoro was suspended. Matthijs de Ligt made his first start in 325 days after back problems. He only got in because of a wider injury crisis at the club, Sky Sports reported. Como were also short, missing Chalobah (ankle), Diao (thigh), Kean (shin) and Jesús Rodríguez.

The attack is struggling as well. Bruno Fernandes has been directly involved in only one goal in his last nine United appearances. Leeds, Tottenham and Fulham all held United to 1-1 draws. Brighton beat them 3-2.

— Manchester United (@ManUtd) October 21, 2026

Sabah were beaten 4-0 in United’s European opener on 10 September. A 2-0 defeat away to Atlético de Madrid followed on 13 October. Como began their first Champions League campaign with a 4-1 home win over Leipzig, then lost 3-2 at Feyenoord. The league phase finishes on Wednesday 27 January 2027.

United host Bournemouth in the Premier League on Sunday 25 October, with kick-off at 14:00 GMT. The game was moved because Bournemouth play in Europe on the Thursday before. A trip to Chelsea follows on Saturday 31 October at 12:30. In Europe, United travel to Sporting CP on 25 November and host Leipzig on 8 December.

#11. Como 4-1 Manchester United: Champions League – as it happened

A dismal Manchester United performance ended in an emphatic defeat to Cesc Fàbregas’s completely dominant Como side

And with that, I’m off. Here’s Jamie Jackson’s match report again. Bye!

Here are some snippets from Michael Carrick’s post-match interview:

double quotation markIt was more than disappointing. I think everybody’s got to take responsibility for that. I’ll take responsibility for the overall team and performance, it stops with me and I’m fine with that. But as a supporter of this football club, to see a performance like that is unacceptable, I have to say.

I’ll look at what we’ve done. It’s the motivation and the purpose and the intensity of a game of football that you have to bring. We didn’t bring that today. You can’t choose when you want to perform or you don’t, when you give absolutely everything and when perhaps you don’t.

I care an unbelievable amount about the supporters. They’re the heartbeat of this football club and always will be. I’ve got to apologise for the performance. Everything they’ve done to get here tonight, for them to watch that performance tonight, I feel for them.

Tonight’s not about me. It’s about digesting the game, and how it went. That’s the most important thing right now.

I assume this is a wave of apology rather than farewell.

Thankfully we can rely on Jamie Jackson, who is lakeside for us tonight. And here is his match report!

In sight of the Alps, a boat ride from George Clooney’s villa, Manchester United will hope, in what has been a terrible start to the season, that things do not get any worse.

They can’t – for the team’s prospects, and Michael Carrick’s job security, because witnessed here by Lake Como was a humiliation of the three-times European champions by Cesc Fàbregas’s competition debutants, as United suffered their worst defeat in continental football since 1994.

By 57 minutes this was over, as Como were 3-0 ahead, and Nico Paz had destroyed Carrick’s men with a scintillating display featuring two goals and the assist for Tasos Douvikas’s strike.

It may take Manchester United fans some time to get over this performance/result, but TNT Sports have moved on pretty quickly. As I suppose they had to, given that loads more games start in five minutes, but doesn’t help me bring you the hottest reaction from a windswept, drenched Como.

The fun doesn’t end here! The 8pm games include Bayern Munich v Arsenal, which Scott Murray is monitoring here:

And also Aston Villa v Viking, Real Madrid v RB Leipzig and quite a lot more, which Sarah Rendell has to keep up with here:

The good news for Michael Carrick is that Dimitar Berbatov, on TNT Sports, doesn’t say that United now have to beat Bournemouth on Sunday for him to stay in his job. The bad news for Carrick is that Berbatov’s reasoning is that Carrick might not still be in his job on Sunday. “I don’t think everyone was giving their best,” Berbatov says of this performance. “It didn’t look like that on the pitch. Como were having fun.”

Sunday 25 October: Bournemouth (h) Saturday 31 October: Chelsea (a) Tuesday 3 November: Roma (h) Sunday 8 November: Aston Villa (h) Sunday 22 November: Liverpool (a)

United’s players wander over to apologise to whatever flimsy remnant of the travelling support isn’t already drowning their sorrows in a nearby hostelry.

90+4 mins: Say what you like about this United performance, they completely dominated that period of stoppage time, and won it 1-0!

United do have their consolation! Amad chips the pass into the box, Zirkzee escapes from his marker and brings it down beautifully, leaving himself with a pretty easy finish which he calmly completes.

90+1 mins: There’ll be three minutes of stoppage time, or thereabouts.

89 mins: … Not like that they can’t. Lacey takes the corner, and three defenders line up at the near post to boot it clear, which one of them emphatically does.

89 mins: Amad wins United a corner. Could they end the night with a consolation?

87 mins: Lacey swiftly earns himself a booking. “The Guardian called for jeopardy in this competition. I think Man Utd have found it for you,” writes Ian Copestake. Their remaining Champions League fixtures: Roma (h), Sporting (a), Leipzig (h), Bayern Munich (h), Villarreal (a).

86 mins: Another United substitution, Lacey replacing Mbeumo.

84 mins: Kempf is booked, I think less for bringing down Amad than for being grumpy with the referee for giving the free-kick.

82 mins: The last time Manchester United lost by four goals in Europe was more than 30 years ago, against a team that included Stoichkov, Romario and some chap called Guardiola.

80 mins: Como bring on Smolcic, Ricci and Dossena, and take off Couto, Paz and Perrone.

79 mins: Amass comes on for De Ligt, whose return from injury is the silver lining to tonight’s miserable, charcoal-grey cloud for United.

78 mins: Como are hunting for a fifth, but Paz shoots too close to Lammens.

That is delicious. Ramon, the centre-back, spots Maguire creeping out of position and reckons he can get into that empty space before anyone notices. And turns out he was right. Kaiki Bruno sees him sprinting into an empty penalty area and sends in a perfect, precise cross, and Ramon hammers his header home from six yards!

73 mins: Zirkzee has brought a bit of extra pace and energy to the United team, if not any improvement in control. “Well, although it is quite funny (as a Liverpool fan) to watch a club swing from one manager to another chasing a dominance they once had (ahem), how much of the current malaise can be blamed on trying to keep up with the noisy neighbours across the city?” wonders Andy Flintoff (not that one). “Feeling that they have to do that has probably accelerated that swing and made the lurches more extreme.” I suppose it can’t have helped, though it seemed to have more of a positive effect on some other teams (if not their bank balances).

69 mins: Kaiki Bruno and his exemplary caterpillar moustache comes on for Da Cunha.

67 mins: Bruno Fernandes is taken off, which feels a bit like an admission of defeat. Zirkzee replaces him.

66 mins: Amad shoots from 22 yards or so. The ball flies over Butez’s head and was probably also going over the bar but the keeper made sure.

64 mins: Como bring Caqueret on for Baturina. United have been playing for the last five minutes or so with Mazraoui on the right and Dalot on the left.

62 mins: Amad has been bright since he came on at half-time. He’s at the heart of a United attack but it ends with a blind cross, and a clearance. “Watching United at the moment, they look like a side completely in limbo, unable to move on,” writes Paul Flint. “The players surely know that Carrick is going sooner rather than later, and must be wondering about their futures. It doesn’t encourage in-game focus, however much they may be paid.”

60 mins: Tielemans has a shot from the edge of the area, but it’s more of an apology than a challenge and Butez picks it up.

59 mins: Paz tries another curler, this one from the edge of the D, but this one floats too close to Lammens.

Baleba hits a pass into Nico Paz’s shins just inside the United half and the ball runs beautifully into the Argentinian’s path, so he heads off, runs into the area, draws the keeper, and slides the ball across goal and in at the far post!

56 mins: An actually good United move ends with Mount flicking the ball towards Bruno Fernandes, but it’s intercepted.

55 mins: Como should have had a third! Paz passes to Da Cunha, who shifts onto his left foot to open up a great shooting chance, 12 yards out, but then cuts across the ball rather than curling it with his instep, and it skews the wrong side of the post!

54 mins: “I know watching United’s downfall has been fun for rival fans, I totally get that and don’t blame them for relishing it,” writes Lee Woolsey. “And I know this gets said a lot by fans about their team when they’re playing badly, but I’m sorry: this United team just look like they don’t care. They were a goal down after two minutes and the remaining 43 were an exercise in collective shoulder shrugging by a team of mercenaries assembled by a parasitic reign of witches. People DIED for this club. These players are a risible disgrace.” It’s very easy to blame the players, though. They aren’t well coached. They haven’t been well recruited. It’s the whole edifice.

52 mins: “Not having a screen to watch this on, and just going on your mbm, I don’t think that Mount hasn’t been mentioned, is he doing as little as suggested?” wonders Martin Lancon. In brief, yes. United’s midfield hasn’t done very much. There were a couple of moments in the first half when Mount exchanged passes with Bruno Fernandes and the pair briefly looked pretty classy, but they haven’t achieved anything creatively. And as I type that Mount has a rubbish shot from 30 yards.

50 mins: That’s just so easy. Paz carries the ball forwards, Douvikas runs into a massive space between De Ligt, who just didn’t see the run coming, and Maguire – the Dutchman stumbles at a crucial moment, but he was already out of the race – and finishes superbly!

A brighter restart for United, but in just over three minutes they’re two down!

46 mins: Peeeeep! The game has restarted, and United have made a double halftimely substitution, Matheus Cunha and Andrey Santos going off, Diallo and Baleba coming on.

Just one goal, and that from a wonderful shot from outside the area, does not really reflect what has not been a tightly-contested game. United had one good chance, after just a couple of minutes, and even that came after some terrible ball control from Diogo Dalot and a lucky bounce off Perrone, for whom Dalot’s inability to control a football had been not so much unpredictable as unimaginable. I’m not sure what Uefa classify as an “attack” but Como have had 16 of them, and United five.

45+2 mins: It never rains but it pours, etc.

45+1 mins: One minute of stoppage time stands between United and the hairdryer treatment (or whatever it is they get these days when they’ve been rubbish).

45 mins: An actual shot for United! Matheus Cunha gets the ball on the left-hand corner of the area, back to goal, then spins and sends a curling effort onto the roof of the net.

43 mins: More work for Lammens, this time to keep out Baturina’s 20-yarder. He can’t hold it, but Douvikas is offside again as he reaches the rebound. “It’s mindblowing,” writes Joshua Keeling, “that Diogo Dalot is a professional footballer.”

41 mins: United are saved by the offside flag! Douvikas is played through again, Lammens comes out and the striker goes over his leg … but the flag is up!

39 mins: Real chance! Yan Couto, no pressure on him, plays a lovely pass over the United defence to Douvikas, who brings it under control before wildly mishitting his shot, which skews off his shin high and wide when really he should have scored.

#12. Erik ten Hag recalls being ‘embarrassed’ by Man United after promise broken over keeping player

Please refresh the page or navigate to another page on the site to be automatically logged inPlease refresh your browser to be logged in

The Independent's journalism is supported by our readers. When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn commission.

The Dutchman, now technical director of FC Twente, accused the club’s leadership of breaking promises to him

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

Erik ten Hag has revealed he felt “very embarrassed and disappointed” after Manchester United failed to keep a promise not to sell a key player during his time at the club.

The Dutchman, who was sacked in October 2024, also backed current head coach Michael Carrick to remain in post in a wide-ranging interview with The Athletic.

Now technical director of FC Twente, his boyhood team, Ten Hag appeared to have no hard feelings about his time at Old Trafford, telling the outlet he had “such wonderful memories”.

But he was critical of the leadership since Jim Ratcliffe’s takeover in late 2023, with the Ineos era failing to bring the club back to its glory days. He said: “The takeover process brought a lot of noise, uncertainty with the people, and disruptions. It was difficult to work, that’s clear. It was very complicated.”

The Dutchman nearly lost his job shortly before the FA Cup final the following summer but the 2-1 victory over Manchester City gave him a lifeline, with a one-year extension in his contract triggered two weeks after.

He said that during crisis talks before the extension was agreed, “They promised me loyalty, they promised me backing, they promised communication, that we get aligned. We spoke about the issues. But the promises, they didn’t keep.”

“That’s why I got disappointed. In Ibiza [during talks], we agreed. I said, ‘No selling Scott McTominay’. We would go aligned in the transfer window. I make an argument, a reason: we don’t have scoring abilities. Scott brings scoring abilities and great mentality. He has United in all his veins. So when they bring up the subject, ‘No’. And in the end they sold him. I got, of course, very embarrassed and disappointed.”

McTominay was sold to Napoli that summer for £25.7m and has flourished there, rapidly establishing himself as a key figure in the squad which won the Serie A title in his first year at the club. United bought Manuel Ugarte with the proceeds from the Scot’s sale.

Ten Hag also said there were issues regarding the exact nature of his role, foreshadowing Ruben Amorim’s explosive outburst over being the manager of United rather than the head coach, despite head coach being his formal title.

Ten Hag said: “After the first meeting (at Carrington), [Ratcliffe] started talking about the structure in the football club, how the manager should be head coach. I said, ‘OK, let’s discuss, but my contract is different’. I never heard anything.

“I always want to cooperate in a club — you can’t do this on your own. You need to find a strategy about philosophy, game model, about team profile, individual profiles. That is what I was used to, but with them there was no communication.”

He said dismissing the under-pressure Carrick after a dismal start to the season was “100% not the solution” and put more of the onus on the wider leadership at the club, saying: “The leadership, you need their competence, you need the right strategy. What is your identity? What are the work methods? You need the right structures and quality people. Then you need time.”

#13. Carrick’s Bryan Mbeumo change vs Como was pointless, Lacey opportunity can’t hide desperation

Michael Carrick can do nothing right at this moment, and even the good thing he did vs Como came at the cost of changing the only thing that was working.

The 4-1 loss to Como was the kind of performance that gets a manager sacked, so if Michael Carrick has reached this point, he has already done plenty of wrong things.

He did do the right thing against Como by giving a chance to Shea Lacey again, albeit it was too late to change anything at that point.

However, even that can’t hide the mistake he had already made by changing the only thing that was working so far this season.

Man Utd have hardly been the gold standard of club sides this poor season, but at least they could count on one constant.

Bryan Mbeumo, playing as the striker, missed some clear-cut chances, but he has regularly looked like the most dangerous man on the pitch for the club.

It has been as simple as the fact that if Mbeumo doesn’t score, United don’t score, which is why Carrick’s decision vs Como was so puzzling.

Who do you want to see as Manchester United manager?

In his continued (and ill-advised) efforts to fit both Youri Tielemans and Bruno Fernandes in the same XI, Carrick created a tactical mess of player positions.

It took away Mbeumo from the position he has looked the most potent at – striker – and stationed him out wide on the right to create chances for nobody in particular.

The result was an expectedly disjointed performance where none of the forward players quite knew where they were supposed to be on or off the ball.

It even hurt United’s defence, as there was no cohesion in pressing, with Mbeumo often covering both, the left-back and the centre-back, while pressing the opposition.

For all the talk of Carrick not wanting to play Amass and Lacey because this was an unstable team, the pair came on when the team had already conceded four goals. How’s that for unstable?

It’s clear that the Lacey sub was the last throw of the dice from Carrick, who has come across as scared and safety-first, two words that should never be attached to a Man Utd manager.

A brave manager starts Harry Amass instead of playing Noussair Mazraoui out of position, and drops Bruno Fernandes for Lacey just to jolt the team.

Instead, he kept hiding behind the experienced players, even set them up to fail, and when nothing worked, threw the youngsters on in a last roll of the dice out of pure desperation.

The board made it difficult for him to succeed after that transfer window, but he’s doing a fine job of making a difficult job impossible with these decisions.

#14. Three key details Man United Women have got right that Michael Carrick's side haven't this season - opinion

Manchester United men and women’s teams entered the summer with very different attitudes.

The men’s team was bullish after an incredible five-month spell which saw them climb up to third in the table under Michael Carrick and qualify for the Champions League.

Ineos were also promising a huge midfield rebuild, with perhaps other positions even being filled as the club went all in for the return of Champions League football to Old Trafford.

On the other hand, the women’s team had ended a promising season poorly, as they failed to make the Champions League and won just one of their final 10 matches. It was a run that would ultimately cost Marc Skinner his job in August.

In addition, there were significant doubts over recruitment, as United lost significant experience in Millie Turner and Lisa Naalsund, and star striker Melvine Malard to Chelsea.

As things currently stand, though, the two sides of the same club seem on very different paths.

The men’s side have been horrendous this season, winning just two matches out of 11. They have only defeated Ipswich Town and Sabah, keeping just one clean sheet in this time.

They are currently 13th in the league, 20th in the Champions League and already knocked out of the League Cup. In contrast, after a poor opening two league matches, the women drew 1-1 with Arsenal and are now on a six-game winning streak under new head coach, Eva Olid.

Whilst it is still very early in the season and much can change for both teams’ fortunes, here are three things the women’s side of the team have done better than the men’s.

1) Recruit a manager with a clear philosophy Michael Carrick did a great job as an interim; that is undebatable.

Nonetheless, the signs of weakness were already present last season. The Red Devils often benefitted from red cards, individual brilliance, and opposition wastefulness in their fine ending to last season.

What’s more, the only real tactical acumen introduced was hitting teams on the break and having a better team spirit after it was crushed under Ruben Amorim.

This year, though, teams have gotten wise to United and they have been unable to implement any clear tactical approach. Michael Carrick also seems, at best, unwilling and, at worst, incapable of expressing what he wants his team to actually do on the pitch.

Compare that to Eva Olid, who, in her third language, has even been able to tell the media exactly how she wants to play and what she specifically expects from her players. In spite of a tough pre-season and opening couple of matches, United Women already look a much better team on the ball and seem so much more clinical in front of goal, scoring at will in the Players’ Cup, with a 5-3 victory over Liverpool last night being a prime example.

That is not to say Olid is going to be a success, but at least fans know what she is trying to put into action and there is logic to her player choice and decision-making. This cannot be said for Michael Carrick’s United.

Squad photos = dropping soon 👀📸 https://t.co/Owt5LIjXUl

— Manchester United Women (@ManUtdWomen) October 5, 2026

United sells itself as a club that believes in youth, but this is becoming harder and harder to defend for the men’s team at least.

After scoring a brilliant goal in September against Brighton, Shea Lacey has been isolated on the bench. There has been a defensive crisis where Matthijs de Ligt had to start away to Como in the Champions League last night despite not playing for over 11 months.

Dan Armer could have been given the opportunity, or Harry Amass, instead of playing Diogo Dalot and Noussair Mazraoui out of position. Before his illness, Ayden Heaven could also question his lack of involvement under Michael Carrick.

This is not to say younger players would have changed the result, but at least they would have been given the chance to grasp their opportunity the way Kobbie Mainoo did in 2023. On the other end of the spectrum, Olid has had outstanding success using younger players.

Mared Griffiths has scored six goals in three cup matches, including a hat-trick against Sheffield United and a brace last night versus Liverpool. The likes of Layla Drury, Scarlett Hill, Jess Anderson and Lucy Newell have all been given opportunities and are showing they have what it takes to contribute at the club.

Six academy players finished the match against Durham at the start of October and Olid spoke just yesterday on how well the young players have come in and improved the talent level of the squad.

3) Togetherness Last season, there seemed to be a real change in United’s dressing room atmosphere with victories and improved performances. Players seemed to be pulling in the same direction and finally United had gotten the balance right.

This fantasy has been torn to shreds this season, with numerous stories of players being unhappy with the return of Marcus Rashford and Lisandro Martinez and Bruno Fernandes in successive weeks savaging aspects of the club and dressing room in the glare of the media. If this is compared to the women’s side, there always seems to be such a positive vibe around the dressing room.

This would be easy if things were always going swimmingly, but they haven’t been for the women. They finished last season poorly, lost major faces in the summer and had problems with manager Marc Skinner.

Nonetheless, a common feature of new signings is that they also seem to compliment the banter and common feeling in the dressing room towards a united purpose. What’s more, United have recruited numerous Swedish players in recent years, such as Julia Zigiotti, Fridolina Rolfo, Hanna Lundkvist, Ellen Wangerheim and Monica Jusu Bah, to add to Anna Sandberg.

This is not to say this is always the right philosophy, but they are clearly trying to build camaraderie in the dressing room and this never really seems to be a priority on the men’s side of things.

Arriving in high spirits 🥰 https://t.co/pXvpz1C81u

— Manchester United Women (@ManUtdWomen) October 20, 2026

Obviously, fans are supporters of both sides; we want the best for both. There is no point scoring and neither team is where they need to be. There is also the possibility of things changing very quickly either way for both teams. Nonetheless, after reflecting on both teams for the opening part of the season, there are clearly some aspects the men could take from the success of the women’s team.

#15. 'Having fun': Dimitar Berbatov delivers honest reaction after 4-1 Man Utd defeat at Como

Manchester United favourite Dimitar Berbatov has provided an honest assessment following the Red Devils’ 4-1 Champions League defeat to Como on the road.

The Red Devils went into the game on a six-game winless streak, and many expected them to struggle against a Como outfit that have been playing with confidence.

However, the margin of defeat yesterday was quite embarrassing. Como went 4-0 ahead before Joshua Zirkzee scored a consolation goal in second-half stoppage time.

Speaking after the humbling loss, Berbatov said that it was a ‘terrible game’ for United and acknowledged that Como outplayed them in every possible way on the night.

The Bulgarian added that Como were just ‘having fun’ on the pitch and it looked like men against kids.

He told TNT Sports: “It was a terrible, terrible night. A terrible game for United, nothing was happening and Como outplayed them in every way possible. From the goals, from the taking the ball, the possession, the confidence.

“Everything was there for Como. It was like they were playing a second team. Look at the first goal, the confidence on the ball, the time on the ball. Sometimes it’s not about the loss, it’s about how you lose, and today that was the point, how we lost that game of football.

“After a performance like this, probably, like every time, are these the right players to compete on the highest stage? Is that the right manager? Is this the right way? Of course after games like this, people will say we need more players, we need better players, we need this and that.

“What can you say after a performance like this? Today it didn’t look like everyone was giving their best on the pitch. Como looked like they were just having fun. It looked like men against kids.”

United were second-best in almost every department. They conceded in just the second minute to Nico Paz, which pretty much summed up the night. The Argentine stood out with two goals and an assist.

To make things worse, Como manager Cesc Fabregas said in his post-match press conference that he had asked his side to slow down at 4-0, which is undoubtedly embarrassing for a club of United’s stature.

The Red Devils hierarchy have continued to back manager Michael Carrick in recent weeks, but the 45-year-old could be sacked if United don’t return to winning ways against Bournemouth on Sunday.

The Cherries have a tough game against AC Milan in the Europa League tonight. United have an extra day to prepare for the Old Trafford meeting, and anything less than a win could see Carrick dismissed.

Most United fans may feel Carrick should be backed regardless, given the insufficient investment in key positions, but ultimately the results on the pitch will decide what happens to his club future.

#16. Tensions boil over at Manchester United as Michael Carrick caught in fierce row with £62.5m star

The tensions at Manchester United reached boiling point as Michael Carrick and Matheus Cunha had a terse touchline exchange during Wednesday’s defeat by Como.

Carrick is under more pressure than ever at United after the damning 4-1 loss, which leaves his side winless in seven.

The discontent in the squad appears to be growing at pace. Bruno Fernandes became the second player to publicly question the club in an interview following the match at Como as he hit out at Carrick’s tactics, just days after Lisandro Martinez’s outburst in Madrid.

But the animosity between Carrick and his players became increasingly clear mid-match in Italy, as he and Cunha were spotted in the throes of an argument missed by the broadcast cameras.

The Sun’s Samuel Luckhurst wrote the pair “had a relatively robust exchange of views” during a stoppage in play, after which United again lost possession, prompting frustrated ‘cursing’ from Carrick.

Cunha, 27, got the nod on the left flank in Como but was hauled off at half-time having managed one shot and just 27 touches of the ball.

He has been a fixture since Carrick’s appointment, scoring eight goals and assisting a further six in 26 appearances under him.

Amad Diallo came on in Cunha’s place, but struggled to affect a turnaround as United were played off the park by Cesc Fabregas’ Como.

Cunha was not alone in his frustration either, with Bruno Fernandes also among the players left visibly frustrated by the result.

The mood at United appears to be rapidly deteriorating after the dour start to the campaign, with Carrick now the bookies’ favourite to be the first Premier League manager dismissed this season.

Speaking at full-time, midfielder Youri Tielemans could only apologise to those fans who had made the trip to Italy to watch United’s lakeside embarrassment: “They travel all the way, they don’t want to do this, the only thing we can do now is apologise.

#17. United’s December run set with Newcastle, Palace and Arsenal dates

The Premier League has confirmed three December fixture amendments involving Manchester United, completing the Reds’ league schedule for the rest of 2026 with dates, kick-off times and selected UK broadcast details now in place.

The Manchester Evening News reported that the changes affect United’s trips to Newcastle, Crystal Palace and Arsenal. Coventry’s visit to Old Trafford remains at its original Saturday 3pm slot, while United’s festive home game against Nottingham Forest had already been confirmed.

United’s visit to Newcastle remains on Wednesday, December 2, but now starts at 8.15pm and will be shown live on Sky Sports. The match is one of the Premier League’s announced UK broadcast selections.

Coventry City visit Old Trafford on Saturday, December 5, with kick-off at 3pm. That fixture has not been moved and will not be shown live in the UK, according to the Manchester Evening News.

The away match against Crystal Palace is scheduled for Sunday, December 13, at 7pm at Selhurst Park, with TNT Sports carrying the live UK coverage. United then travel to Arsenal on Sunday, December 20; that game at the Emirates Stadium starts at 4.30pm and is selected for Sky Sports.

Manchester United’s 2026-27 Premier League fixture list

The December programme also includes Nottingham Forest’s visit to Old Trafford on Sunday, December 27, with a 4.30pm kick-off. That match was among United’s festive fixtures confirmed before the latest amendments.

The Reds begin 2027 away to Brighton on Saturday, January 2, at 12.30pm. The Premier League’s fixture announcement covers the early-December broadcast selections and changes, alongside its previously released amendments for the period from December 26 to January 7.

Those league dates sit within a demanding run under Michael Carrick. United’s upcoming Premier League schedule under Carrick provides the wider season context, while the confirmed December dates now give supporters the match-by-match outline for the close of the calendar year.

The Premier League says January 2027 broadcast selections are due to be announced separately.

#18. Como demolish dismal Manchester United to pile more pressure on Carrick

In the shadow of the Alps, a boat ride from George Clooney’s villa, Manchester United’s torrid season plunged into chaos and infighting just 11 games in.

Michael Carrick’s status as United manager is now precarious because witnessed here by Lake Como was a humiliation of the three-time European champions by Cesc Fàbregas’s competition debutants.

At the end about 200 of United’s 700 travelling fans remained. Carrick walked over to say sorry, raising his hands three or four times in supplication. But, markedly, he ignored his players, the manager later admitting to “anger” at what was witnessed.

He was then embroiled in a wrangle with his captain after Bruno Fernandes stated “the staff” decided on a tactic not to press. Carrick’s riposte, deep in the stadium at the post-game media conference, was pithy.

“We actually half-pressed man-to-man the second half and then conceded a goal from that,” he said.

And so, after Lisandro Martínez called out Jason Wilcox last week following the 2-0 defeat at Atletico Madrid (for the director of football’s recruitment; the defender later apologised), we now have the spectacle of the manager and his captain at loggerheads.

Whether the 45-year-old will be in place for the visit of Bournemouth on Sunday is the searing question because Carrick was unable to rouse his players when 1-0 down at half‑time after a dire start, and if he has a tactical plan this is a mystery. Carrick brushed away questions about quitting and fearing for his job, but the executive will surely discuss this.

The manner of the defeat will be top of the agenda because by 57 minutes this was over: Como were 3-0 ahead, and Nico Paz had destroyed Carrick’s men with a scintillating display featuring two goals and the assist for Tasos Douvikas’s strike.

When the Italian side attacked and defended it was as a unit drilled supremely by Fàbregas. Carrick, on this and the evidence of only two wins all term, is a coach struggling to match this. Como’s supremacy in the most famous victory of their 119 years came with only 62 total Champions League appearances by their players to United’s 242.

Or, more simply, this was the Italians’ men against United’s boys at a sold-out Stadio Giuseppe Sinigaglia, where the visitors went down to a club whose tagline is “the most beautiful football in the most beautiful location”. For United, though, this was an ugly night in Lombardy.

Disaster struck two minutes in. Carrick’s men snoozed, allowing a Paz curler from the right to beat Senne Lammens. This was a whole 60 seconds longer than Atlético were kept out, as United became the first English team to concede inside two minutes in consecutive major European matches.

The opener came moments after Diogo Dalot spurned a simple opening to dizzy the hosts, so here was United, in microcosm, at each end of the pitch: a basic lack of quality.

Kick off your evenings with the Guardian's take on the world of football

Mason Mount lined up alongside Andrey Santos in a midfield that had Matheus Cunha, Youri Tielemans and Bryan Mbeumo ahead, with Fernandes a false-ish 9. As Como hogged possession throughout, any United foray needed to threaten Jean Butez’s goal: Mbeumo’s corner from the right patently did not when failing to clear a first defender.

After a near 24-hour deluge the rain finally stopped but Como’s blue wave continued relentlessly. United’s press was piecemeal, as Fernandes identified, maybe involving one or two, but never a group act.

At the interval, Carrick had to earn his corn. In missing nine players because of injury, suspension or illness, there was mitigation. Matthijs de Ligt, partnering Harry Maguire in central defence, being thrown straight in for a first appearance in 325 days after a serious back injury illustrated Carrick’s straitened resources.

Carrick’s move was to take off Cunha and Santos for Carlos Baleba and Amad Diallo in a reshuffle that took Tielemans into central midfield, Fernandes to No 10, and Mbeumo leading the line. The net result: Como’s second. Paz enjoyed a universe of time to thread in Douvikas, and he powered past Lammens. This was a mere four minutes into the second half and thoughts turned to the clock ticking on Carrick’s tenure, before Como scored twice more.

First came Paz’s second, the Argentinian skating along the right and beating Lammens for 3-0. Then, on 76 minutes, Como waltzed through for a fourth time. Up rose Jacobo Ramón to head in Kaiki Bruno’s cross from the left and United’s disintegration was complete – Joshua Zirkzee’s stoppage-time goal not even a consolation.

By the end Carrick seemed as broken as his team. Even if Carrick survives until Sunday, who knows how much longer he will remain after Bournemouth’s visit. He was let down in the summer market but at this club the manager usually pays, not the hierarchy.

#19. What Julian Nagelsmann has previously said about man-to-man pressing after Bruno Fernandes complaints

Manchester United lost 4-1 to Como on Wednesday evening in what may well go down as the worst display of the Michael Carrick era.

Simply put, United were all over the place against Como, they were outclassed, out-fought and out-thought all over the pitch.

The inquest is well and truly underway after this game, and it didn’t take long for Bruno Fernandes to make his voice heard on this matter.

Fernandes was critical of Carrick and his staff after the game, pointing out the way the team pressed as a major issue:

“Conceding early makes us go into game with a mood of defeat, it’s difficult to get out of that, trying to be brave, to have the ball and make something happen. If we are not able to do that, it is going to be very difficult to get out of this situation,” Fernandes said.

“It’s a decision from the staff to not press man to man and sometimes leave space behind and try to get the ball, wait for them, but sometimes when you give space to these teams they get confidence.”

Fernandes clearly has an issue with the way United pressed, and interestingly, the Red Devils could now hire a manager who is very keen on precise pressing systems.

Who do you want to see as Manchester United manager?

According to The Telegraph, Julian Nagelsmann is a leading contender for the United job should Michael Carrick be removed from his post.

Interestingly, after Fernandes’ complaints, Nagelsmann is a manager with a clear view on how a team should press.

During his time as Bayern Munich manager he noted that his team needs to press relentlessly if they are to be successful.

“Our counter-pressing is what worries me most. We need to get a better handle on that. The counter-pressing simply has to be relentlessly effective and very powerful,” Nagelsmann said, via FC Bayern Insider.

Meanwhile, Nagelsmann noted in an interview with The Guardian that pressing is key to his team’s structure, claiming that he much prefers to pressure his opponents into mistakes rather than making tackles.

“I am not too keen on tackles. I much prefer us to win the ball by pressuring the opponent into a mistake, that we cut off his angles so that he makes a mistake when he tries to find a team-mate with a difficult pass. And then, when we get the ball, we need to be quick in order to be able to create a chance and then it’s better and the player is fresher when he hasn’t had to make five tackles before starting an attack,” Nagelsmann said.

The cherry on the cake may well be Nagelsmann’s past comments on playing man-to-man and how that works.

The German was very clear that a man-to-man system can’t work unless every player is giving 100% – a sentiment that United fans may find familiar after Michael Carrick’s comments post-Como.

“If you’re playing man-to-man and individual players aren’t giving 100 percent, aren’t pressing, then you’re outnumbered,” Nagelsmann said, via Bavarian Football Works.

Fernandes would get what he wants if United hired Nagelsmann – a manager who puts a huge emphasis on how a team presses and playing man-to-man, but as both Nagelsmann and Carrick say, that will only work if every player gives their all, and, at the moment, it’s hard to argue that every member of the United squad is doing that.

#20. "A team who had won a raffle": Rio Ferdinand launches tirade against former side after Italian nightmare versus Como

Manchester United hit new depths yesterday when they were thrashed by 1907 Como 4-1 in the Champions League.

United were comprehensively beaten by a side who operate on a fraction of their budget.

Once again, the defence surrendered in the opening minutes of the game, just as they had in the previous round against Atletico Madrid.

Thereafter, Como controlled the match and bulldozed United until added time, when Joshua Zirkzee added a late consolation.

Naturally, pundits were left gobsmacked by United’s lack of fight and control in the match, including Dimitar Berbatov, who claimed it was like kids versus men.

Former Manchester United defender and 2008 Champions League winner Rio Ferdinand took to X to voice his displeasure at what he had just witnessed.

Ferdinand wrote, “Cesc Fabregas’ Como have made us look like a team who had won a RAFFLE to be in the Champions league. At times it looked as though Como had 12 players on the pitch.”

His attack on his former side continued when he exclaimed, “we were TOOTHLESS in attack. NO running power in the team. An INABILITY to press Como. NO patterns in possession.”

The former defender did credit the opposition and particularly Cesc Fabregas’ ability to construct and instruct a team to play.

Nonetheless, he claimed there was no excuse for United and reflected, “we were poor on the night and look a team on zero confidence right now – so much to do to turn this around….and I haven’t even got onto the injuries! The difference in budgets certainly wasn’t reflected in the difference between the two teams on the pitch!”

He finally complimented the outstanding Nicolas Paz, who scored two goals and created another for a rampant Como side.

United will need to pick themselves up and respond on Sunday afternoon against AFC Bournemouth.

Cesc Fabregas Como have made us look like a team who had won a RAFFLE to be in the Champions league. At times it looked as though Como had 12 players on the pitch. We were TOOTHLESS in attack NO running power in the team An INABILITY to press Como NO patterns in possession

A lot of credit has to go to Cesc and the way he has built this team & then you see the way he has got his ideas into this team. Everyone knows their jobs and are dialled in in and out of possession. They play to teammates under pressure – a great example of trust in one another. But that is no excuse for us, we were poor on the night and look a team on zero confidence right now – so much to do to turn this around….and I haven’t even got onto the injuries! 🤬 The difference in budgets certainly wasn’t reflected in the difference between the two teams on the pitch!

— Rio Ferdinand (@rioferdy5) October 21, 2026

#22. Jamie Carragher tears into ‘disgraceful’ Bruno Fernandes over Man United captain’s Como outburst

The Portuguese star was critical of how Michael Carrick set up Man United in their 4-1 defeat to the Italians in the Champions League, with more pressure on the manager after a torrid start to the season

Cesc Fabregas’ side swept past Carrick’s United with Fernandes visibly upset on the pitch before an outburst at his side’s inability to press.

“It's a decision from the staff to not press man to man and sometimes leave space behind and try to get the ball, wait for them,” Fernandes said. “But sometimes when you give space to these teams they get confidence.”

#23. United weigh January move for Roma’s Wesley Franca as rivals circle

Manchester United are considering a January move for 23-year-old Roma full-back Wesley Franca, with the Brazilian reportedly valued at around £50 million and Arsenal and Newcastle United also interested.

The Peoples Person says the latest development comes from Football Insider, which reports that United are monitoring Wesley ahead of the winter window. It is reported interest, not a confirmed bid, while a separate report from Swedish outlet Fotbolltransfers puts Roma’s asking price at around €60 million.

Wesley has featured among United’s reported defensive targets as the club considers its options across the back line.

United’s defence is under scrutiny after the side managed one win in its first seven Premier League matches and sat 13th when The Peoples Person published its report. The outlet says the club could seek cover at both full-back positions, with Luke Shaw’s place on the left and Diogo Dalot’s displays on the right part of the wider picture.

Wesley joined Roma from Flamengo in the summer of 2025 and has become a regular for the Serie A side. Naturally right-footed, he can play on either flank, a versatility The Peoples Person identifies as central to United’s interest. He has recorded one assist in eight appearances across competitions this season, starting seven.

🔴 Follow Stretty News on Google as a preferred source and be first with every United update.

The Peoples Person cites Transfermarkt for the figures. Wesley has played 432 minutes in Serie A and 180 in the Champions League, where he has also received one yellow card.

Arsenal and Newcastle are also pursuing the Brazilian, according to the Football Insider report relayed by The Peoples Person; that account says both clubs are prepared to make a move. Fotbolltransfers separately names United, Arsenal and Newcastle among the Premier League sides interested.

A reported £50 million valuation could make a January deal difficult for United, particularly with Roma holding a regular starter midway through the season. Wesley’s ability to cover both full-back roles gives the link a clear fit with the positions United are reportedly prioritising.

United’s search for full-back reinforcements is part of a broader set of reported options, with Alejandro Balde also linked with the club. For Wesley, the next step would be whether United turn their reported interest into an approach as the January window nears.

#24. Carrick insists Manchester United board back him after Ten Hag points to ‘toxic culture’

Manager acknowledges he needs to start winning games

Ten Hag accuses Cristiano Ronaldo of letting club down

Michael Carrick believes Manchester United’s board continue to back him, although the manager admits he must start winning matches to keep his job. Carrick also denied there is a toxic culture at the club, after Erik ten Hag’s claim there had been when he was manager.

Carrick’s side travel to Como on Wednesday for their third Champions League game, aiming for a third victory of the season in all competitions. Sunday’s 1-1 draw at Leeds left United 13th in the Premier League, having won only once.

Their only other success was last month’s 4-0 home win against Sabah, of Azerbaijan, in the Champions League. Carrick was asked on Tuesday if he feels backed by the executive.

“Yeah – why wouldn’t I?” said the 45-year-old. “I’m in this position, I have a really good relationship with everybody at the club. We’ve got to win games of football and that’s what we’re striving to do.”

Despite the poor results Carrick has not sought reassurance from the hierarchy. “Certainly not. I don’t need any reassurance,” he said. “It’s pretty clear. I know the role, my responsibilities. I know we’ve got to win ­football games for everything to flow and ­everything to fall into place.”

Ten Hag was Carrick’s predecessor from April 2022 to October 2024. During his tenure the Dutchman claimed that the executive did not support him regarding some disciplinary ­matters. In early 2024 Jim ­Ratcliffe, who had recently become the largest ­single minority stakeholder, assumed control of the football department.

“They are continuing the toxic culture, they fuel it, they give power to the players,” Ten Hag told the Athletic, referring to his time in charge.

Ten Hag claimed that Cristiano Ronaldo “let the team down” ­when the Dutchman was managing United. The ­forward’s second spell at the club featured him walking out twice.

“He let the team down and he let me down, because we planned with him,” said Ten Hag. He also questioned ­Ronaldo’s commitment, saying that after United were beaten 4-0 at Brentford in the second game of his tenure, the ­Portuguese refused to complete a run in training.

“I confronted him with this,” Ten Hag said. “Many times I say: ‘Ronnie, everyone wants to work for you to bring you in the right position, but if you can’t bring yourself in the box, you can’t score.”

Carrick, who became interim manager in January and was appointed permanently in May, was asked if he has encountered toxicity at United. “No, I haven’t. The players, the staff, they’ve given us everything that they could give,” he said. “I couldn’t be happier with the togetherness.”

Ten Hag led United to the FA Cup in 2024, but claimed the owners then failed to meet promises made during talks that led to him agreeing a contract extension.

He said: “They promised me ­loyalty, they promised me backing, they promised communication, that we get aligned. We spoke about the issues. But the promises, they didn’t keep. “I said: ‘No selling Scott ­McTominay.’ And in the end they sold him. I got, of course, very embarrassed and disappointed.”

Carrick was asked if Ten Hag’s comments about the leadership raised any red flags. In answering he mentioned Jason Wilcox, the director of football, and the chief executive, Omar Berrada.

“Not for me, that’s Erik’s opinion, it’s totally wrong for me to comment or get involved, it’s nothing to do with me at that stage. We’re pretty stable. There’s certain conversations we have that we’re really clear with, with Jason with Omar, the owners, and to the players. It’s really important we’re all pulling in the same direction.”

Carrick will be without nine players for Como: Leny Yoro is ­suspended and Ayden Heaven is ill, while Luke Shaw, Lisandro Martínez, Kobbie Mainoo, Marcus Rashford, Benjamin Sesko, Patrick Dorgu and Manuel Ugarte are injured.

The absence of five frontline defenders (Yoro, Shaw, Heaven, Martínez and Dorgu) will force Carrick to field an unfamiliar backline that may include Matthijs de Ligt, who has not played since last November due to a back problem. An appearance for the 19-year-old left-back, Harry Amass, is also being considered.

There is a severe weather warning for lightning and rain in Como on Wednesday that may affect the 5.45pm kick-off.

#25. Four Man Utd players who should 'escape criticism' from 4-1 Champions League loss to Como, including Amad – opinion

Amad Diallo stood out alongside three other first-team Manchester United players during the shocking 4-1 loss to Como in the Champions League on Wednesday evening.

Michael Carrick watched on from the dugout at the Giuseppe Sinigaglia Stadium as Serie A side Como humiliated Manchester United in a thumping victory in Italy.

The 45-year-old United manager suffered another setback in his Old Trafford reign and Michael Carrick is walking a tightrope over his role at the Premier League club.

Following United’s embarrassing defeat to Como, United In Focus has now looked at the Red Devils players who put it ‘all on the line’ in the Champions League clash.

READ MORE: Man Utd fans have lot to say about Amad after humiliating defeat to Como as one point is perfectly clear

Who do you want to see as Manchester United manager?

Amad Diallo climbed off the bench at half-time and replaced Matheus Cunha after the Red Devils trailed Como 1-0 in the match. The 24-year-old Ivorian winger could not be faulted for his effort in the second half, as Amad’s desire to fight for United was on full display.

Amad’s performance was mainly in vain due to the scoreline on Wednesday evening, but he was able to pick up an assist to prove that he deserves to hold his head up high. The Ivory Coast international was quite easily one of the standout players from a dizzying result.

READ MORE: Man Utd fans react to Bruno Fernandes’ performance vs Como and all conjure Michael Carrick theory

Speaking of Amad, Joshua Zirkzee was the man to ensure that the Ivorian attacker secured an assist. Joshua Zirkzee produced a superb finish deep in injury time and VAR did not chalk off his effort at the Giuseppe Sinigaglia Stadium.

While it was not a mind-blowing performance for Zirkzee, the 25-year-old Dutch forward already had expectations at an all-time low after he replaced Bruno Fernandes in the second half. The fact that Zirkzee scored and had more of a direct impact than United captain Bruno Fernandes did spoke volumes.

READ MORE: Steve Bruce claims ex-Man Utd figure who was ‘slaughtered’ already proved what Michael Carrick would ‘face’

United fans have been screaming for Harry Amass to have more opportunities this season and the 19-year-old replaced Matthijs de Ligt in the second half. However, Harry Amass was essentially thrown to the lions after the Red Devils left-back came on while United trailed Como 4-0.

It was embarrassing for Michael Carrick not to put more faith in Amass to start the match and his cameo felt like the Red Devils boss was out of ideas. Either way, Amass does not deserve any kind of major criticism after he was tossed right into the deep end of the clash.

READ MORE: Why Jose Mourinho’s ex-Man Utd star does not want extra Premier League medal if Man City are stripped

Just like Harry Amass, Shea Lacey shared the same energy in the second half and perfectly captured the ethos of the United youth academy. Shea Lacey came off the bench and replaced Bryan Mbeumo in the dying stages of the match and the 19-year-old Liverpool-born winger did what needed to be done.

Lacey benefited from the United first-team minutes under the circumstances and showed a willingness to put it all on the line before the full-time whistle. Kudos to Lacey for stepping up and trying to deliver where others had let United fans down.
