#26. German manager "sounded out" to replace Carrick, United board member "really appreciates" Premier League alternative - Fabrizio Romano, Talksport

Antonio Conte, Julian Nagelsmann and Eddie Howe are expected to feature prominently in consideration over the Manchester United job if the club dismiss Michael Carrick, whilst a fourth name is “appreciated” by a United board member.

Carrick initially returned as head coach in January, following Ruben Amorim’s departure, and earned a contract until 2028 after guiding United to third place with 11 victories from 16 matches.

The situation deteriorated sharply this season. Sunday’s draw at Leeds left United with seven points from seven Premier League fixtures and only one league win. It was followed by last night’s thumping 4-1 Champions League defeat at the hands of Serie A side Como.

Speaking on his YouTube channel after the defeat, Fabrizio Romano said the situation at United has become untenable.

“So this is something not acceptable at all from Manchester United… [t]o lose 4-1 against Como for sure is absolutely humiliating,” he said.

“The real change between this game and the other games, it’s also in the players…[it] was Bruno Fernandes to go and speak and saying something about the staff. Is the staff deciding? Then he gave his own opinion, they don’t want to go man-to-man. Then Carrick replying and saying, we went man-to-man in the second half and that’s how we conceded.

“That interview was strong because we are talking about Bruno Fernandes. This is not a normal player… this is the player of Manchester United, the face of Manchester United.”

“For the very first time we have players at Manchester United not so happy with the current situation. And that’s something that we can consider as a big change compared to 1, 2, 3, 4, 5 weeks ago.”

The transfer guru then went on to discuss the likely candidates to replace Michael Carrick if he is sacked.

First, he ruled out Argentina coach Lionel Scaloni.

“There have been several clubs over the last one or two years asking about Scaloni availability, and the answer was always no, Argentina, no, only Argentina, no, I want to stay with Argentina. And today Lionel Scaloni has decided to put pen to paper and to sign and stay as Argentina coach until the World Cup 2030.”

“Antonio Conte would be ready this year to take a job mid-season, and that’s a big difference to what he always did, almost always in his career, apart from the Tottenham chance,” Romano said.

Yesterday, Talksport claimed that former Germany head coach Julian Nagelsmann has been “sounded out” for the United job, and Romano also name dropped the German.

“Nagelsmann is a coach that INEOS already considered in the past, but Nagelsmann was under contract, and so the decision of Nagelsmann was to continue with the German national team,” the expert explained. “Now he’s free, and so that’s a different situation.”

Romano then put forward two Premier League manager names.

“Hürzeler is doing fantastic at Brighton. Manchester United have someone on their board who really appreciates Fabian Hürzler, but that’s it at the moment. He’s completely focused on Brighton.

“And then, obviously Eddie Howe, free after leaving Newcastle, English coach, knows the league so well. Completely different character compared to Antonio Conte and all the others. So for sure more calm, more relaxed, already used to work in complicated situations.”

If last night’s resounding victory was a job interview for Como manager Cesc Fabregas, he passed it with flying colours. However, Romano does not think he would consider the move.

“When they were considering coaches [in the summer], Cesc Fabregas was really appreciated by Man United, but Fabregas was in any case not going to become the new United coach for one reason. Fabregas is completely involved in the Como project … so Fabregas was never gonna leave in summer 2026. He wanted to lead Como into Champions League football.”

#27. “You are not”: Michael Carrick loses patience after Bruno Fernandes takes aim at Man United coaching staff

Manchester United manager Michael Carrick has given a strong reaction to the comments made by Bruno Fernandes after the humiliation suffered against Como.

The Red Devils were thrashed 4-1 by the Serie A side, but it was the manner of their collapse that made the result particularly alarming.

Fernandes blamed the tactics deployed by the coaching staff after the game. He revealed that the players had been instructed not to press man-to-man and were sometimes asked to leave space in behind.

Carrick was asked about those comments in the post-match press conference and he made little attempt to hide his frustration.

The 45-year-old said: “We actually half pressed man-to-man in the second half and conceded from that.

“If you don’t run, don’t run forward and you don’t run back you are not going to win.”

These are very damning statements from the United head coach.

Carrick’s blunt assessment marks a striking departure from the diplomatic tone he has typically adopted in front of the media.

The United manager, who has generally remained composed under pressure, appeared visibly affected by the scale of the humiliation in Italy.

Fernandes’ comments could be interpreted as a sign that the captain has concerns about whether the current coaching methods are helping the team perform at their best.

United struggled to establish any meaningful spell of possession against Como, with their build-up play repeatedly breaking down under pressure.

They failed to produce a convincing attacking move until their consolation goal in injury time. Como were already in party mode and barely seemed concerned by the late strike.

Fernandes has played under several managers at Old Trafford but he has never pointed the finger at anyone.

His relationship with Carrick has generally appeared positive, dating back to the period when the former midfielder served on the coaching staff under Ole Gunnar Solskjaer.

It remains to be seen whether Fernandes will face any consequences for his comments or be expected to offer a public apology, as Lisandro Martinez reportedly did following his own post-match controversy last week.

#28. Manchester United injury update: Marcus Rashford, Lisandro Martinez, Kobbie Mainoo latest news and return dates

It is going from bad to worse for Manchester United as defeat to Como in the Champions League has increased the pressure on Michael Carrick.

Carrick’s side were well beaten by Como on Wednesday night, a result that means Man United have now gone seven games without a win across all competitions and some supporters are calling for a change of head coach.

United travelled to Italy on the back of a 1-1 draw against Leeds, but were without several senior players - including Leny Yoro after he was sent-off in the 2-0 loss to Atletico Madrid on matchday two.

Speculation is rife that Man United have sounded out a replacement for Carrick, though most of the attention will be on recovering some players for the next fixture - against Bournemouth this weekend.

With that in mind, here is the latest Manchester United injury news and return dates…

A few days removed from the post-Atletico Madrid outburst, Martinez played the first half at Elland Road and went in at the break with United 1-0 up thanks to Bryan Mbeumo’s smart finish 10 minutes before half-time.

However, he did not appear for the second half as he was replaced by a returning Noussair Mazraoui before joining the rest of the Man United substitutes on the bench.

Carrick has since provided an update, confirming Martinez picked up a knock.

“He felt his thigh a little bit,” the Man United boss told reporters.

Carrick said on Tuesday that Martinez’s injury was “not that bad” which suggests he could return against Bournemouth at the weekend.

Potential return date: Sunday October 25 vs Bournemouth (H)

Another notable absentee from the Man United travelling squad for the Como game was Marcus Rashford as he is struggling with a hip issue.

“Marcus is still feeling a hip issue that we're trying to overcome,” Carrick revealed in the pre-match press conference.

The fact Rashford remained an unused substitute at Elland Road last weekend, particularly with Man Utd chasing a winner, suggests the issue is far more serious.

Man Utd 'sound out' Michael Carrick replacement after sack demand

Carrick fires back at Fernandes after Man Utd tactics criticism

Carrick issues Man Utd verdict after sack calls in Como defeat

Art After Dark is back with an exciting calendar of cultural events

Kobbie Mainoo has now missed Man United’s last three games after playing 90 minutes in the 1-1 draw with Tottenham immediately following the international break.

The nature of the midfielder has not yet been disclosed and the updates had been few and far between since he missed training before the Atletico Madrid match.

Though, speaking before the loss to Como, Carrick revealed Mainoo would be out for another “10 days or so”, meaning he is in a race against the clock to be fit for the Chelsea game at the end of the month

Potential return date: Saturday October 31 vs Chelsea (A)

Luke Shaw’s absence has caused a reshuffle at left-back as Ayden Heaven, Diogo Dalot and Mazraoui have all filled in there recently.

While not a great deal is known about Shaw’s knock, he is expected to be sidelined longer than Mainoo.

Heaven went from a starter in Madrid to not even making the matchday squad at all against Leeds or Como.

It has been confirmed that the reason for his absence is down to illness, so it is just a case of seeing how quickly he can make a full recovery as he should not be sidelined for too long.

Potential return date: Sunday October 25 vs Bournemouth (H)

Patrick Dorgu started for Denmark against Wales in the Nations League last month, but was hauled off with 30 minutes on the clock after sustaining a hamstring injury.

With the Dane capable of operating at left-back - having done so against Sabah and Manchester City earlier in the season - his absence comes at the worst possible time.

The worry is that Dorgu is facing a prolonged spell on the sidelines.

Speaking ahead of the 1-1 draw with Tottenham, Carrick said: “It looks like he'll be out for a number of weeks.”

Benjamin Sesko has suffered a fresh flare-up of the shin injury which saw him miss the last few games of his debut season at United.

He had seemed to be back at full fitness after featuring against Hull, Ipswich, Everton, Sabah, Manchester City and Brighton, but was left out of the squad entirely for the trip to Fulham and has missed the last three games since the international break.

Speaking before the Leeds game, Carrick insisted it was too early to set timeframe on a return for the striker.

“Ben’s kind of still undergoing a couple of checks before we update on timelines or anything,” the Man United head coach told reporters.

“We’re just trying to be clear with what that looks like.

“He’s still a little bit further away, but when we know we’ll let you know.”

Manuel Ugarte has not played a single minute for Man United this season after his World Cup earlier in the summer was cut short due suffering a ligament injury while representing Uruguay.

The midfielder was stretchered off during Uruguay’s 1-0 defeat by Spain in group phase and while Ugarte’s recovery period is still to be determined, he is not expected to return until 2027.

That said, the 25-year-old has started on the long path to a full recovery as he has been pictured in the gym under the watchful eye of the Man United’s medical and performance staff, working hard on his rehabilitation at the club’s Carrington training base.

Potential return date: Spring/Summer 2027

#29. Champions League power rankings: PSG remain top – but at what cost? Man United tumble

Please refresh the page or navigate to another page on the site to be automatically logged inPlease refresh your browser to be logged in

Michael Carrick’s side suffered their one of their heaviest-ever European defeats in the 4-1 thrashing at Champions League debutants Como

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

Matchday three of the Champions League brought two battles of the heavyweights, but what did we learn from the early meetings between title contenders?

Paris Saint-Germain delivered a statement of their ambitions of winning a third consecutive European crown by inflicting a first defeat of the season on in-form Barcelona, but could be without Khvicha Kvaratskhelia for the remainder of their campaign after the star winger ruptured his ACL.

Arsenal rescued a 2-2 draw against Bayern Munich in an entertaining meeting of the champions of England and Germany, suggesting both could be bound for the latter stages of the competition once again, while Real Madrid, Liverpool, Aston Villa and Real Betis joined PSG in making it three wins from three.

Things got a whole lot worse for Manchester United, though, as Michael Carrick’s side stumbled to a 4-1 hammering at Champions League debutants Como in one of their heaviest-ever European defeats.

We compiled our 36-team power rankings by combining team’s previous performance in the Champions League, their results in the competition so far, and the likelihood of them going all the way this season.

Agree or disagree with our standings? Let us know and have your say in the comments below.

Still the team to beat in the Champions League, but at what cost? PSG’s win over Barcelona came with the huge blow of losing Khvicha Kvaratskhelia to a ruptured ACL. The Georgian winger was named the Champions League player of the season last campaign as PSG defended their crown but he faces a significant lay-off and may not play again this season, while Ousmane Dembele and captain Marquinhos are likely to miss the next several weeks due to thigh injuries suffered in the same match. Kvaratskhelia scored 10 goals and assisted six in last season’s Champions League and Luis Enrique’s back-to-back champions will not carry the same threat without him. PSG’s summer recruits Ferran Torres, Maghnes Akliouche and Mika Godts are now set to play an even greater role this season following the sale of Bradley Barcola to Liverpool.

Barcelona’s 11-match winning streak to start the season came to a halt at the Parc des Princes and raised the question that could well define their season in the Champions League. We know about their goalscoring – Hansi Flick’s side reached 50 in just 12 games this season – and the quality of their forward line led by Lamine Yamal and Raphinha. But whether the defence holds up come the knockout stages is another question. Conceding four against PSG, despite going into the 80th minute level with the hosts in Paris, suggests there is work to do against the very elite before Barcelona can think about winning a first Champions League crown since 2015.

An entertaining draw at Bayern Munich, who have such a strong home record, gave a good account of whether Mikel Arteta’s side can take the next step against a heavyweight side in the business end of the Champions League. This has been a typically assured start from last season’s runners-up, who are determined to go one better after their heartbreaking defeat on penalties to PSG. Arsenal’s squad depth means the Gunners have a very good chance of finishing one of the top two spots in this season’s group phase but if there’s one area where Arteta’s side are short it’s attack, so Bukayo Saka’s injury is a further worry.

WIthout Michael Olise, Jamal Musiala and Luis Dias from the start against Arsenal, Harry Kane gave a timely reminder of his Ballon d’Or credentials as he produced a sublime all-round attacking display in the 2-2 draw at the Allianz. Certainly, if Kane continues his astonishing rate of goalscoring then Bayern will be there or thereabouts.

No one knows what Manchester City’s season from here is going to look like. Could the Champions League become their top target if they are ruled out of the Premier League title race, for instance? No one knows. Still, there is quality and spirit throughout the team – as shown in a strong start domestically and in the second half of their 4-2 defeat to Paris Saint-Germain.

Performances remain unconvincing but Real Madrid continue to find a way in the Champions League, with a couple of belting goals from Kylian Mbappe and Yann Diomande lighting up the 4-1 win over RB Leipzig. Somehow with maximum points, despite trailing Roma 3-1 at the Stadio Olimpico, and Inter and Leipzig spurning chances to take the lead at the Bernabeu, the 15-time Champions League winners still carry an aura in this competition and have so far offered a bit of a throwback to the days of Carlo Ancelotti.

Still sitting outside the top tier of contenders, you’d have to say it would be a surprise for Liverpool (or any of the teams below the Reds in these rankings) to win the Champions League in their first season under Andoni Iraola. But it has been a fine start in Europe for the Basque, with three consecutive wins to start the Champions League while managing to rotate his squad successfully. Alexis Mac Allister has been the star with key interventions against Atletico and Villarreal, but the likes of Rio Ngumoha, Trey Nyoni and Lewis Koumas have also benefited from Iraola’s rotation. The knockout rounds, where Liverpool have been knocked out by PSG in the last two seasons, already beckons.

Last season’s semi-finalists have the added motivation of hosting this season’s Champions League final at the Metropolitano in Madrid. Following the opening defeat at Anfield, Diego Simeone’s side clicked into form with six consecutive victories in all competitions until they were held to a 1-1 draw at Stuttgart. While it would be another colossal overachievement from Simeone to get Atleti into a third Champions League final of his 16-year reign, his side were impressive in beating Manchester United. You know what you’re going to get from a Simeone side in the Champions League but Alex Baena and Pablo Barrios have also added a touch of class into midfield, too.

Unai Emery’s new-look Aston Villa side are finding their home and may have secured their qualification for the knockout stages already, ahead of a tough fixture list and games against Barcelona and PSG. Bright showings from the refreshed attacking line-up of Nicolas Jackson, Johan Manzambi, Ibrahim Mbaye and Alysson led to convincing Villa Park wins over Fenerbahce and Viking, with the Europa League winners retaining their identity under Emery despite the huge squad turnover in the summer.

After starting with back-to-back defeats, how badly Inter needed their late win over Shakhtar, which came after Alessandro Bastoni’s daft red card. Still top of Serie A, but missing talisman Lautaro Martinez for the next few weeks due to a hamstring injury, Inter’s group-phase fixtures were rated as the easiest of the 36 teams in this season’s competition but the Italian champions have made an unconvincing start of it.

#30. Premier League: 10 talking points from the weekend’s action

Florian Wirtz takes on more responsibility in Liverpool’s attack while Pierre Sage is under pressure at Crystal Palace

Dominic Calvert-Lewin’s physicality overpowering Bryan Mbeumo in Manchester United’s area at a corner grabbed him a fifth goal in 10 outings for Leeds: a pretty respectable ratio. “I just tried to cause a ruckus,” said Leeds’ No 9. “I make it difficult for the defender. I read the flight of the ball and was quickest to react. I feel really comfortable [here]. I feel the support from the club and the fans and it is a great feeling stepping on to the pitch, particularly at Elland Road.” Of the contenders to be understudy to Harry Kane as England’s centre-forward, Calvert-Lewin may be the lead act – if he can stay injury-free, which has been a difficulty throughout his career. Jamie Jackson

Match report: Leeds 1-1 Manchester United

After the defeat to Atlético Madrid last Tuesday, Manchester United’s director of football, Jason Wilcox, could at least sit a bit more comfortably watching Michael Carrick’s side battle to a draw at Elland Road. Lisandro Martínez bemoaned, among other things, the lack of squad depth at United in his post-match comments in Madrid, but one area Wilcox did strengthen over the summer was United’s midfield. Carlos Baleba, a £65m arrival from Brighton, started his first game for the club alongside Andrey Santos, a £50m signing from Chelsea, and the pair held their own against Leeds’ combative midfield three. “They’re both young players adapting to life at this football club and I thought both of them were terrific today,” Carrick said. “That’s a massive positive for us.” As for Martínez, he was taken off at half-time with a tight thigh. Billy Munday

Florian Wirtz created nine chances during Liverpool’s comeback win against Brentford. The German’s effectiveness grew when Brentford were reduced to 10 men but it was still one of Wirtz’s better games in a Liverpool shirt. The playmaker took on responsibility in the second half. Andoni Iraola was happy and perhaps sees a connection growing between Wirtz and Víctor Muñoz. Both of Wirtz’s assists in the league this season have been for Muñoz, a £34.5m summer signing from Osasuna who hugs the touchline and stretches the play with speed and directness. Iraola plays positive, vertical football. Wirtz could be more dangerous if his passes are hitting speedy wingers such as Muñoz and Bradley Barcola. Jacob Steinberg

Ayyoub Bouaddi made his first league start for Manchester City in the win against Ipswich but anyone watching would have thought he was an old hand. The teenager belied his years with a mature performance at the base of midfield, controlling the tempo while keeping things simple in possession and spotting danger when required. The Moroccan cost £85m in the summer from Lille to battle for a place in Enzo Maresca’s revamped side, fighting Enzo Fernández and Elliot Anderson for a spot in the team – and he is looking their equal. “Not bad, yeah?” Maresca joked about Bouaddi’s performance. “I think that the manager is always looking to find minutes and space for good players. Ayyoub is very good. He is very young, coming from France, so we need to help him, not put him under pressure every game. I think he is doing well.” Will Unwin

Match report: Manchester City 3-2 Ipswich

If you were at Craven Cottage on Saturday and didn’t know which three players Álvaro Arbeloa had brought with him from the Real Madrid setup, you would have assumed one of them was Josh King. The 19-year-old has started every league game this season ahead of Emile Smith Rowe in the No 10 position and has already taken on the role of chief creator. King has been in Fulham’s academy since under-nine level and became a regular in the first team under Marco Silva last season. Arbeloa has spoken of wanting to help the youngster raise his level, to help him kick on. “He’s a top talent,” said Arbeloa after King’s all-action performance against Hull that helped Fulham to their first league win of the campaign. “The sky is his limit. He has the talent, the mentality, the character – he has everything to become a top, top player.” BM

David Moyes claimed Chelsea got the rub of the green with the two goals that condemned Everton to a first defeat of the season but there was no question it was deserved on the basis of their respective second-half performances. Everton’s response to Pedro Neto’s opener was nonexistent, with Moyes’s decision to introduce a seemingly unfit Christian Nørgaard instead of Hayden Hackney a contributory factor, but they could feel aggrieved about Morgan Rogers avoiding a second yellow card in the 44th minute for leaping into James Garner. With Harrison Armstrong booked for what the referee, Paul Tierney, deemed a reckless challenge on Josh Acheampong earlier in the game, his inconsistency on the Chelsea forward’s tackle was a key moment. “I would love to speak on the referees but I would probably get fined,” said Garner. “All this camera stuff on their heads, you need to keep out of the game. Just let referees ref it and stop making it about them. That’s all I’ve got to say.” Andy Hunter

Sven Botman was extremely fortunate not to be sent off for a nasty tackle on Nicolas Jackson as Newcastle lost 1-0 at home to Aston Villa, with the excellent Johan Manzambi heading the first-half winner. The rash, high way Botman lunged in on Jackson seemed emblematic of the centre-back’s decline since he suffered a serious knee injury three years ago. The moment has surely arrived for the now under pressure Matthias Jaissle to drop Botman and recall not merely Dan Burn but Fabian Schär, too, in a switch to a more stabilising back three. Newcastle’s failure to keep a clean sheet in their last 13 Premier League games at home – a run stretching back into last season under Eddie Howe – emphasises the need for defensive fortification. Moreover Schär’s ball-playing ability, and particularly his dangerous long diagonal passes, would enhance Newcastle’s pretty dismal threat as an attacking force. Tellingly, Jaissle’s team registered only one shot on target on Saturday, paving the way for Unai Emery’s 100th Premier League win as a manager. Louise Taylor

When Arsenal signed Eberechi Eze, it was hard not to be a little suspicious. Though he started his top-level career relatively late, it is rare a player steps up levels at 27 – all the more so given Mikel Arteta is not exactly in love with mavericks and risk. Ultimately, Eze looks better suited to being the main man in a mid-ranking team rather than a cog in a top one which prioritises shape and system above all else. He was anonymous against Nottingham Forest prior to his removal at half-time. Though Arsenal did not explode thereafter, Noni Madueke did offer greater threat, his speed challenging defenders inside and out before scoring a winning goal that, though fortuitous, also reflected the difference he made. There is surely little chance Eze is picked to start Arsenal’s next game, at Bayern Munich, and he is running out of time to establish himself at the club. Daniel Harris

Match report: Nottingham Forest 0-1 Arsenal

Pierre Sage is treading on very thin ice at Crystal Palace after another disastrous defensive display allowed Brighton to equal their biggest win against their arch rivals. A poor start to the season has been marked by Palace conceding 17 goals in seven league matches – the worst in the division. The chair, Steve Parish, admitted during the international break that the Frenchman’s position remains on “high alert” and, judging by the angry reaction of some Palace fans to the 5-0 thrashing at the Amex Stadium, Sage could be in big trouble. But there must be some sympathy given the failure to adequately replace the influential Maxence Lacroix in central defence after he was sold to Chelsea. That is believed to have led to disagreements with Parish, who has shown a tendency to act quickly if things aren’t going his way. It could be a nerve-racking few days for Sage before Thursday’s trip to face Besiktas in the Europa League. Ed Aarons

Match report: Brighton 5-0 Crystal Palace

Sunderland’s problems are stacking up. Brian Brobbey’s continued absence with an injury sustained while playing for the Netherlands is beyond Régis Le Bris’ control but defeat at Bournemouth was self-inflicted. The swift journey from the Championship to the Europa League has become a stumble. Kevin Danso’s red card was by no means the only error committed during a performance where cross words were exchanged between befuddled defenders. Granit Xhaka found himself incapable of exerting anything like the influence he showed last season. Instead, Tyler Adams and Lewis Cook dominated midfield. Trips to Poznan, Milan and Anderlecht await for Sunderland but Le Bris’ squad is struggling to straddle two fronts. A horrible performance at the Vitality Stadium came despite five purported first-team players being rested against Torreense. Play like they did on Sunday and a struggle in the bottom half surely awaits. Just about everything that went right last season has taken a turn for the worse. John Brewin

#31. "Going to be a disaster": Ashley Young gives his verdict on hypothetical Antonio Conte to Manchester United move

Manchester United’s season plunged to new depths yesterday as they were soundly defeated 4-1 by Como in northern Italy in the Champions League.

Michael Carrick’s side are now seven matches without a win and sit 13th in the Premier League and 20th in the Champions League table as things currently stand.

They have also been eliminated from the League Cup after a 2-3 defeat to Brighton and Hove Albion, having squandered a 2-0 lead.

As a result, Michael Carrick’s position as manager is looking increasingly untenable.

There have been numerous managers linked to the job, with big names such as Antonio Conte, Julian Nagelsmann and Eddie Howe all currently available to take over the club.

Nonetheless, former player Ashley Young has warned the Red Devils against pursuing a move for his former manager at Inter Milan.

Commenting on The Overlap (via The Manchester Evening News), Young explained, “I’ve worked underneath him at Milan. He’s crazy, by the way. Like he is crazy. He’s a bit like Diego Simeone on the sidelines. Like you hear him non-stop.”

Nonetheless, this was not the reason Young believes he would not be a good fit at Old Trafford.

Explaining his methods in more detail, he added, “meetings were long. When I say long, I’m talking, if we lost the game, we could go in and do the meeting after the game and it stops literally everything and you could be there for three, four hours.”

He continued, “I think that’s one of the reasons why he doesn’t last long at places because not just the players, he tries to test mentally, but the board as well and the owners.”

Referring specifically to his former club’s case, he added, “there has been a lot of talk about Man United’s running stats. If they’re not going to run and Antonio Conte goes in there, it’s going to be a disaster.”

United and Carrick will have a chance to respond on Sunday afternoon against AFC Bournemouth at Old Trafford.

#32. Maguire the only senior centre-back as eight miss United training before Como

Marcus Rashford and Lisandro Martínez were among eight players absent from training before United's Champions League trip to Como, with Leny Yoro suspended. Carrick may have to patch up his defence, possibly with academy players, as his winless side face opponents level on three points.

Of United’s senior centre-backs, only Maguire trained at Carrington on Tuesday. Martínez started at Leeds but was taken off at half-time. Leny Yoro is suspended for the trip to Italy.

Rashford sat on the bench at Elland Road on Sunday without getting on the pitch. Alongside him and Martínez, the absentees were Ayden Heaven, Luke Shaw, Kobbie Mainoo, Benjamin Sesko, Patrick Dorgu and Manuel Ugarte.

Several of those absences are not new. Shaw and Mainoo have both missed United’s last two matches. Heaven was ill for the Leeds game. Sesko, Dorgu and Ugarte are all injured.

Yoro’s ban stems from the defeat in Madrid on 13 October. He was sent off in the 74th minute as United lost 2-0 to Atlético. Pablo Barrios scored in the first minute. Ademola Lookman added the second in the 79th.

With Martínez also missing, Carrick may have to field a makeshift back line. Academy defenders Jaydan Kamason and Dan Armer trained with the first team and could be involved. For a side already short of options, that leaves little room for further setbacks in Italy.

The weekend brought no relief. Bryan Mbeumo put United ahead in the 35th minute at Elland Road. Dominic Calvert-Lewin equalised in the 51st. Mbeumo has now scored in two weekends in a row. Even so, three Premier League points slipped away.

Seven points from the first seven Premier League games of the season is United’s return after the trip to Leeds.

Como offer no easy reprieve. The Champions League newcomers beat RB Leipzig 4-1 on Matchday 1. That was the same night United beat Sabah 4-0 at Old Trafford. Both sides now sit on three points in the league phase.

The hosts have their own problems. Como are without Assane Diao because of a thigh injury. Trevoh Chalobah, with a muscle problem, and Moise Kean, with a shin injury, are doubts, according to UEFA’s team news preview.

The European schedule offers no breather later in the phase either. United host RB Leipzig on 8 December 2026 and finish away at Villarreal on 27 January 2027.

Closer to home, the next test comes straight after Como. United return to Old Trafford for a Premier League game against Bournemouth.

#33. Bruno Fernandes drops bombshell after Como humiliation, takes aim at Man United coaching staff

Manchester United captain Bruno Fernandes has fired shots at manager Michael Carrick and his coaching staff after the 4-1 defeat to Como on Wednesday evening.

The Red Devils were torn apart by the Serie A side in the UEFA Champions League encounter at the Stadio Giuseppe Sinigaglia.

Joshua Zirkzee scored a consolation goal in the 91st minute but United had already suffered major embarrassment by then.

Fernandes revealed that the players had been instructed not to press their opponents man-to-man and, at times, to leave space behind as they waited for opportunities to win possession.

He said (via Football on TNT Sports): “It’s a decision from the staff to not press man to man and sometimes leave space behind and try to get the ball, wait for them, but sometimes when you give space to these teams they get confidence.

When asked whether he believed Carrick had got his tactics wrong, Fernandes refused to give a direct verdict but made it clear that a more aggressive approach might have served United better.

He said: “That’s on the manager and staff to see, not me. Sometimes you have to be more brave under pressure, take the risk of pressing high and making them uncomfortable. As you saw with them [Como], the centre-back was coming to mark me in my own half. We needed to do that as a team but we were unable to.

“A team that plays through the middle with small connections, if you give them too much time and space then they can hurt you out wide.”

Carrick made several questionable decisions but none were bigger than starting Fernandes in the false nine role.

The 32-year-old is renowned for his creativity and ability to dictate attacks, but he struggled to influence proceedings while operating in an unfamiliar centre-forward role.

There was no cohesion when United attempted to press Como.

The post-match comments from the club captain now show why the players were not on the same wavelength.

Rather than aggressively closing down their opponents and forcing mistakes, United’s players were instructed to maintain their positions and allow Como time on the ball.

The statistics painted an equally worrying picture, with United completing more than 100 fewer passes than the Italian side while struggling to match the hosts’ intensity.

Lisandro Martinez raised fingers at the technical director after the 2-0 defeat to Atletico Madrid last week. Now Fernandes has publicly criticised the coaching staff.

United find themselves engulfed in yet another crisis, but the growing signs of discontent suggest this could be more serious than previous setbacks.

#34. Julian Nagelsmann: Manchester United 'sound out' Michael Carrick replacement after sack demand

Julian Nagelsmann has, reportedly, been sounded out by Manchester United as a replacement for Michael Carrick.

Man Utd were beaten 4-1 by Como in the Champions League on Wednesday night, a result that stretches their winless run across all competitions to seven games.

Calls for Carrick to be sacked as Man United head coach grew even louder on social media following the result and talkSPORT claim the club now appearing to be preparing for life without him too.

The report states Carrick is now under immense pressure and as speculation surrounding his future intensifies, Nagelsmann has emerged as a candidate to replace him in the dugout at Old Trafford.

Nagelsmann, a former Bayern Munich manager, is currently a free agent after stepping down from his role as Germany boss after a disappointing World Cup campaign in the summer.

It is understood that the 39-year-old has admirers within the Red Devils hierarchy as he was considered before Carrick was appointed on a permanent basis in May.

In addition, talkSPORT claim Brighton boss Fabian Hurzeler is seen as an option by United as he has admirers at the club too, but they are yet to express interest in Antonio Conte who has made it clear he would be open to taking charge.

Carrick, meanwhile, admitted he was “really angry” about Man United’s performance in their defeat by Como.

“I’m bitterly disappointed,” he said. “I’m frustrated, more than frustrated, I’m really angry to be honest.

“You’ve got to move on and think about what’s next. For us to play in that way is not acceptable, it wasn’t a good performance at all.”

#35. Noussair Mazraoui had a nightmare against Como but Carrick has no excuse after betraying Man Utd

There were hardly any “winners” against Como for Man Utd, but as far as losers go, it’s hard to look beyond Noussair Mazraoui.

The World Cup version of Noussair Mazraoui is firmly in the rearview mirror as he continued his torrid form for Man Utd vs Como.

Looking completely off the pace, he was given the runaround by Como, not helped by playing on his wrong foot and behind a rotating carousel of left wingers.

Still, his nightmare is not an excuse for Michael Carrick, who betrayed Man Utd by selecting him in the first place.

So far, Carrick escaped without playing Harry Amass because he had natural options, even though they weren’t in the best form.

Luke Shaw was replaced by Patrick Dorgu, and Amass played a game or two off the bench, but the situation against Como was dire.

Diogo Dalot and Noussair Mazraoui were the only two fit full-backs, and they are both right-backs by nature, so the stage was set for Amass to be challenged.

Do you believe Michael Carrick's United will make the most of the young talent? Who will break through this season?

Instead, Carrick played the veteran duo together, and Amass came on when the game was already lost, with United trailing 4-0.

In one swoop, all of Carrick’s excuses fell apart spectacularly. Amass is not ready? Mazraoui had a stinker, and not for the first time.

Options in senior players? Carrick had none for Como. Not the ideal circumstances to play them in? He sent on Amass when the team was losing 4-0.

Simply put, Carrick is a scared manager who would rather hide behind the experience of Mazraoui in his unnatural position rather than roll the dice on Amass and see if he can sink or swim.

If he sank, then Carrick would be justified in reducing his minutes, as he was already doing. If he swam, then all of a sudden, the team has a genuine option at LB who can change the whole outlook of the market next summer.

We already knew Mazraoui is not good enough as a left-back, and Carrick ensured we learned nothing. His nightmare was expected, and it came at the cost of Carrick betraying the club’s mantra of “Youth. Courage. Success.”

#36. Man United’s latest shambolic performance leaves Michael Carrick on the brink

Please refresh the page or navigate to another page on the site to be automatically logged inPlease refresh your browser to be logged in

Como 4-1 Man Utd: Nico Paz inspired Como to a famous win, but an abject United performance leaves Michael Carrick facing immediate questions over his future

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

The saying goes that a picture is worth a thousand words, and in some ways the photos of Michael Carrick on the pitch in Como may tell you more than a match report ever could.

United have had many a night to forget in recent seasons, but a 4-1 loss to Como in the Champions League goes down as one of the worst in living memory.

In fact the Italian minnows, taking part in Europe’s premier competition for the first time, put United to the sword in a second-half performance that could well cost Carrick his job.

The fact that Cesc Fabregas – a man who has been touted as Carrick’s successor – delivered this chastening defeat is all the more intriguing.

United conceded within 47 seconds in a dispiriting defeat to Atletico Madrid last week and they just about managed better on the shores of Lake Como, although barely...

Man of the match Nico Paz curled in a superb effort within two minutes to put the hosts ahead. In hindsight it set the tone for the evening, though even the most pessimistic United player could not have imagined the embarrassment they were about to endure.

Carrick’s side managed to settle despite conceding so early last week, but they were all over the place during a wet and windy start in Como, with the hosts threatening twice more within the opening 15 minutes before Harry Maguire flashed a header wide.

That half-chance was to be United’s best opening, at least until after the game had completely got away from them. Como continued to have the best chances, with Tasos Douvikas forcing Senne Lammens into a save before blasting over from inside the area a few minutes later.

Shouts of ‘attack, attack, attack’ in the 34th minute summed up the United display, though a flash of the stats board in the 37th minute showed Como had completed 307 passes to United’s 172, with three shots on target to zero and 62 per cent possession. This was more than just an off-day.

Possession may not be nine-tenths of the law in football anymore, but this was still an embarrassing showing from the visiting side. United were outplayed by Como in every department in the first half and they could barely get near them in midfield at times; when they did get the ball, they quickly looked out of ideas in possession too. This was not possession that was deliberately ceded, but rather possession that could not be won in the first place.

Carrick clearly identified something was wrong, though he waited until half-time to make his two changes. It did not have the desired effect.

Carlos Baleba and Amad Diallo were brought on at the break to try and lift United but the visitors instead found themselves two goals behind within five minutes of the second half starting, with the electric Paz slipping a through ball into the path of Douvikas to leave the Greek striker with a simple finish lifted past Lammens.

It was no less than Como deserved but the game still did not feel like it had completely escaped the visitors, even if they had put in an abject performance leading up to it. In fact, United’s first real spell of pressure came around the 55-minute mark.

However, even that dissipated almost as soon as it began. Instead of clawing one back the visitors found themselves three down in the blink of an eye, with Paz nicking the ball off Baleba before driving into the area and steering a finish into the far corner.

The third goal summed up the farcical nature of United’s performance, in what was one of their worst displays on the European stage, but there is always time for more embarrassment with this side. Indeed, it came with just under 15 minutes left, with Como centre-back Jacobo Ramon powering in a header when unmarked in the box to give Como a fourth. Joshua Zirkzee scored a late consolation for United, but even that just felt slightly embarrassing.

The away end emptied, and as the support from the fans disappeared, you began to think that maybe the hierarchy’s support for Carrick may have too.

As Lisandro Martinez noted in explosive fashion last week, Carrick has not had the required level of support from the club’s hierarchy. That was clear for all to see on a United bench that contained seven youth team players, with the likes of Martinez, Luke Shaw and Patrick Dorgu now injured, Leny Yoro suspended and Ayden Heaven out with illness.

Everybody knew Martinez was right when he made the comments, though few would have thought it would been illustrated in such emphatic fashion just over a week later.

However, there comes a point where the board can’t be blamed for all of the failures on the pitch. The fact remains that Carrick produced some of the leading form in the league with a nearly identical set of players last season, and yet this term he has only managed to produce a series of desperately poor performances, with no sign of an upturn in form on the horizon.

It is true that there is little jeopardy for many teams in this new Champions League format, though that does not extend to a United side without a win in seven games. In many ways this was a must-win game for Carrick’s side, especially considering that they’ll have to face Bayern Munich, an in-form Roma side and Sporting away from home among their remaining five league phase games.

And yet, rather than winning and potentially stopping the rot for now, they put in one of the worst United performances in living memory.

One can complain about support in the form of summer signings, but this was a performance that was created on the training field, not in the boardroom, and the man in the dugout will have to answer for that eventually.

#37. Elite-level incompetence is Manchester United’s problem – not Michael Carrick | Barney Ronay

Getting rid of head coach is not going to solve the problems that led to his appointment. Perhaps it’s worth, for once, doing something different

At the final whistle at Elland Road the TV cameras cut to a lingering shot of the Manchester United chief executive, Omar Berrada, looking down pensively from the stands, deciding, it is to be hoped, on a little clemency, a re-sheathing of the managerial shotgun.

Here we have the man who hired the man. The man who fires the man. The man who also hired or rehired the previous two men he had to fire. Berrada was poached from Manchester City on the basis of his ability to grow commercial revenue (yes, OK, stop laughing), and appears, like everyone else with any actual reach around here, to exist in a strangely consequence-free bubble.

How do you solve what is, on revenue versus results, the worst-run club in world football? By changing the name of the latest patsy on the touchline, of course. For now a hard-fought point is perhaps enough to stave off that pre-programmed emergency response.

Stand down the cowboy cops. Call off the blue and white saloons. Although with a run of Bournemouth, Chelsea, Aston Villa and Liverpool next up in the league it will no doubt soon be time to go out there and arrest Lee Harvey Oswald again. What’s that you say? OK, well, dig him up again. Re-arrest him. Whatever it takes. Someone has to stop these lone gunmen, these managerial magic bullet merchants from derailing the Manchester United supremacy.

This was a dual-track football match between Leeds and Manchester United, on the face of it a good, even game between two teams of well-matched players. A 1-1 draw was fair in the end, although Leeds played well and probably should have won. But of course they won’t be the story.

Instead this game had already been billed as that familiar thing, a referendum on the future of Michael Carrick. A stay of execution for a week or two. Or another livestreamed firing squad for the Super Sunday cameras.

There has been a predictable sense of low comedy about Carrick’s own transformation in the United dugout this season. It was always going to be the case that his widely praised qualities in victory, blazer, T-shirt, jawline, inscrutable sense of calm, would become his fatal flaws in defeat.

Where’s the passion, the whirling arms, the range of tailored collars? At Elland Road Carrick simply looked drained in the second half as United were pegged back and overrun at times, the beard a little more ragged, the hair unkempt but still sweeping, the look of a brave, doomed tugboat captain resigned now to the approaching tsunami wave on the horizon.

A point leaves United 13th in the league and still struggling to reverse a dreadful start to the season. But it was a good game for two people. First for Carrick, who remains a million miles away from creating the problems afflicting this club, even if you accept that his presence as head coach is one of them.

Michael Carrick did not hire Michael Carrick. Getting rid of Michael Carrick is, by definition, not going to solve the problems that led to his appointment. But it might just help to prolong them. Whatever his ultimate ceiling, it is surely worth keeping him just to try, for once, to do something different.

This was also a good game for Lisandro Martínez, who went off injured at half-time with United 1-0 up, and who was also vindicated in his allegedly controversial (translation: entirely correct) comments on the club hierarchy in midweek.

United’s energetic first-half performance clarified one half of this. The players are committed in support of the manager, at least to the best of their ability. The second half made the other half of the argument, that this remains a mismatched, lopsided, cut-and-paste squad, always likely to be pulled apart by opponents as urgent as this Leeds team, which fully deserves its current seventh place in the table.

The problems are so obvious it is barely worth repeating them. But here goes. The third-richest club in the world played a second half with three of its back four out of position. Harry Maguire, signed under Ole Gunnar Solskjær, remains the best straight defender. But Maguire is also essentially a man-shaped solid wood armoire in a pair of shorts at this stage in his career, always the slowest person on any pitch, which means you will not be able to cover space, play a high line or counterattack without leaving yourself exposed to the counter-counter.

So good luck, guys. And remember Omar, who gave you this whisky bottle and loaded pistol of a squad, will be watching.

This was made painfully clear after half-time as Martínez went off and Noussair Mazraoui came on. Leeds scored an equaliser that felt structurally inevitable. They found space down their right side repeatedly. At times they seemed to be constantly peppering the seats behind the United goal, like a group of kids hammering a ball against a garage door. By the end Leeds had more possession, more shots on target and just looked a happier, better structured entity than the travelling heritage exhibit at the other end.

Kick off your evenings with the Guardian's take on the world of football

The key point here is to accept that all of this is entirely logical. This is not a mystery, a haunted house, a place of bad voodoo. Why do we talk about Manchester United so much? For obvious reasons. It’s a massive part of the English football landscape. Mainly, though, because this is a story of mind-bending waste, real-time elite-level incompetence played out on a weekly basis.

Yes, you could sack Michael Carrick. It won’t change much. Let’s destroy Eddie Howe for a bit instead. But the available evidence suggests every single person making executive decisions here is outmatched and outmanoeuvred.

There is a reason Manchester United feels like one of those high street legacy chain shops just before it collapses, piles of Quality Street by the door, a rat in the lingerie department, destined to become a pop-up vape shop run by Albanian money launderers. There’s a reason it feels so badly run. This is because: it is.

We know the Glazer ownership has zero interest in minding the details, that this has been a wonderful acquisition whatever the performance on the pitch, an asset supremely sweated. We know also that Sir Jim Ratcliffe is pub-level incoherent on the details he promised to fix. Remember when he was going to sit down and decide what the “style of football” would be? How’s that working out?

Instead Carrick is left shuffling a first XI acquired by endlessly revolving administrations. Here United had four defenders signed under Erik ten Hag, two Ruben Amorim recruits, two from the mist-shrouded age of Solskjær. No players were recruited under Ralf Rangnick, of course. But then Rangnick wanted to sign Erling Haaland, Luis Díaz, and Julian Álvarez, which all sounds far too sensible.

An organisation can be built with many hands. But not when so many of those hands appear to be so far from best in class it’s hard to work out which class they’re supposed to be attending. John Murtough, the former Manchester United football director, is currently director of global development at Atalanta, who have a 24,000-seat stadium and almost zero global presence. Meanwhile Berrada, the axeman, seems entirely invulnerable from the axe himself, despite the fact his job seems to orbit around resolving the consequences of his own mistakes.

United probably will sack their current manager before the year is out. This is the way this machine has to operate, always looking to buy itself, and those in the executive seats, a little more time on the payroll, an entity that has been hollowed out and allowed to rust, run on buzzwords and brown paper and string. Meanwhile, over to you Michael. We’ll see you next week, and maybe even the week after that, for more of exactly the same.

#38. "Always so important": Eva Olid pinpoints fuel behind her free-flowing Man United Women side

Manchester United’s Eva Olid has made giant strides with her side lately.

After a poor start, they have now won six matches in a row.

The latest victory came in a stunning 5-3 win over Liverpool in the Players’ Cup.

Lea Schuller, Mared Griffiths, Monica Jusu Bah and Maya Le Tissier all found the back of the net.

The team now sit top of the league phase after scoring an incredible 20 goals in three matches.

Speaking after the match to the club’s media, the Spanish coach was understandably happy with her side’s attacking intent.

The first point Olid addressed was how fortunate she feels to be able to bring in quality players to rotate in a busy football schedule.

She explained, “it’s a difficult week, Charlton, Liverpool and City [to come] on Sunday. We need to use everyone because we need everyone to win matches. Today, some players showed it’s not 11 players. Other players in the team can win matches too.”

The Catalan praised her side’s running and efforts but admitted it was harder for some than others.

She added, “some players are not used to playing 90 minutes. They just run until the end, no? But I understand they are not used to 90 minutes. They worked so hard [but] in the last minutes you could see that they were tired.”

One feature that has always been present during the Women’s team’s existence is the unwavering support they receive from their fans, both home and away.

Commenting on this, Olid stated, “they’re always so important. They were so supportive at the beginning when things were difficult and, now that we have the momentum, we need them too.”

Captain Maya Le Tissier echoed her thoughts, stating, “I mean, Wednesday night, it’s raining. I think they deserved some goals, so it’s obviously great to score five. They come everywhere with us when we’re at home, away, midweek, rain and sunny, and we’re very grateful. Massive thanks to them for coming out today.”

United will now face the daunting task of taking on WSL champions and fierce rivals Manchester City on Sunday evening.

#39. Manchester United face €90m-€100m Dortmund demand for Nmecha

Manchester United have made contact over Borussia Dortmund midfielder Felix Nmecha as they assess January options, with Dortmund reportedly valuing the Germany international at €90m-€100m.

The Peoples Person reported on Monday, 19 October, that United had approached Nmecha as they consider midfield targets for the winter window. The account is based on a report from TEAMtalk; Africa Soccer and CaughtOffside have also covered the reported contact and Dortmund’s financial terms.

Nmecha, 26, is a central midfielder who came through Manchester City’s academy before establishing himself in Germany with Wolfsburg and Dortmund. United were linked with him over the summer, and the latest report puts that interest back in focus as the club continues to assess its midfield options.

There is no release clause available for a January move. Nmecha extended his Dortmund contract until June 2030, while the reported €80m clause is due to become available only in summer 2027. For the winter window, Dortmund are said to value him higher, at around €90m-€100m.

That makes a mid-season deal a steep proposition for United. The Peoples Person’s report says a move is more likely after the season than in the coming weeks, with Dortmund viewing the midfielder as an important part of head coach Niko Kovač’s plans.

Figures and contract details are reported by The Peoples Person, Africa Soccer and CaughtOffside.

Midfield recruitment has been identified as a United priority

United’s interest also comes amid reported competition from Liverpool, who are monitoring Nmecha as they consider midfield alternatives. Africa Soccer says Liverpool’s pursuit is linked to their interest in Bournemouth’s Alex Scott; CaughtOffside likewise reports that Nmecha is being tracked as an alternative.

United have not been reported to have made a formal bid. The next significant step would be whether either Premier League club tests Dortmund’s valuation as the January window approaches, or whether the reported interest remains an option for a later transfer window.

#41. Man Utd Player Ratings vs Como | Five players earn disastrous 1/10s as Carrick faces sack ultimatum

Manchester United were ruthlessly dismantled by Como, slumping to a humiliating 4-1 defeat at the Stadio Giuseppe Sinigaglia in the UEFA Champions League on Wednesday evening.

United head coach Michael Carrick made four changes to the side that dropped points against Leeds United last weekend, but his reshuffled line-up failed to produce the desired response.

Como made a blistering start as wonderkid Nico Paz curled a stunning effort into the net in just the second minute, leaving United chasing the game almost immediately.

The visitors attempted to fight back but looked completely out of their depth.

Anastasios Douvikas struck shortly after half-time to double Como’s advantage and leave the Red Devils facing an uphill battle to salvage anything from the contest.

Carrick and his men showed no fight or urgency, allowing Como to exploit their defensive frailties and run riot.

The hosts added two more goals to complete a stunning 4-1 victory, adding another memorable chapter to their rise while leaving United with serious questions to answer.

Saw his defenders making a mockery of a crucial away game.

Mazraoui was given the runaround by Paz at left-back before moving to right-sided centre-back following switch to a back three. Made matters worse by allowing the cross for Como’s fourth goal to come in far too easily.

The England international was at fault for the opening goal after failing to close down Paz. Produced a last-ditch tackle to deny Martin Baturina a clear goalscoring opportunity, but failed to track Jacobo Ramon’s run for the fourth goal.

Made his long-awaited return after 11 months out but looked rusty, reacting too slowly to close down the Como striker for the second goal.

Missed a golden opportunity to put United ahead in the second minute, only for Nico Paz to score from a similar angle seconds later. He was woeful defensively and wasteful in attack, delivering a dismal all-round performance.

Was beaten far too easily by Paz and then made a rash challenge in an attempt to recover the situation, earning himself a yellow card in the 25th minute.

Handed a rare start but he neither brought defensive steel nor any attacking intent.

Lack of service and fight around him also brought him down.

Showed disappointing body language despite displaying plenty of fight in his previous two games. His lack of energy was concerning but his half-time substitution was still a major surprise.

Tielemans looked exhausted and tired but Carrick still starts him in every game. The midfielder offered no energy or creativity.

Carrick deployed Fernandes as a striker yet again, and it was hardly surprising to see the Portuguese midfielder struggle to make an impact up front. A terrible performance, although the decision to use him in an unfamiliar role was questionable from the outset.

Committed a costly error to gift Como their third goal, showing unforgivable carelessness in a dangerous area. His casual approach proved disastrous for United.

Missed a crucial chance at 3-0 but found Zirkzee with a pin-point cross into the box.

Scored the consolation goal to potentially earn the starting spot against Bournemouth.

#42. Peter Schmeichel puzzled by Matthijs de Ligt and another disappointing Man Utd star after 4-1 Como loss

Peter Schmeichel turned his attention to Matthijs de Ligt and the returning Manchester United player’s underwhelming teammate after Como inflicted a crushing defeat on the Red Devils in the Champions League.

Matthijs de Ligt played under Michael Carrick for the first time ever after the 27-year-old Netherlands international made his long-awaited comeback from injury.

The Manchester United defender was sidelined under Ruben Amorim during the 2025-26 Premier League season and was out of action due to a back problem.

De Ligt underwent surgery on his back in the summer after he missed the second half of last season and had been working on his rehabilitation in recent months.

READ MORE: Man Utd fans react to Bruno Fernandes’ performance vs Como and all conjure Michael Carrick theory

Who do you want to see as Manchester United manager?

Matthijs de Ligt returned for the Red Devils and had been on the bench for Manchester United’s 2-0 defeat to Atletico Madrid in the Champions League and 1-1 draw against Leeds United in the Premier League. However, United manager Michael Carrick then made the major call to immediately start De Ligt for Wednesday evening’s Champions League clash against Como.

Speaking after Como’s 4-1 win over United at the Giuseppe Sinigaglia Stadium, Peter Schmeichel explained that it immediately showed that De Ligt was lacking first-team minutes after his return from a long-term injury absence.

“Don’t forget how happy I was on this program that we were back in the Champions League,” he said on CBS Sports.

“I couldn’t wait to follow this team. And today we line up with De Ligt, for instance, at centre-half. He’s not played a minute since November last season.

“You know, he’s on the bench against Leeds for the [second] time. I don’t know how much he’s trained with [United] and it shows.”

READ MORE: Man Utd fans have lot to say about Amad after humiliating defeat to Como as one point is perfectly clear

Outside of De Ligt’s return in Man Utd’s encounter with Como, Schmeichel turned to the woeful performance of Noussair Mazraoui in the Champions League. The 62-year-old Man Utd treble winner questioned Carrick’s tactics with Noussair Mazraoui this season and called out the 28-year-old right-back for his key mistake against Como.

“You got Mazraoui there, who played at left-back to begin with. Obviously, something wasn’t working,” Schmeichel added.

“And I don’t know how long it’s going to take and what it’s going to take to correct it, but not good enough.”

Man Utd have two defeats and one win from their Champions League campaign so far under Carrick this season. The Red Devils’ only win came at the start of their Champions League run after United thrashed Sabah 4-0 at Old Trafford in September.

#43. ‘Like a fresh start’: Michael Carrick relieved by Manchester United’s draw at Leeds

Head coach insists ‘we can still have a really good season’

‘We were the dominant side,’ says Leeds manager Farke

After a week in which Michael Carrick had “the headphones on” to quieten the noise, the Manchester United head coach claimed his side’s 1-1 draw at Leeds on Sunday “felt like a fresh start” and insisted that the season can still be a good one.

Carrick’s team have seven points from seven Premier League games and have won only once in the competition, against Ipswich in August. Sunday’s draw followed the previous weekend’s 1-1 draw with Tottenham.

Yet after a week in which United lost 2-0 at Atlético Madrid, after which Lisandro Martínez criticised the recruitment of the director of football, Jason Wilcox, Carrick was relieved at the point secured against Daniel Farke’s team.

“[It’s been] quite quiet inside [the club] – the headphones have been on this week,” Carrick said. “It felt like a bit of a fresh start today – where we’re at and accepting it and doing something about it. That didn’t surprise me today. We’re not getting too carried away because we haven’t won the game, but if you keep doing the right things for the right reasons then you get what you deserve in the end.

“It definitely shows that the boys are desperate to do well for each other as much as anything else and how much they appreciate playing for this club, because there was a real purpose to the group over the last couple of days and they took it on the pitch. There was an edge to the game, an intensity. I spoke to the boys this morning about the history and what it means to the supporters, the club, to us coming over here into the territory.”

Carrick admitted United’s league position is disappointing but he believes the club’s campaign can still be rescued. “If we keep doing the right things, we can still have a really good season. We’re not accepting it as everything’s great by any stretch, so I’m realistic with that. But sometimes you do struggle.

“We’ve got to find a way through it. We have to be stable enough to keep doing the right things and we’re more than capable of winning matches. I understand the situation doesn’t look too good if you look at it from a certain way, but I have no doubts that we can go on a good run. I’m encouraged by a lot of things.”

Bryan Mbeumo’s first-half goal was cancelled out by Dominic Calvert-Lewin’s strike after the break, leaving Farke disappointed not to take all three points.

Kick off your evenings with the Guardian's take on the world of football

“We took it as a compliment that we have done many things right over the last month that we were seen as favourites against Manchester United,” the Leeds manager said. “But we have earned this. We wanted to prove the comments right. We were the dominant side, but we couldn’t capitalise as we lacked quality in and around the box. It is still difficult in this league to win points. You have to be clinical and be very, very effective, but we have to keep going.”

#44. 0 duels won, 50% aerial battles won, 1 clearance: Returning Matthijs de Ligt brutally left hung out to dry by INEOS' failings

Manchester United were ruthlessly taken apart 4-1 by Como in rainy northern Italy on Wednesday evening.

It was a chastening event and demonstrated to all of Europe how far the club has fallen.

United were outclassed all over the pitch and can probably count themselves fortunate to have only lost by three, as Cesc Fabregas masterminded a famous victory for the Serie A club.

The Dutch centre back has not played since last November but had to fill in today due to United’s crippling defensive injuries.

Leny Yoro was suspended, and Lisandro Martinez and Ayden Heaven were both injured for the game.

This meant De Ligt had to start alongside Harry Maguire, and the two never looked comfortable together.

The former Bayern Munich defender was at sixes and sevens for Como’s second goal when he lost his man and Anastasios Douvika raced in to give the home side the lead.

In 79 minutes of action, De Ligt only managed two defensive actions: one clearance and one interception on the night.

He did not block any shots and, incredibly, did not even compete in a ground duel—something very unusual for the usually commanding and duel-loving centre back.

The centre back also only competed in two aerial battles, winning one.

His passing was accurate, completing 92% of his attempts, but they were not dangerous as he did not complete any key passes.

It seems harsh to blame De Ligt for such a poor showing, as he should never have been thrown in at the deep end like he was, especially with such an ill-fitting partner as Maguire.

Nonetheless, it demonstrates the sad state of affairs the club is in that their squad is so weak they must face a Champions League encounter in this scenario.

#45. United’s possession problem highlighted by Maguire after Leeds draw

Manchester United let a first-half lead slip in a 1-1 draw at Leeds on Sunday, 18 October, with Harry Maguire saying the Reds must show greater bravery and composure on the ball to win games.

Bryan Mbeumo put United ahead before Dominic Calvert-Lewin equalised after the break. Maguire’s post-match assessment, reported by The Peoples Person and supported by coverage from The Independent and TNT Sports, focused on United’s reluctance to take possession under pressure rather than simply the point dropped at Elland Road.

His comments came after a match in which the visitors failed to protect their advantage, leaving them with seven points from their first seven Premier League games, according to The Peoples Person.

The Peoples Person criticised Michael Carrick’s in-game decisions, reporting that the head coach took off Carlos Baleba and did not make an attacking substitution despite United needing a win. That is the outlet’s assessment of Carrick’s choices; Maguire’s own comments centred on the team’s response with the ball.

The draw also continued a broader concern around United’s management of matches after taking the lead. Against Leeds, the advantage came through Mbeumo in the first half, but United could not turn it into a victory after Calvert-Lewin’s second-half leveller.

🔴 Follow Stretty News on Google as a preferred source and be first with every United update.

Maguire also pointed to a better work rate than in some recent performances. He said Carrick had worked with the squad on its responsibilities both in and out of possession, and described a plan to match Leeds more aggressively without the ball while looking to exploit space through the middle when United had it.

That distinction matters: the effort and organisation Maguire acknowledged did not prevent Leeds from finding an equaliser, while United’s limited boldness in possession left them unable to make their first-half lead count. The defender’s message was that the team must be prepared to receive the ball under pressure, not retreat into protecting a slender advantage.

United now need to put that call for composure into practice in their next fixture: the primary report says Carrick’s side travel to Como on Wednesday for a European tie.

#46. Michael Carrick issues Manchester United verdict after sack calls in Como defeat

Michael Carrick admitted that he was “really angry” about Manchester United’s performance in their defeat by Como.

The Red Devils were drubbed 4-1 by the Serie A side on Wednesday in a crushing Champions League loss that leaves them 22nd in the league phase table after three games.

It takes United’s run of matches to seven without a victory in all competitions, and fans began to turn on the head coach after the full-time whistle, with calls for Carrick to be sacked on social media.

Facing reporters afterwards, Carrick emphasised that his side’s showing was “unacceptable”.

“I’m bitterly disappointed,” he said. “I’m frustrated, more than frustrated, I’m really angry to be honest.

“You’ve got to move on and think about what’s next. For us to play in that way is not acceptable, it wasn’t a good performance at all.”

The under-fire United head coach remarked that he wanted a repeat performance of what his team produced in the 1-1 draw at Leeds United over the weekend.

However, the levels were nowhere near the standard expected of a side of United’s side, as they were second best to Cesc Fabregas’ men all night.

“That bit of intensity, spirit, aggression and willingess to give everything [was not there],” he continued.

“It was the total opposite of a performance, I have to say, and it was tough to watch.”

United return to Premier League action on Sunday when they host Bournemouth.

They sit 13th in the English top flight, and haven’t won since August, when they beat Ipswich 5-2 at home.

#47. ‘Angry’ Michael Carrick responds to questions on Man Utd future after Como calamity

Please refresh the page or navigate to another page on the site to be automatically logged inPlease refresh your browser to be logged in

Carrick is under huge pressure after Man United were thrashed 4-1 by Como in the Champions League

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

Michael Carrick has insisted that he is not considering his future after Manchester United’s humbling 4-1 defeat to Como left him on the brink.

United were thrashed by Cesc Fabregas’ side in their third Champions League outing of the season, extending the club’s winless run to seven.

Carrick’s position as United manager is now clouded by uncertainty but as the former Red Devils reflected on his side’s performance, he declared that speculation surrounding his job was not what he was focussing on.

“I’ve not given my position any thought,” he told a press conference. “It’s [about] the supporters, I was one of them, and I didn’t like what I saw.”

Speaking to TNT Sports in a separate interview, Carrick echoed the same sentiment and said “it’s not about me” when asked if he still expected backing from the cliub.

Carrick took “100 per cent responsibility” for how the team performed but nevertheless expressed his anger and frustration at the level he witnessed, apologising to the travelling supporters for the latest dismal result.

"I'm really angry but you've got to move on and deal with what's next and see what that looks like,” he said.

"At this moment in time, there's a lot to do before the next game to get the team ready, because that was a difficult night for everybody.

"It's not about playing for me - it's about playing for this football club. It’s the responsibility what comes with it. I’m in the position, I’m responsible for the club and the supporters.

"I’m giving my absolute best all the time. My whole life's wrapped around it [Man Utd]. It might go well, it might not go so well, but I genuinely give everything I possibly can.

"It's not about me and the relationship, it's about what we all give, players, staff, for this football club.”

Carrick also responded to Bruno Fernandes’ criticism of his tactics, specifically their lack of pressing which gave Como too much time and left them somewhat toothless.

"We half pressed man-to-man in the second half and ended up conceding a goal from that,” he said.

"Whatever tactics you play, it you don't run back, you ain't going to win."

Man United’s next outing comes on Sunday as they host Marco Rose’s struggling Bournemouth in the Premier League.

#48. Carrick’s Cunha decision vs Como confirms indefensible agenda that will lead to Man Utd sacking

There are far bigger problems at Man Utd than how Michael Carrick treats Matheus Cunha, but it’s another symptom of a major problem.

The 4-1 loss to Como doesn’t happen due to just a single person, even if the concerned person is the manager, Michael Carrick.

It happens due to rot from top to bottom, where nobody makes good decisions, nobody is held accountable, and there is a lack of strong characters.

One such problem is Carrick’s indefensible agenda against Matheus Cunha, which was visible again in the loss to Como.

When it comes to team selections, Carrick has made some truly head-scratching calls, from not trusting the youngsters to making some players unworthy untouchables.

Curiously enough, Matheus Cunha fits neither of those categories, but look under the surface, and it becomes clear that Carrick doesn’t rate him much.

When he became the interim manager, Cunha was the one sent to the bench as Patrick Dorgu started.

Dorgu’s injury forced Carrick to play Cunha off the left, and the Brazilian made himself undroppable by playing some brilliant football.

Who do you want to see as Manchester United manager?

Cunha was back on the bench when Dorgu returned. When Marcus Rashford failed to earn himself a permanent move, off came Cunha again.

Instead of keeping him at his best position, he was shifted to the centre to make space for Rashford, despite the Brazilian playing much better football.

Rashford was dropped, and Cunha came back on the left, and against Como, he was a half-time change for Amad, despite being one of the better players in the first half, which is not saying much but still true.

Simply put, Cunha is always on the brink with Carrick, as he is looking for a reason not to play him.

Cunha’s personality doesn’t match Carrick’s, but that’s precisely the point. When there’s no fire in the dugout, you need some on the pitch.

Cunha is that fire, and Carrick has seemed intent on dousing it ever since he stepped through the Old Trafford doors at the start of this year.

This weird Cunha agenda is just another in a long line of puzzling decisions that will eventually get him sacked.

#49. "Men against kids": Former Man United striker Dimitar Berbatov savages players after 4-1 humiliation by Como

Manchester United suffered one of their most embarrassing results and performances in recent times on Wednesday evening.

They were taken apart 4-1 by Italian side Como, and the truth is, it could have been more.

The Serie A side were dominant in almost every metric and made a fool of United’s players and coaching staff on a dismal night in the Reds’ European history.

United now find themselves seven matches without a win, with only three points from three matches in the Champions League phase.

Speaking to TNT Sports after the game, former Manchester United striker Dimitar Berbatov was far from impressed by what he saw.

Reflecting on the match, he claimed, “it was a terrible, terrible night. A terrible game for United, nothing was happening, and Como outplayed them in every way possible. From the goals, from taking the ball, the possession, the confidence. Everything was there for Como.”

Building further on his analysis, he explained, “it was like they were playing a second team. Look at the first goal, the confidence on the ball, the time on the ball.”

The Bulgarian exclaimed that the result was one thing, but the performance was something else that needed to be assessed.

He explained, “sometimes it’s not about the loss, it’s about how you lose, and today that was the point, how we lost that game of football. After a performance like this, probably, like every time, [people ask], ‘Are these the right players to compete on the highest stage? Is that the right manager? Is this the right way?'”

Finally, Berbatov gave a damning assessment of the mindset of the current players at the club.

He asserted, “today it didn’t look like everyone was giving their best on the pitch. Como looked like they were just having fun. It looked like men against kids.”

United will have to respond against AFC Bournemouth on Sunday afternoon when they return to Old Trafford.
