#51. Leeds United 1-1 Manchester United: Premier League – as it happened

Leeds’ Dominic Calvert-Lewin cancelled out a Bryan Mbeumo opener to end a turbulent week for the visitors

⚽ The Joy of Six: Leeds v Manchester United matches

That’s all from me. Thanks for reading and for your emails.

A reminder that Nottingham Forest v Arsenal is just about to start. Enjoy! Bye!

Here are the match reports from the two other early kick-offs in the Premier League:

Just 20 minutes to go until our next Premier League kick-off. Daniel Harris has minute-by-minute updates of Nottingham Forest v Arsenal, right here.

“It is time that Manchester United realised that they are going to be a mid-table team in the foreseeable future?”, emails Krishnamoorthy V.

It’s true that Manchester United have had a desperately poor start to the season, while they are also out of the League Cup. But Carrick’s side finished third last season, I’m not sure they will be languishing down in the bottom half for long.

Speaking of the table, here is the latest Premier League standings after those three early kick-offs.

That was a real game from the 1990s. All blood and thunder, maybe a little light on quality, but thoroughly entertaining.

Man of the match? Harry Maguire, real leadership at the back for Manchester United. Lisandro Martínez, replaced at half-time, will have appreciated his teammates’ performance.

90+4 min: Heroic block from Longstaff to deny Cunha with a long-range block. Manchester United are pushing.

90+3 min: Yellow card for Ampadu, who brings down Diallo with late, cynical challenge. No complaints from the Leeds captain.

90+2 min: Leeds continue to pepper Manchester United’s box with crosses, with Lammens coming off his line to punch clear. But it’s a poor punch and the ball falls to Stach, who shoots … the ball goes through Lammens’ legs but Mazraoui is there to hack clear!

90 min: Excellent challenge from Dalot to half a rampaging Calvert-Lewin, with the Leeds striker left writhing on the turf after a textbook sliding tackle.

Four minutes added on here at Elland Road.

88 min: From a Manchester United corner, Leeds clear at the near post and then Fernandes arrows a fierce long-range shot into Cunha’s midriff. Ouch. Manchester United regather the ball and feed Diallo on the right wing. He cuts in and sends a shot to the heavens. Poor.

86 min: Nmecha goes down in the area after an aerial challenge from Maguire. There is a brief check from VAR for a Leeds penalty, but play is waved on. There was nothing in that.

84 min: A positive change from Carrick and Manchester United: Cunha comes on for Tielemans.

82 min: Leeds look the more likely, at present. Nice link up play from Calvert-Lewin and Nmecha but Justin’s cross is poor from the right wing, the autumn sunshine blinding the Leeds wing-back, perhaps.

79 min: Both sides are now playing with the handbrake on, somewhat. Tanaka being replaced by the less enterprising Longstaff is evidence of that. The draw is not a terrible result for either side.

76 min: Excellent work from Dalot, who wins the ball from Nmecha, rides a couple of challenges and releases Diallo down the right wing. Perhaps a little fatigued, the Ivorian cuts back when he might have gone full pelt towards goal, and is eventually crowded out by the retreating Leeds defenders. Had Rashford been on the end of that through ball, he would surely have gone straight for goal.

For Manchester United, Mount replaces Baleba in central midfield. That looks like a straight swap, which is surprising.

73 min: Half-chance for Stach! Okafor gets to the byline down Leeds’ left and stands up an excellent cross towards Calvert-Lewin at the back post. Dalot wins the header but only knocks the ball back to Stach on the edge of Manchester United’s box. The German, normally so technically sound, can’t control the bouncing ball, sending his effort into Row Z.

71 min: Bruno Fernandes tries his luck from range. Straight at Trafford. But these are more encouraging signs for Manchester United, who still have plenty of attacking options off the bench: Mount, Rashford, Cunha, Zirkzee, and Lacey.

69 min: Diallo had to leave the field of play after treatment so Manchester United are temporarily down to 10 men. But the visitors have changed the momentum of this match again and seem comfortable. It’s is anyone game once again.

67 min: Tanaka flies into a tackle with Amad, leaving the Manchester United in a heap on the ground. The Japanese won the ball, and Leeds counter but Okafor is wasteful when trying to find Calvert-Lewin with a through ball.

65 min: Twenty minutes into the second half, Manchester United enjoy the first period of possession in Leeds’ half. Yoro gets forward from right back and finds Fernandes with a neat cut-back. The Portuguese takes a touch inside Leeds’ area, but drags his shot wide of the far post. A half-chance.

63 min: Corner to Leeds and the home side try exactly the same routine, with Ampadu blocking Maguire as Stach swung the ball in. Ampadu and Maguire tangle under the ball, did the Manchester United man pull the Leeds captain to the ground?! Play is waved on, but Maguire took a chance there. Leeds look so dangerous from set pieces.

60 min: Leeds centre-back Muharemovic rakes a sensational diagonal pass out to Bogle on the right wing. The young Slovenian, 6ft4in, is some player.

58 min: It’s chaos out there, the ball pinballing in and around the Manchester United penalty box. Leeds just need a loose ball to fall to the right person again. Michael Carrick and Manchester United will surely make changes soon, or else this game is only going to go one way.

56 min: Leeds smell blood. They have had 69% possession in this second half and are now pushing for a second goal. Manchester United look shellshocked. Tanaka is first to a loose ball and blazes a shot high and wide. But his wayward effort only serves to fire up the home crowd even further.

54 min: It would have been a Herculean effort if Manchester United were to survive another 45 minutes of aerial bombardment. They have no conceded, not helped by this makeshift back four: Yoro, Maguire, Mazraoui, Dalot.

Mazraoui concedes a soft corner and Stach sends another delivery to the near post. Maguire is pinned (held?!) by Ampadu and no other United defender gets near the ball. Calvert-Lewin has the time and space to control a bouncing ball, just seven yards out, and crashes his finish past a despairing Lammens with his second touch. Elland Road erupts!

49 min: Leeds have the ball and are crossing relentlessly into the box. United’s defence are standing firm but the visitors are not offering much going forward so far in this second half.

#52. Confirmed Man United XI vs Como: Carricks makes four changes | De Ligt returns, Lacey & Baleba on bench

Manchester United head coach Michael Carrick has officially unveiled his starting XI for today’s UEFA Champions League clash against Como.

The Red Devils are without a win in their last six games across all competitions.

Carrick has made four changes to his side that played out a 1-1 draw against Leeds United on Sunday.

Lining up for United this evening…? #UCL pic.twitter.com/nyKC4GpWgH

Goalkeeper Senne Lammens is still chasing his first clean sheet of the season despite making a few crucial saves.

The United manager confirmed at his pre-match press conference that nine senior players were unavailable.

Lisandro Martinez sustained a thigh injury against Leeds. Matthijs de Ligt replaces him and will partner Harry Maguire at the heart of defence.

The Dutchman is returning after an 11-month absence following back surgery in May.

Noussair Mazraoui returns to the starting XI and is expected to play at right-back. Diogo Dalot delivered a promising display against Leeds and is set to continue at left-back.

Kobbie Mainoo is still out and may not be available for another week.

Andrey Santos keeps his place in the starting XI for the third consecutive time.

Mason Mount replaces Carlos Baleba in midfield, allowing Youri Tielemans to operate in the number 10 position.

Bruno Fernandes is again deployed on the right flank, while Matheus Cunha replaces Amad Diallo in the starting XI and is expected to operate on the left.

Bryan Mbeumo spearheads the attack again, with Benjamin Sesko sidelined due to a shin injury.

Dan Armer, Shea Lacey, Jaydan Kamason, Harry Amass, Jack Fletcher and Tyler Fletcher are named on the bench.

Confirmed Man United line-up (4-2-3-1): Senne Lammens; Noussair Mazraoui, Harry Maguire, Matthijs de Ligt, Diogo Dalot; Andre Santos, Mason Mount, Youri Tielemans; Bruno Fernandes, Bryan Mbeumo, Matheus Cunha

#53. Mbeumo goal not enough as United’s winless run reaches six

Bryan Mbeumo put Manchester United ahead at Leeds, but a 1-1 draw stretched the Reds’ winless run to six matches and left Michael Carrick describing the result as a possible reset rather than a breakthrough.

Dominic Calvert-Lewin levelled after the break at Elland Road, denying United a first win in the run. BBC Sport’s Simon Stone reported that Mbeumo has now scored four times in seven Premier League appearances, a rare source of attacking output in a difficult start.

Carrick’s side have seven points from seven league games, with their only Premier League victory coming against Ipswich in August. The Leeds result followed a 1-1 draw with Tottenham and a 2-0 defeat by Atlético Madrid in the preceding week, leaving little room to treat one improved display as a change in fortunes.

The Tottenham stalemate had already kept United searching for a response, while the five-match winless run before Leeds set up the pressure surrounding the trip to Elland Road. The point there made it six without a victory across competitions.

According to the Guardian’s match report, Carrick said the performance felt like a fresh start, but stressed that United were not getting carried away because they had not won. He also acknowledged that the league position was disappointing and that the campaign was not yet where the club expected it to be.

The head coach’s message was that United must remain steady through a difficult spell. He believes the squad can win matches and put together a stronger run, while accepting that the current situation looks poor from some angles. That optimism is now being tested against a six-game sequence without a victory.

The week had brought added scrutiny after Martínez criticised director of football Jason Wilcox’s recruitment following the Atlético defeat. Carrick had tried to shut out the noise, the Guardian reported, but the wider context made the Leeds point significant without making it a solution.

🔴 Follow Stretty News on Google as a preferred source and be first with every United update.

Daniel Farke offered a different reading of the contest, saying Leeds had been the dominant team but failed to make their pressure count. He pointed to a lack of quality around the penalty area as the reason his side could not turn the draw into all three points.

For United, the immediate challenge is to convert Carrick’s call for a reset into a win. Their next fixture is a Champions League trip to Como on Wednesday 21 October, before Bournemouth visit Old Trafford in the Premier League.

#54. Fabrizio Romano drops major Carrick update, claims situation has changed behind the scenes after “humiliating” defeat

Manchester United sank to a new low in Italy, suffering an embarrassing 4-1 defeat against Como, who are playing in the Champions League for the first time in their history.

Michael Carrick’s reaction after the full-time whistle underlined his disappointment at his side’s performance as he went to the away supporters and held up his hands in apology, without his players.

With United now winless in seven games across all competitions, Carrick’s tactical ideas are clearly not working, and the gesture in Stadio Giuseppe Sinigaglia suggests the end of another managerial spell is on the horizon.

Fabrizio Romano addressed the situation on his YouTube channel, explaining why the feeling after the defeat against Como is different compared to other defeats under Michael Carrick.

Romano underlined the gravity of the situation after the absolutely “humiliating” loss by saying he is recording the video at 2 am in Italy to reveal the internal tension at Manchester United.

“Tonight I’m recording this video, and this is at 2 am at night here in Italy. But the real difference between this game and the other games, the real change between this game and the other games, is also in the players,” the Italian journalist stated.

“For the very first time we have players at Manchester United not so happy with the current situation. And that’s something that we can consider as a big change compared to one, two, three, four, five weeks ago, when the season had almost started and Man United were already not at the best level possible.”

After the defeat at Atletico Madrid, Lisandro Martinez appeared to back Carrick by criticising the club’s director of football, Jason Wilcox.

However, this time, Romano insisted that “there is a feeling inside that not everyone is so happy with the current situation, and I’m also talking about the coach.”

It is understood many players are not happy with the technical and tactical approach under Carrick at Carrington.

“That’s why I think we have to be very careful with the Manchester United situation, because now we enter into a different territory,” Romano added.

For now, United continue to publicly back Carrick, but as Romano highlighted, they did the same before sacking Erik ten Hag and then Ruben Amorim.

Given the Red Devils head coach is rapidly losing the support of his dressing room, we could hear an official statement from INEOS sooner rather than later.

#55. Michael Carrick sack verdict delivered as Manchester United ‘problem’ revealed

Michael Carrick is not the problem at Manchester United, Joe Cole said after the Red Devils slumped to a seventh successive game without a victory.

The former midfielder took over the managerial reins on a permanent basis after a successful second spell as interim head coach last term.

United sit 22nd in the Champions League table after a 4-1 thrashing by Como on Wednesday, while they lie in 13th in the Premier League after they were held to a 1-1 draw by Leeds over the weekend.

They were outplayed by Cesc Fabregas’ men in Italy, as Nico Paz shone at the Stadio Giuseppe Sinigaglia.

Fans turned on Carrick on social media after the full-time whistle, with the United boss forced to front up the travelling support and apologise himself, holding two arms aloft.

Yet while the majority are calling for his head, Cole, the former Chelsea and England midfielder, believes that the under-fire manager is not at fault for United’s predicament.

“It was humbling for them,” Cole said on TNT Sports.

“The spotlight will fall on Carrick's feet, and he is not the problem, believe me. The club's rot has been there for many many years, and they [the club] need to get out of their own way.

There are a lot of players in that United squad who probably are not good enough to play for United.

“So when does the criticism come to the director of football? I think it's fair that you are trying to get rid of players on long-contracts, but if nobody wants them it's difficult to get rid of them at the right price.”

Fellow TNT Sports pundit Dimitar Berbatov, the former United striker, called the players’ efforts into question.

"What can you say after a performance like this?” the Bulgarian asked rhetorically.

“Today, I don't think everyone was giving their best. It didn't look like this on the pitch. Como were just having fun.”

#56. Manchester United held as Calvert-Lewin’s Leeds leveller keeps pressure on Carrick

What next for Michael Carrick and Manchester United? Game-wise, the answer is the trip to Como on Wednesday yet after failing, again, to double their league win tally to two, you have to wonder the destination for an already flatlining team.

This display was the latest unconvincing outing under the 45-year-old head coach. It featured an odd formation, zero rhythm or control, and the same prevailing sense that the wheels could clatter off anytime.

Bryan Mbeumo’s first-half opener was answered, inevitably, by Dominic Calvert-Lewin after the interval for hosts who were not markedly better but did not need to be, so fragile are Carrick’s cadre. Elland Road was the scene of Ruben Amorim’s last stand, Carrick’s predecessor reacting to the 1-1 draw in January by demanding his colleagues in the recruitment department “do their job”. The result? Being sacked two days later.

For now Carrick should avoid a similar fate, but for how long? United remain stuck on one league victory – at the end of August, against Ipswich – and so Como, in the Champions League, becomes the next must-win fixture.

To hear Carrick hail this result as a “fresh start” hints at desperation and the state his team are in. Even more quaint was another comment. “I understand the situation doesn’t look too good if you look at it from a certain way, but I have no doubts that we can go on a good run,” he said.

Precisely how else United having only seven points from their opening seven games can be viewed seems comical to even consider. As the Leeds manager, Daniel Farke, said: “We should have won the game but we move on. We are slightly disappointed it is just a point.”

Five days after his scathing diatribe aimed at Jason Wilcox – for the director of football’s questionable recruitment – Lisandro Martínez was selected by Carrick, the defender’s apology on Thursday enough to clear the way, though his day ended at the interval because of a tight thigh.

Farke’s team, unchanged for a fifth consecutive game, were roared on by a crowd who delighted in Martínez’s first contribution, a stray pass hoofed out of play.

The visitors lined up in an “Amorim shape” of 3-4-3, a lesser‑seen Carrick configuration. In this, Bruno Fernandes was fielded as the nominal centre-forward, curtailing the captain’s ability to pull strings from midfield, though his was a free role.

The noise from a febrile home support was dialled up when Carlos Baleba sent Anton Stach to the turf and Darren England saw no infringement. After Fernandes ran through and a clumsy touch caused him to scythe down Tarik Muharemovic, England did act – booking Manchester United’s captain when play next paused, to the delight of Leeds fans.

Gabriel Gudmundsson was also shown a yellow card – for clipping Andrey Santos – in a contest that was thunderous but lacked regular goalmouth incident. Instead, the fare was bitty throughout. Gudmundsson, the left wing-back in Farke’s 5-2-2-1, splayed the visitors’ defence with a cross that wrongfooted Harry Maguire but found no teammate, before the right wing‑back Jayden Bogle repeated the act as Leeds exerted momentary control. A Stach free-kick, from the right, had Nico Elvedi flying at the ball to fashion a header that Senne Lammens had to be quick to repel.

Kick off your evenings with the Guardian's take on the world of football

At last, the match had livened up. Carrick’s men carved a clear chance when Youri Tielemans dropped the ball on to Mbeumo’s head at close range but he missed – badly. But next Mbeumo struck via a quick move that sundered Leeds. Along the right Tielemans found Amad Diallo who returned the ball and the Belgian’s cross was finished by the forward.

Martínez’s removalt brought Noussair Mazraoui to the fray as 45 minutes stood between Carrick and a morale-boosting victory. They held firm for five. A Mazraoui header sent Bogle’s cross out for a corner. When this was swung in Mazraoui was bested by Muharemovic whose flick fell to Calvert-Lewin: he outmuscled Mbeumo and crashed home.

With the crowd propelling them forward, Ao Tanaka boomed a volley just over and Farke’s men pinned back Carrick’s side, who lacked ideas: Baleba’s unconvincing long-range effort summing this up. Better was Fernandes’s turn and shot that spun narrowly wide but he followed this with a weak attempt that the Leeds goalkeeper James Trafford held easily.

On 73 minutes Farke took off Noah Okafor for Lukas Nmecha and Carrick removed Baleba for Mason Mount but none of the changes could spark a winner.

Maguire continued Carrick’s attempt at PR. “You can’t question the togetherness, spirit, the fight,” the veteran defender said.

“This is a tough place to come. Leeds have been flying in the league. We can be better. We have to show more quality with the ball. Throughout we felt comfortable in our shape. They didn’t create too many chances. To concede a set piece after half-time is disappointing – we had to dig in and we got a foothold at about 70 minutes and probably looked the more likely to go and win it but a draw is probably fair.”

#57. 'Not what a captain should be' - the debate around Fernandes' outburst

Man Utd humbled by four-goal Como to pile pressure on Carrick

Bruno Fernandes' comments after Manchester United's humbling by Como have got fans and pundits talking - but what now for the captain?

United's 4-1 Champions League defeat in Italy was the result of a wretched performance and Fernandes questioned boss Michael Carrick's tactics.

"It's a decision from the staff to not press man-to-man and sometimes leave space behind and try to get the ball, but sometimes when you give too much the ball to these teams they get confidence," said the Portugal attacking midfielder on TNT Sports.

Asked if Carrick got his tactics wrong, the 32-year-old continued: "That's on the manager and staff to see it, not me."

Fernandes rounds on Carrick after Man Utd humiliation

Man Utd humbling by Como leaves Carrick job in danger

Not according to former Liverpool vice-captain Jamie Carragher, working as a summariser, on CBS Sports.

"He's reacted like that because he's been embarrassed by the manager [who] brought him off," said the ex-England defender.

"And when he talks about pressing, whether it's man-to-man or zonal, for years, Bruno Fernandes has meant Manchester United can't press because he does things for the crowd.

"He runs all over the place like a kid in the schoolyard for the supporters. They all think he's working hard. He's not. He's killing the rest of the team doing it."

He added: "Watching him throw his arms up every week in a game is an absolute disgrace [and] calling the manager out considering how he's performed this season on the pitch.

"[It's an] awful attitude and not what a Manchester United captain should be."

Fernandes' comments come a week after defender Lisandro Martinez made similar headlines following United's previous Champions League defeat by Atletico Madrid.

The Argentine said he expected more investment in the squad and "more from our technical director [Jason Wilcox]". He later apologised.

And while Martinez's outburst sparked debate, Fernandes' comments could have even larger repercussions.

Carrick, who was under huge pressure already following a terrible start to the season, said in reply: "There's a little bit of whatever system you play, whatever tactics you play, if you don't run, and you don't run forward, and you don't run back, you aren't going to win."

United have not won in their past seven games since beating Champions League minnows Sabah. They have just one win in their seven Premier League games this season.

'Simply not good enough - Warnock on Man Utd's Como display

Not many people have defended Fernandes' public questioning of the tactics.

Former Leicester and Celtic keeper Kasper Schmeichel, whose dad Peter won the Champions League, five Premier League titles and three FA Cups as a United player, said on BBC Radio 5 Live: "Given last week and what's happened, he probably should not be saying these things because it's only going to add to the problems.

"With experience you learn that you need to keep these things in-house. It's a problem, with everything that happened during the week.

"Michael Carrick didn't need this, the club didn't need this. It's exposing all of the issues that are at Manchester United.

"It's very plain to see that there are loads of issues but you need to be fixing them, you need to be having meetings within the club, which I'm sure that they are, but it's obviously not resolved. It's just translating on to the pitch."

The issue that most pundits have is not that Fernandes has these opinions - but that he has let the world know them.

Former Liverpool defender Stephen Warnock, speaking on BBC Match of the Day, said: "I think there will be ex-players now from Manchester United saying, 'why are you letting this get out, this should be a collective in the changing room where we come together'.

"Whether you like the manager or not, you've got to come together and say 'OK how do we correct this?'

"Senior players go to the manager and say 'listen we didn't agree with the tactics, we didn't think we got it right, how do we go about this, how do we move forward', because you start airing it in public and it doesn't end well for anyone."

Former Chelsea midfielder Joe Cole on TNT Sports called it "sacrilege".

"I've played for managers where I don't agree with the tactics but you might say that to your mate or to your wife. You don't come out as captain of the club and question the manager," he said.

Andy Mitten, editor of United We Stand, told BBC 5 Live Breakfast that Fernandes has "thrown his coach under the bus".

"And Manchester United fans are divided as to whether he should or should not do that. Last week, Martinez was also critical. A lot of Manchester United fans agreed with him.

"Again, there's mitigation, he wants a new contract. That's because he's not been playing enough, because he's been injured. And injuries are a real problem for Manchester United. There's no recognised centre-forward. So it is a really difficult time for United."

Former United midfielder Carrick, who took over in January initially as caretaker manager, seemed to blank his players at full-time before heading over to the United fans.

"I don't blame him for ignoring the players," said Mitten.

"He went to the fans and he held his hands up and he said, 'I'm sorry'.

"You can tell he's hurting. He's a Manchester United fan himself. He was a season ticket holder only a year ago before he took the job. I think he's trying his best, but it's just not working at the moment. Manchester United are really, really struggling."

Bruno Fernandes has been United's captain for three years

Fernandes has been Manchester United's one consistent outstanding player since a Â£47m move from Sporting in January 2020, when Ole Gunnar Solskjaer was the manager.

He has scored 111 goals and made 106 assists in 338 games for the club in all competitions.

Former manager Erik ten Hag made him the skipper in the summer of 2023 after taking the armband off Harry Maguire, whose place in the team was far from certain.

But this is not the first time his body language, attitude and behaviour as captain has been criticised.

He was called a "disgrace" and "embarrassing" by United legends Gary Neville and Roy Keane after the 7-0 loss to Liverpool in 2023, while Chris Sutton said he should never captain the club again.

However, last season Fernandes was named the Premier League's player of the year after a record 21 assists in a season.

"That award was on the back of Michael Carrick becoming the manager," said Carragher.

"He wouldn't have got that award without Michael Carrick, because Michael Carrick put him back in his rightful position and he then went on to have a fantastic season because he's a talented footballer.

"But in terms of attitude and the way he goes about things, it's not right at all."

Fernandes has made headlines with his comments before. In August 2025 he said the club needed to make more signings.

That December he was asked about the fact United had been willing to sell him to Al-Hilal and told Canal 11: "From the club I felt it was, 'If you go it's not so bad for us'. It hurts me a bit.

"More than hurting me, it makes me sad. I'm a player that no-one can criticise, I'm always available, I always give my best.

"I could have left in this transfer window, I would have earned much more money.

"I would have won many trophies this season, but I decided not to go, not only for family reasons but because I genuinely love the club."

But now the narrative might change entirely after his latest comments.

Many will be wondering whether he should even start in Sunday's game against Bournemouth.

Martinez did keep his place, after apologising, at Leeds last Sunday.

But as United fan Mitten says: ''There was mitigation with Martinez because he wants a new contract. Fernandes is captain."

There has been no word from the club about whether Fernandes' role as captain is in jeopardy after his outburst.

#58. Major blow for Carrick: 23yo Man Utd star could be sidelined until 2027

Manchester United fear that striker Benjamin Sesko could be sidelined until the new year, as reported by The Sun.

Sesko has not featured since the Manchester Derby five weeks ago.

He has been struggling with a shin injury that had already kept him out for several months before the start of the 2026-27 season.

The club have reportedly put the 23-year-old through a series of tests over the past few weeks to establish the full extent of the problem and determine how long he could be unavailable.

United now fear that Sesko could miss around four months of action, potentially ruling him out until January and dealing a significant setback to their plans for the season.

Joshua Zirkzee is the natural alternative, but the Dutchman has yet to prove that he can be trusted to lead the line consistently when Sesko is unavailable.

There were already warning signs last season, when Sesko battled a series of minor injuries but still managed to finish the campaign with 12 goals across all competitions.

His tally was joint-highest at the club, with Bryan Mbeumo also netting 12 goals.

Expectations were high for the Slovenia international, but his poor luck with injuries has forced him out again.

United must now seriously consider bringing in another centre-forward during the January transfer window to ease the goalscoring burden and avoid being left short up-front.

Mbeumo and Matheus Cunha have both been deployed centrally this campaign, but neither looks as threatening through the middle as they do when given the freedom to attack from wider positions.

#59. Bruno Fernandes criticises Michael Carrick tactics as sack looms for Manchester United boss

Bruno Fernandes appeared to call out Michael Carrick’s tactics in Manchester United’s 4-1 defeat by Como.

The Red Devils were thrashed in the Champions League by Cesc Fabregas’ men, with Nico Paz playing a starring role.

The result piled the pressure on Carrick, who has now overseen a seven-game winless streak as he continues his first season as the club’s permanent head coach.

The former midfielder held his hands up to the travelling United support in Italy to apologise for the performance, and he faces huge criticism from those on social media, with plenty calling for a change in the hotseat.

Fernandes, who was substituted uncharacteristically in the second half, criticised his manager’s strategy in the dire drubbing.

"It's not the first time, we started on the wrong foot, and they scored very early,” the United captain explained.

“Not much pressure on the ball, not many good movements with the ball, not many things going our way.

"Conceding early makes us go into a game with a mood of defeat, it's difficult to get out of that, trying to be brave, to have the ball and make something happen. If we are not able to do that, it is going to be very difficult to get out of this situation.

"It's a decision from the staff to not press man to man and sometimes leave space behind and try to get the ball, wait for them, but sometimes when you give space to these teams they get confidence."

He continued: "That's on the manager and staff to see, not me. Sometimes you have to be more brave under pressure, take the risk of pressing high and making them uncomfortable.

#60. ‘It’s really not a drama’: Carrick sticks to the script after Martínez storm

Manchester United head coach produced a masterclass in dialling down emotion before a tricky trip to Leeds

Michael Carrick returned his most impressive performance precisely when required as he took questions in the midst of the first crisis engulfing him as Manchester United’s manager.

His team are bombing in the league after the club’s poorest start since 1986 and out of the Carabao Cup, and after Tuesday’s Champions League defeat at Atlético Madrid Lisandro Martínez showed up ruptures at United by calling out the director of football, Jason Wilcox.

All of this two months into a first full campaign piloting United after last term ended so rosily for the former United midfielder.

On the final day of last season, United won 3-0 at Brighton and Carrick secured an impressive third-place finish, 48 hours after agreeing a two-year deal to continue as head coach.

By last Saturday, United had six points from the first six games after drawing 1-1 with Tottenham at Old Trafford, placing Carrick’s job in jeopardy as the walls closed in on him a fortnight before the clocks go back.

A game at Leeds in January proved to be Ruben Amorim’s last in charge at Manchester United after he greeted the 1-1 draw by demanding his colleagues in the recruitment department “do their job”. With an uncanny circularity Carrick heads to Elland Road on Sunday after Martínez made similar comments. Amorim’s team were sixth going into that game; Carrick’s start the weekend 13th.

On Friday, though, came a masterclass from Carrick in dialling down emotion as Martínez’s explosive comments about Wilcox – “I expect more [investment], 100%. I expect more from our technical director” – were addressed and he repeatedly referred to the “really good feeling” at the club.

“It’s really not a drama for us as a group,” he said. “The boys have trained really well. This morning there was a really good feeling around the squad … so we’ve moved on pretty quickly.”

If this seems less than credible, and almost certainly a stance coached by United’s PR mandarins, what else would Carrick be expected to say? More obvious was how the zen-like performance required from the club’s leader was being delivered. Even when he broke from the script to interrupt an inquisitor this strengthened the sense of Carrick being in control – hardly easy given the heightened glare on United since Tuesday evening.

When the positive vibes trope was challenged by a questioner saying: “We’ll have to take you at face value”, Carrick’s interjection came with a smile. “I’m telling you the truth when I say that,” he said. “Do you not believe me, no? The players totally believe me. I’m telling you the truth. The squad today was in a fantastic place, better than I’ve seen it for a while, actually, and that’s it, plain and simple, you know? We’re emotional, we’re passionate, we care a lot.”

When signing on to be the permanent United manager, Carrick had overseen only 21 games at elite level – in two caretaker spells at the club. United had 17 league games to play when he replaced Amorim, and Carrick has shown signs of faltering this season when having to compete on four fronts.

Elimination by Brighton at the earliest stage of the Carabao Cup was not a great calling card regarding an ability to juggle competitions, and there was a surprising reaction from him after the 2-0 opening-day loss at Hull. He bridled at the suggestion United had lacked “passion” during a first-half horror show. “Oh come,” Carrick said. “That’s ridiculous.”

A faulty United rearguard is hampering an attack that is functioning far better and has the most shots (125) and most touches in the opposition box (205) in the Premier League. Nine goals from six matches is not paltry but greater ruthlessness would transform a promising front six into a potent one. Carrick has to find the right blend from Benjamin Sesko, Matheus Cunha, Bryan Mbeumo, Marcus Rashford, Amad Diallo, Bruno Fernandes, Kobbie Mainoo, Youri Tielemans, Andrey Santos and Carlos Baleba.

Kick off your evenings with the Guardian's take on the world of football

Baleba has sparkled in his first two appearances – against Spurs and Atlético – and might have provided the shield from the No 6 berth to ensure a less leaky defence had he not arrived injured.

This has to be the hope for Carrick. Playing Tielemans at No 10 at Atlético suggests the manager may doubt the 29-year-old’s legs, and questions have been raised about whether United are putting in enough of the hard yards. Their 671.4km distance covered in the league is the fifth-lowest (Arsenal’s 716.2km is the highest), and their 581 sprints the fourth-lowest (Brentford’s 765 the most).

The trip to an always febrile Elland Road that saves a particular welcome for their cross-Pennines rivals presents a stern challenge. Leeds are ninth and have lost once.

“It’s a great rivalry,” Carrick said, who was full of praise for his Leeds counterpart, Daniel Farke. “There’s big history between the clubs, the areas. That’s what makes these types of games special. You’ve got to embrace the history, what it means.

“I’m sure it will be a fantastic atmosphere on Sunday. It brings an intensity and it’ll be a little bit extra again, on Sunday against us … Of course, we have to be ready, and the boys will be.”

Carrick requires a positive result, though in denying this, he again sounded the right note. “I don’t need results, we all need results,” he said. “It’s not about me. Manchester United is a lot bigger than any individual, bigger than me, bigger than any player, anyone else who works at this club. It’s not about me, so please …

“I don’t go into every day at work, in this situation, this game, thinking about me. It’s about what we stand for, what we live for, the history, the things that we need to live up to, and that’s what drives us. So whatever’s best for the club and for the team on Sunday is the most important thing.”

#61. Como 4-1 Manchester United: Pressure piles on Michael Carrick after Champions League crushing

Manchester United fell to one of the most embarrassing European defeats in their history as Champions League upstarts Como ran out 4-1 victors to heap pressure on head coach Michael Carrick.

The under-fire Red Devils boss had felt Sunday’s purposeful point at rivals Leeds marked a “fresh start” after Lisandro Martinez highlighted the club’s shortcomings in a set of stunning interviews last week.

But United instead saw their wretched start to the season hit a new low on Wednesday as Cesc Fabregas’ Como punished a display devoid of shape, grit and skill to extend their winless run to a seventh match.

This humbling loss puts Carrick’s future under intense scrutiny, with the storm that battered the Stadio Giuseppe Sinigaglia nothing compared to the one brewing at Old Trafford.

United made a nightmare start, just as they did at Atletico Madrid last week, as burgeoning star Nico Paz struck against a side that were shaky out of possession and shapeless with it.

Carrick’s side were fortunate to only be a goal behind at the break and their luck soon ran out, with Tasos Douvikas’ strike followed by Paz scoring his second and Jacobo Ramon heading home.

Substitute Joshua Zirkzee added a consolation goal on a night that ended with Carrick holding his hands up apologetically in front of those United fans that stayed until the end.

United were without nine players in Italy but Como were also beset by injuries and managed to play bold, joined-up football that had the visitors chasing shadows.

Things could have been different had Diogo Dalot scored inside 90 seconds but that miss was followed by Fabregas’ men scoring a dream opener with two minutes on the clock.

Paz collected a pass on the edge of the box and caught out United by immediately cutting onto his left foot and bending the ball brilliantly past Senne Lammens.

There was little sign of life beyond Harry Maguire’s header wide, with United’s understandably frustrated following bellowing “attack, attack, attack”.

But Como looked most likely to score as Douvikas showed what the presence of an out-and-out striker can do, forcing a save from Lammens and driving over towards the end of the first half.

Carrick reacted by making changes at the break, bringing on Carlos Baleba and Amad Diallo as well as making some tactical tweaks, but just four minutes after the restart Como scored again.

Incisive attacking play cut through the middle of the United team, with Douvikas slamming home from Paz’s pass having left Matthijs De Ligt stumbling on the defender’s first start in 325 days.

United looked punch drunk and the 57th minute brought Como’s third. Baleba saw a pass cut out on the flank by Paz, who darted forward and scored a fine left-footed finish.

Carrick redoubled their efforts and Diallo was denied by Jean Butez during that push, but the damage had already been done.

In fact, the hosts would inflict further harm in the 76th minute when substitute Kaiki Bruno crossed for unmarshalled Ramon to head home in front of the raucous Como support.

United’s end emptied as their side stumbled on, with Lammens preventing Paz from completing his hat-trick.

Substitute Zirkzee scored in stoppage time but there was no disguising what a catastrophic display this was on the shores of Lake Como. Little wonder Carrick felt the need to apologise.

#62. Not Como: These games could decide Michael Carrick's future at Man Utd - view

Manchester United have had a dismal start to the Premier League season, picking up only seven points from as many matches.

The club are languishing 13th in the table and are already six points adrift of fourth-placed Chelsea for the final Champions League spot.

United will be in Champions League action at Como on the road tonight, but the game may not have much of a bearing on manager Michael Carrick’s future.

The Red Devils are without nine first-team stars for the European game, but at least four could be back in the squad for the league clash against Bournemouth at home.

Leny Yoro (suspension), Lisandro Martinez (thigh), Marcus Rashford (hip) and Ayden Heaven (illness) are likely to be involved versus the Cherries on Sunday.

Bournemouth have had a poor start like United after their managerial change, and they have picked up just six points. Their only win came over the weekend against Sunderland.

Hence, United should be the favourites on paper with home advantage, and anything less than a victory against the South Coast outfit would increase the pressure on Carrick.

The hierarchy’s stance on his future may not change by the result, but the next set of fixtures before the international break could be a make-or-break for the English tactician.

United travel to Chelsea thereafter before hosting Roma (UCL) and Aston Villa in quick succession. If United’s winless league streak does not end by then, Carrick could be dismissed.

The 45-year-old is one of the leading candidates for the sack at the moment, but things could change quickly in football. A couple of victories could alleviate the pressure.

On a positive note, a victory over Como would be a huge confidence booster heading into the Bournemouth game, where another win could change the entire perception of Carrick’s future.

#63. Tielemans’ Leeds Display Gives Carrick More to Weigh

Youri Tielemans broke down the right and supplied the pass Bryan Mbeumo turned into Manchester United’s opener against Leeds, adding three key passes and four duels won to a display that sharpens Michael Carrick’s midfield selection question.

United were held to a 1-1 draw at Elland Road, leaving them with one league win in seven. The Peoples Person highlighted Tielemans’ contribution in a more advanced role, where his creativity offered Carrick a clear positive amid growing pressure on the manager.

Tielemans featured higher up the pitch with Carlos Baleba starting alongside Andrey Santos, a midfield arrangement used in each of the last two matches, according to The Peoples Person. He made the most of the opportunity in the 35th minute, advancing down the right before delivering the precise pass Mbeumo finished to put United ahead.

Leeds drew level after the break through Dominic Calvert-Lewin, and neither side found a winner. Tielemans continued for 84 minutes before making way for Matheus Cunha, one of the attacking changes Carrick introduced as he pursued a late goal.

🔴 Follow Stretty News on Google as a preferred source and be first with every United update.

The assist was the headline contribution, but Tielemans also created two big chances and completed two of his three crosses. He attempted 28 passes and completed 22, while his three key passes show that the opener was not his only meaningful contribution to United’s attacking play.

His 78.6 per cent pass accuracy came alongside a blocked shot and defensive work: one interception and three clearances. Tielemans contested nine duels, winning two in the air and two on the ground. He did not put a shot on target, so the performance was about providing and competing rather than adding a goal threat of his own.

Tielemans has been playing regularly and has become an important part of Carrick’s plans, the primary report says. The manager’s recent change has given him a more attacking assignment while Baleba and Santos start behind him; the Leeds display showed how that positioning can bring his passing into the decisive areas without removing his defensive contribution.

That is a useful argument for retaining the arrangement, but one assist does not settle the selection debate. United’s league form leaves little room for a draw to be treated as enough, and Carrick needs the midfield to help turn promising attacking moments into wins. The midfield roles of Tielemans and Andrey Santos are part of the wider selection picture, while Baleba’s place in the midfield mix adds another decision for the manager to balance.

Carrick also brought on Mason Mount and Cunha in search of a winner, but United could not find one. His next choice is whether to keep Tielemans in the advanced position with Baleba and Santos supporting him, or adjust the setup again; the Belgian’s two-match run in the role has now produced a substantial individual contribution at Elland Road.

#65. 'Sack him tonight': Manchester United fans turn on Michael Carrick after Como thrashing

Manchester United fans turned on Michael Carrick after the Red Devils’ 4-1 thrashing by Como on Wednesday.

The Premier League outfit were pulled apart at will by Cesc Fabregas’ Serie A side, as the hosts were rampant by the Lake in the Champions League.

The defeat extended United’s winless run to seven games in all competitions, and leaves them 22nd in the league phase table, and 13th in the Premier League standings.

Carrick held up both hands in apology to the travelling faithful at the full-time whistle, and he faces further criticism after ‘Carrick Out’ started trending on X.

United fans let the full wrath of their emotions out on social media, with popular yet controversial personality Mark Goldbridge, who fronts The Overlap’s ‘Stick to United’ podcast and ‘The United Stand’ YouTube channel, simply tweeting: “Sack him tonight”.

Another fan wrote that the job was “far beyond” Carrick, who was “out of his depth, doesn’t inspire confidence, and clearly can't do anything with this team.”

Others also pointed fingers at the under-fire manager, with one remarking that there was “no coming back from this”, and another labelling the result “embarrassing”, and that if Carrick were not sacked “immediately, [then] this club and the powers that be have no ambition or standards.”

United were outplayed throughout at the Giuseppe Sinigaglia Stadium, with Nico Paz, the former Real Madrid prodigy, running the show.

He notched the opener inside two minutes before Anastasios Douvikas doubled the hosts’ advantage.

Paz had another on 57 minutes before Jacobo Ramon completed the rout late on. Joshua Zirkzee snared a consolation for the visitors, but it was too little, too late on a dark night for Carrick and his injury-hit side.

#66. Carrick brushes off Martínez’s failure to apologise publicly to United director Wilcox

Manchester United coach accepts defender’s apology

‘He’s realised he’s made a mistake and that’s enough’

Michael Carrick brushed off Lisandro Martínez’s failure to offer Jason Wilcox a public apology after the defender criticised Manchester United’s director of football in the wake of Tuesday’s defeat at Atlético Madrid.

Martínez referred directly to Wilcox after the 2-0 Champions League loss, saying: “I expect more from our technical director.”

On Thursday Martínez issued an apology that did not mention Wilcox or withdraw the essence of his outburst: the need for more squad investment. “The frustration of the defeat led me to express how I felt at a time and place that perhaps were not the right ones, and for that I want to apologise,” he said.

Carrick was asked whether he respected Martínez for not saying sorry to Wilcox. “He’s released his statement, he’s realised he’s made a mistake and that’s enough for all of us to move on with,” United’s manager said.

Carrick was pressed on whether it would have been better if Martínez had apologised to Wilcox and for his wider comments about investment, rather than just saying sorry for the timing and location of his interview. “From our point of view, the acknowledgment of what’s been done has certainly been enough, we’ve moved on and we’re definitely not looking back at it now,” he replied.

Carrick said Martínez was available for Sunday’s trip to Leeds, where Manchester United go having taken only six points from their opening six league games. They were knocked out of the Carabao Cup by Brighton last month and have one win and a defeat in the Champions League. Poor form has increased the pressure on Carrick, who was asked whether Martínez’s comments were an unwanted distraction that had annoyed him.

“No, I understand emotion at times,” he said. “We ask for passion, we ask for emotion, we ask for fire and we ask for players to care. There’s a way of doing that, and that’s already been acknowledged, but we need to carry that intensity and that willingness and that togetherness and that spirit. As a group, we need to use it, and we’ve got to use it in the right way.”

Wayne Rooney, United’s record goalscorer, claimed Martínez’s comments “will rip the dressing room apart”. It was put to Carrick that they may have an opposite, unifying effect.

“There’s a lot of things that can affect the group in all sorts of different ways,” said Carrick, who went on to highlight his players’ reaction to conceding inside a minute at Atlético. “It’s one of the toughest places to play in the Champions League, so to go a goal down so early, and the way we responded shows an underlying belief and spirit and a togetherness,” he said.

Kick off your evenings with the Guardian's take on the world of football

He indicated that events on and off the pitch this week had brought the squad together. “Certain situations can galvanise the group, and it’s certainly galvanised the group as this week’s progressed. The boys are in a really good place right now.”

Carrick was asked why anyone outside the club, including former players such as Rooney who have criticised Martínez, should believe there is unity. “There’s a lot of people that say a lot of things, and ex-pros or not ex-pros, it doesn’t necessarily mean you understand what’s going on here,” he said. “I’m telling you the truth. The squad today [Friday] was in a fantastic place, better than I’ve seen it for a while.”

Carrick said Luke Shaw and Kobbie Mainoo faced fitness tests.

#67. Man United could overlook proven winner in search for Michael Carrick replacement

Ruairidh is a leading expert in La Liga and Spanish football. He's the editor of the #1 Spanish football publication... read more

Stan Collymore is a football pundit and former player, signing for Liverpool FC in 1995 for an English transfer record... read more

Manchester United reportedly have reservations about appointing Antonio Conte if they decide to part ways with Michael Carrick, despite the Italian’s availability and proven record at the highest level.

Carrick is under severe pressure following United’s 4-1 Champions League defeat to Como, a result that extended their winless run to seven matches in all competitions.

Several managers are now being discussed internally, but Conte may not fit the profile senior figures at Old Trafford currently want.

According to The Sun, Conte recently spent around 24 hours in the UK during the international break, but sources close to the former Chelsea and Tottenham manager insist he did not meet Man United representatives.

United have also held no formal negotiations with the 57-year-old.

Sources in Italy reportedly believe the “doors are closed” from United’s side, largely because of concerns over Conte’s demanding and sometimes abrasive management style.

That is significant because Conte remains one of the most successful managers currently available. He won the Premier League with Chelsea, Serie A titles with Juventus and Inter Milan, and most recently guided Napoli to the 2024/25 Italian championship.

Instead, Brighton manager Fabian Hürzeler is reportedly considered the standout Premier League candidate by senior figures at United, although extracting him from Brighton would require a substantial compensation package.

There are other names in contention too. talkSPORT reports that former Germany manager Julian Nagelsmann has also been sounded out as United assess their options.

United’s apparent hesitation over Conte is understandable.

There is little doubt he can improve teams quickly. His organisation, intensity and demand for high standards could be exactly what an underperforming United squad needs in the short term.

Conte has historically demanded significant control over recruitment and has rarely stayed at clubs for long periods. Man United have already been through repeated managerial resets, so choosing another high-intensity short-term appointment could simply restart the same cycle.

Hürzeler would represent the opposite approach. He is younger, tactically progressive and potentially better suited to building a long-term football structure.

Conte offers proven success. Hürzeler offers potential and continuity. United’s decision will therefore say a great deal about whether they want another immediate fix or are finally prepared to commit to a longer-term identity.

#69. Three reported absences leave Carrick short for Leeds

The Peoples Person reports that Benjamin Sesko, Patrick Dorgu and Noussair Mazraoui did not travel with Manchester United for the Leeds fixture, leaving Michael Carrick without options in attack and at full-back.

The report is based on information from club insider Sully, rather than an official squad announcement. Sesko remains out of action, while Dorgu and Mazraoui are unavailable for the trip to Elland Road.

The three absences add to the fitness questions Carrick must manage as United prepare for a difficult away match against Daniel Farke’s Leeds. A Manchester United injury update had already covered Sesko’s unavailability and other players facing checks.

Sesko has been sidelined since sustaining a shin injury against Brighton & Hove Albion in the League Cup in September. Carrick said before the Leeds fixture that the striker was still undergoing checks, with the club not ready to give a clear return timeline.

The United manager’s update made clear that Sesko was not as close to a return as some of the other players being assessed. The striker’s absence removes an attacking option for Carrick, although the report does not establish who will replace him in the starting side.

🔴 Follow Stretty News on Google as a preferred source and be first with every United update.

Dorgu and Mazraoui both picked up injury knocks while representing Denmark and Morocco, respectively. The report says neither full-back travelled with the squad; Dorgu is expected to remain out for a little longer, while Mazraoui trained during the week and is close to returning.

Patrick Dorgu and Noussair Mazraoui’s injury concerns

United were also without Kobbie Mainoo, Luke Shaw and Sesko for the midweek match against Atletico Madrid. Carrick said he would assess Shaw and Mainoo ahead of Leeds, leaving their availability as a separate consideration from the three reported absentees.

The Peoples Person notes that Carrick has recently used Leny Yoro and Ayden Heaven in wide defensive positions to cover for the shortage of full-backs. With Dorgu and Mazraoui both reported absent, those kinds of adjustments remain possible, but the report does not confirm a particular lineup.

Carrick described Sesko’s situation as unresolved when discussing his checks and the lack of a firm timeline:

“Ben’s kind of still going undergoing a couple of kind of checks that we’re really trying to- before we kind of update on timelines of everything, we’re just trying to be clear with what that looks like, so he’s still a little bit further away.”

The Leeds match is scheduled for Sunday, October 18, at Elland Road. United’s final team selection will show how Carrick balances the reported absences with the fitness checks on Shaw and Mainoo.

Armer signed his first professional contract last summer, and a senior debut against Como would represent a significant milestone in his development.

#71. Will Como vs Manchester United go ahead and what happens if game is postponed?

Manchester United’s trip to face Como in the Champions League is under threat of being cancelled due to severe thunderstorms in northern Italy - but what would happen in the event of the match being postponed?

The Red Devils have already made the trip to Lombardy, with Michael Carrick undergoing press duties on Tuesday. However, with an orange weather alert issued by Italian authorities, there is a real chance tonight’s game is postponed.

As a rule, UEFA attempts to rearrange fixtures by pushing kick-off back to the next day.

In Article 27 of the Champions League regulations, pertaining to rescheduling of matches, UEFA states: “If it is decided on the day of a match that the match cannot start or cannot be played in full, the full or remaining match time is, as a rule, played the next day.”

For example, Manchester City’s group stage fixture against Borussia Monchengladbach was postponed due to weather in 2016, with the game moved from Tuesday to Wednesday.

UEFA attempts to reschedule games by just one day even under the most unprecedented of circumstances. When Borussia Dortmund’s team bus was attacked by bombs in April 2017, their Champions League quarter-final tie at home to AS Monaco was pushed back by just 24 hours.

Dortmund went on to lose the first leg 3-2, before a 3-1 Monaco victory in the second leg knocked the German side out of the competition.

Man United are not scheduled to play again in the Premier League until Sunday, when they welcome Bournemouth to Old Trafford. This would represent enough rest days if the game was rearranged for Thursday, but Como are in action against Sassuolo on Saturday.

Arsenal XI vs Bayern: Predicted lineup and confirmed team news

Bayern Munich vs Arsenal: Prediction, Champions League betting tips & odds

Ex-Man Utd boss reveals how Cristiano Ronaldo 'let the team down'

Rediscover your adventurous side on an exotic getaway

As such, any postponement until Thursday would have likely cause further rescheduling for Como.

UEFA will likely push for the game to be played on Thursday if it is postponed this evening, but there is a provision for the game to be rescheduled for later in the season.

In the regulations it is explained: “If the match cannot be rescheduled for the next day, the UEFA administration fixes a new date. This may entail exceptions to the regular match calendar.”

While next week Manchester United have a free midweek due to their early exit at the hands of Brighton in the Carabao Cup, Como are currently slated to play Torino in Serie A on Tuesday, October 27.

The week after, the next round of Champions League fixtures take place, before another international break arrives in the second week of November.

When club action resumes there are no free midweeks for Manchester United, with the fifth round of Champions League fixtures staged on Tuesday 24th and Wednesday 25th of November.

By then the Premier League’s packed December schedule kicks in, but there is one free midweek for the Red Devils, sandwiched between a trip to Crystal Palace and the visit of Arsenal to Old Trafford.

However, there is again a hitch - Como will be enjoying the Italian winter break between December 19 and January 3. Despite this fact, UEFA may order the game to take place the week commencing December 21.

#72. Premier League: 10 things to look out for this weekend

Henderson back on Merseyside, Farke threatens more misery for Carrick and Forest are a threat to Arsenal

Premier League top scorers | Follow us on TikTok

A lively reception awaits the former Liverpool midfielders Jordan Henderson and Xabi Alonso at Everton and, in the case of the former, any jeers can be considered an unintentional tribute. They will speak of what the 36-year-old is able to produce now as much as what, and where, he delivered in the past. With two goals and an assist against Bournemouth last Saturday, Henderson became the oldest player to contribute directly to three goals in a Premier League game, and demonstrated his role at Chelsea is not simply to elevate standards off the pitch. His midfield partnership with Moisés Caicedo gave Alonso’s team balance and enabled the front three of Morgan Rogers, João Pedro and Cole Palmer to flourish. Their confrontation against an Everton defence that had impressed this season, only to litter the draw at Hull with costly mistakes, will provide a stern test of the hosts’ unbeaten start and may well be dictated by the veteran behind them. Andy Hunter

Everton v Chelsea, Saturday 12.30pm (all times BST)

Still unbeaten in the top flight, Brentford are being served well by their commitment to pragmatism. Keith Andrews’ side like to sit deep, close off the middle of the pitch and force opponents to play around them, while flooding the opposition box at set pieces and throw-ins allows them to create the chances from which most of their goals arrive. In spanking six past Lask, the Austrian champions, Liverpool showed how good they can be on the front foot, but they will face far less obliging hosts at the Gtech Community Stadium. If Andoni Iraola’s side can dominate the ball and move it quickly enough to penetrate Brentford’s rearguard, they could win comfortably. However, the Bees’ efforts to turn the afternoon into a saloon brawl and capitalise on dead-ball situations may help to maintain their unbeaten run. Barry Glendenning

Gonzalo García has one league goal from 18 shots this season. The striker joined Fulham from Real Madrid in the summer and has looked promising in patches. García is 22 and capable of getting on the end of chances. He has an expected goals tally of 3.60 but is underperforming with his finishing. The challenge is to become more clinical. Fulham, bottom of the league, have six goals in six games and García is without a goal since scoring on his debut, against Chelsea. Álvaro Arbeloa has put a lot of faith in his fellow Spaniard. The head coach is under increasing pressure and winless Fulham need García to fire when they host Hull. Jacob Steinberg

Even when losing for the first time this season in Wednesday’s 4-2 Champions League defeat by Paris Saint-Germain, Manchester City appeared the real deal as they rallied from 3-0 down after 32 minutes to give the European champions a scare. Enzo Maresca deserves credit for following Pep Guardiola serenely; his City, so far, are a free-flowing attacking machine and invigorating to watch. If not for the guilty verdict against the club for serious financial breaches of Premier League rules, the perma-twinkling Italian might be more widely lauded. Ipswich last won at City in the competition in November 2000: do not expect the statistic to change on Saturday. Jamie Jackson

Newcastle’s latest attempt to play vertical football left Matthias Jaissle’s side horizontal at Coventry on Monday night. That 4-1 defeat has prompted quite an inquest on Tyneside, where Jaissle could do without his players falling flat on their faces once more when Aston Villa visit St James’ Park. Jaissle maintains that his vertical football is the brand of high-octane, aggressive attacking that Newcastle’s hierarchy believes should become the club’s default, and is the way fans want to see the team play. That argument is not without validity but it is also worth remembering that, less than a decade ago, under Rafael Benítez’s charge, Newcastle often played a low block bolstered by three at the back, rarely took unnecessary risks and improved steadily. Admittedly resources were thinner then but the fans almost universally adored Benítez and were heartbroken when he eventually tired of Mike Ashley’s ownership and resigned. Quite apart from making his dislike for chaotic football plain, the Spaniard was a realist who knew a gung-ho approach would spell relegation. Granted, tactical fashions have changed, but Jaissle could do worse than slow things down a bit and search for his inner Rafa against Villa. Louise Taylor

With nine games each scheduled for the month between international breaks, Bournemouth v Sunderland will give an early indication of how both will cope during their first experience of such a relentless Thursday-Sunday autumnal grind. Sunderland are 17th, Bournemouth are 18th, and with seven points between them after six Premier League rounds, scaling similarly dizzy heights to last season already seems a lofty goal. For both clubs, European campaigns are far too much of an exciting novelty to be considered anything like a chore. In circumstances where squad depth is crucial, the returns of Justin Kluivert for the hosts and Brian Brobbey for the visitors will be welcomed by Marco Rose and Régis Le Bris. It would be no great surprise to see either Dutch international make a game-deciding contribution in what is already a six-pointer on the south coast. BG

Kick off your evenings with the Guardian's take on the world of football

Brighton’s momentum appears unstoppable at the moment, although supporters will be wary of Sunday’s showdown against Crystal Palace given their recent history. They haven’t beaten Palace since a 4-1 thrashing at the Amex in February 2024, when Roberto De Zerbi was in charge, while Fabian Hürzeler managed only a point in four encounters with the Eagles under Oliver Glasner. Both are having to juggle European commitments this season and Brighton will start as heavy favourites against Pierre Sage’s side after their superb start to the campaign. Palace have big concerns in defence, but can the Frenchman defy the odds against the Premier League’s top scorers? Ed Aarons

Michael Carrick has lost five of his past six technical-area duels with Daniel Farke. Indeed the only time Manchester United’s manager has bested his Leeds counterpart was in a League Cup tie during his Middlesbrough tenure. If such statistics are not sufficient to put Manchester United’s currently beleaguered manager off a trip to West Yorkshire on Sunday, there is a sense that Leeds are overdue a first victory at Elland Road since 2002 against their arch-enemies from across the Pennines. One thing is certain; it will be a typically raucous latter-day War of the Roses. But, right now, while the White Rose blooms, its red counterpart is wilting alarmingly. Will Carrick survive this latest meeting with a manager who, at Championship level during his Middlesbrough days, proved something of a nemesis? He will not care to remember that last January a 1-1 draw at Elland Road proved to be Ruben Amorim’s final game in charge of Manchester United. LT

Despite losing 3-1 to Crystal Palace last weekend, Nottingham Forest played some excellent front-foot football only to be let down by extreme profligacy in front of goal and vulnerability to their hosts’ counterattacks. Oliver Glasner attributed his side’s defeat to karma upon his return to Palace, saying: “It was like I didn’t deserve to win at Selhurst Park, because the players did everything.” When they welcome Arsenal to the City Ground on Sunday they will give the champions plenty to ponder if they can play as well again. Mikel Arteta’s side were unconvincing for 65 minutes against Leeds last weekend and failed to impress until they brought on their heavy artillery about the same time against Lille on Tuesday. If one or both of Morgan Gibbs-White and Liam Delap can convert the kind of chances they missed at Selhurst Park, Forest have every chance of getting something. BG

Nottingham Forest v Arsenal, Sunday 4.30pm

The last time Tottenham hosted Coventry in the Premier League, Steffen Iversen, Les Ferdinand and Serhiy Rebrov scored in a comfortable 3-0 win. And while Spurs fans will almost certainly have envisaged a similar scoreline when the fixtures were announced, they will now be viewing Monday night’s fixture with even more than the usual amount of apprehension and dread. Like Tottenham, Coventry’s main problem until recently was an inability to convert chances, but in their demolition of Newcastle on Monday they visibly grew in confidence and swagger as four different players took the plaudits. While it’s taken a while and they may still struggle, through back-to-back wins Frank Lampard’s side have now announced themselves as a Premier League force to be reckoned with, which is something Tottenham haven’t managed in more than two years. Bruno Fernandes has told Manchester United that they must stop living off the club’s past glories after Wednesday night’s humiliating 4-1 Champions League defeat to Como.

The result extended United’s winless run to seven matches in all competitions and piled even more pressure on Michael Carrick.

Fernandes, clearly frustrated after another damaging performance, believes the club needs to focus on building something new rather than constantly comparing the current team with successful United sides of previous generations.

Speaking to Sky Sport Italia after the defeat, Fernandes delivered a direct message about the mentality surrounding Old Trafford.

Bruno Fernandes: “Everyone at the club should understand that we are NO longer in the past”.

“We should look at the future, not at the past”, told Sky Italy.

His comments came after United were comprehensively outplayed by Cesc Fabregas’ Como side. Nico Paz scored twice as the Italian club exposed serious defensive and tactical weaknesses, while Joshua Zirkzee’s late goal did little more than make the scoreline slightly less embarrassing.

Fernandes also questioned United’s approach without the ball after the game, arguing that the team needed to be braver when pressing.

Sky Sports reported that Carrick responded by insisting intensity and effort were equally important regardless of the tactical system.

Fernandes’ message is difficult to argue with.

Man United have spent years measuring every new team against the standards set by Sir Alex Ferguson, but nostalgia does not fix recruitment, tactics or performances.

The bigger issue is whether the current United side is actually building towards a better future.

Against Como, there was little evidence of progress. United lacked organisation, struggled to press effectively and were repeatedly exposed when possession was lost.

Carrick can point towards effort, while Fernandes can question the tactical plan, but both arguments ultimately lead to the same conclusion: United remain badly short of the level expected.

Fernandes is therefore right that the club cannot keep living in the past. But Manchester United supporters will also want to know exactly what the future is supposed to look like.

At the moment, that answer remains worryingly unclear.

#74. Carrick says United have moved on amid reported defensive rebuild

Michael Carrick says Manchester United have moved on from Lisandro Martinez’s public criticism, while Sky Sports reports the club is targeting defensive reinforcements in upcoming transfer windows.

The comments came before United’s Premier League trip to Leeds United on Sunday, with Martinez’s remarks about the club’s recruitment and leadership putting the team’s direction under scrutiny.

BBC Sport’s account of Carrick’s press conference focused on the squad’s response and the need for results. The manager did not set out specific transfer targets or announce a new recruitment plan.

Carrick described the situation with Martinez as resolved after the defender acknowledged his comments. He said the squad had trained in good spirits and that the episode was not causing further drama within the group.

Martinez is expected to be in the squad for the Leeds match, according to Carrick’s comments reported by BBC Sport and Sky Sports. His criticism followed United’s 2-0 Champions League defeat by Atletico Madrid and questioned the club’s ambition in the market.

United have gone five matches without a win in all competitions. Asked about the pressure to deliver results, Carrick stressed that the club’s interests came before any individual, including himself. That is a pointed answer with the Reds needing a response on the pitch.

🔴 Follow Stretty News on Google as a preferred source and be first with every United update.

While Carrick did not outline a transfer blueprint in the reported press-conference remarks, Sky Sports News’ Rob Dorsett says United are preparing to revamp their defence in the next transfer windows. The report says the club is looking at centre-backs and left-backs.

There is also no indication in Carrick’s comments that he has publicly challenged director of football Jason Wilcox to speak more often. He declined to prescribe what Wilcox should do, saying he was comfortable answering questions and leading the team.

For now, the immediate test is Leeds. Carrick has framed the squad as united after the Martinez episode, while the reported defensive rebuild remains a matter for the coming windows, not a transfer announcement before Sunday’s fixture.

#75. Lisandro Martínez apologises for outburst after Manchester United defeat

Defender says sorry after talks with Michael Carrick

Any disciplinary action will not be made public by club

Lisandro Martínez has apologised for claiming that Jason Wilcox, Manchester United’s director of football, failed to strengthen sufficiently in the summer window after discussions with the manager, Michael Carrick, and club hierarchy on Thursday.

While any disciplinary action taken against him will not be made public by the club, it is thought that Martínez has been fined.

The defender’s apology came on Thursday evening after the 28-year-old failed to offer one when issuing an initial statement on Wednesday regarding his outburst following Tuesday’s 2-0 Champions League loss at Atlético Madrid.

He posted on Instagram: “Today I met with the club’s leadership and the manager. It was a very positive conversation, where we were able to exchange views and speak openly about what happened after Tuesday’s game.

“Everyone knows that I am a passionate person and that I live football with great intensity. The frustration of the defeat led me to express how I felt at a time and place that perhaps were not the right ones, and for that I want to apologise.”

The apology is a considerable climbdown. After the Atlético defeat, Martínez was scathing of Wilcox and his recruitment in the last window.

He said: “I expect more [investment], 100%. I expect more from our technical director [Wilcox]. As players we have to do more but it is also important when you make a strong squad to be ready for this kind of night. Last season we did everything, we created the base to create an amazing team.”

Martínez claimed that Carrick had not been backed sufficiently. “I don’t think so. He does his best. You can’t keep changing, changing, changing the manager. What about other areas?” he said.

While it is understood Martínez’s apology was his decision alone, United spent £155m in the market – all on midfielders, Andrey Santos, Youri Tielemans, and Carlos Baleba, despite Wilcox targeting a left-back.

Kick off your evenings with the Guardian's take on the world of football

The wider squad agrees with Martínez’s stance regarding recruitment and has been left frustrated following a poor start to the season, with Carrick under considerable pressure.

A return of six points from six league games is United’s poorest since 1986 and Carrick’s side have already been eliminated from the Carabao Cup by Brighton.

#77. 12 clubs ready to move for Man Utd ace in January, could leave for just £6m

Manchester United left-back Harry Amass could be on his way out when the transfer window reopens at the turn of the year, according to The Daily Mail.

The 19-year-old was heavily linked with a move away from Old Trafford last summer amid interest from the Championship. United were prepared to sell him for just £6 million.

The teenager has been starved of playing time despite injuries to left-backs Luke Shaw and Patrick Dorgu.

Right-back duo Noussair Mazraoui and Diogo Dalot, as well as centre-back Ayden Heaven, have been utilised from the left-back position, while Amass has been regularly ignored.

The Daily Mail now claim that there is a growing expectation that Amass will leave in January. A dozen Championship clubs are ready to enter the negotiation table for his services.

The youngster arrived from Watford a few years ago with plenty of potential.

He had a good loan stint with Sheffield Wednesday in the first half of last season, but United recalled him, given the club were languishing at the bottom of the Championship.

Amass joined Norwich City on loan last winter. He made just one substitute appearance for the Canaries before a long-term hamstring injury ruled him out for the campaign.

United failed to sign a quality left-back to support Shaw last summer, but Amass has still failed to convince manager Michael Carrick to give him more playing time.

Having played just 18 minutes in all competitions this term, Amass could definitely be on the move when the transfer window reopens at the beginning of the New Year.

A dozen Championship clubs seem interested. If United stick to the £6m price tag, we would expect them to include a big sell-on clause and an option to buy him back.
