#78. United debriefs reportedly less candid with Amazon cameras

Some Manchester United players reportedly believe Amazon’s All or Nothing cameras are making post-match dressing-room discussions less candid, according to The Sun via The Peoples Person. The claim offers a possible explanation for communication concerns during United’s poor start, but there is no established evidence that filming has affected results.

Amazon Prime’s All or Nothing is following United this season. The Sun’s report, relayed by The Peoples Person, says players and staff are holding back during post-match debriefs because cameras are present.

Those players reportedly feel the filming makes it harder to speak openly about what is going wrong and what needs to change. The concern is about frankness in the dressing room and after games; it does not establish that the cameras have caused United’s performances to suffer.

The Peoples Person itself notes that the cameras’ true effect on results is difficult to determine. That distinction matters: the reported frustration may point to a problem with the environment around the team, but the allegation is not an official finding.

🔴 Follow Stretty News on Google as a preferred source and be first with every United update.

The report lands against a difficult competitive backdrop. United were expected to challenge for major honours after finishing third in the Premier League, but six games into the new campaign they sit 14th and have already been eliminated from the Carabao Cup.

Michael Carrick has faced criticism over the team’s physicality and intensity, while Jason Wilcox has drawn scrutiny for not addressing squad weaknesses in the transfer window. Those are separate criticisms; the report does not establish that either issue is linked to filming.

United’s reported concerns also sit alongside wider questions about communication and unity within the squad. Questions over United’s squad unity have added to the scrutiny surrounding a team that has yet to find consistency.

The Peoples Person also points to Lisandro Martinez’s criticism of Wilcox’s transfer-market work after United’s Champions League defeat by Atletico Madrid. The report suggests that episode could have prompted a heated response inside the dressing room, though it does not confirm what was said privately.

Carrick could face increased pressure if results do not improve, the outlet adds, potentially giving the documentary further dramatic material. For now, the central claim remains that some players feel cameras are inhibiting their discussions-not that filming has been shown to explain United’s poor form.

In an interview with Sky Sport Italy after United’s heavy defeat to Como, Fernandes delivered a scathing analysis of the current situation at Old Trafford.

#80. Martínez has drawn back the curtain on Manchester United’s ruinous ownership | Jonathan Liew

Defender may not be blameless in team’s struggles but his criticism goes to the heart of what has gone wrong at the club

Heartbreaking: the worst defender you know just made a great point. And no, nobody would describe Lisandro Martínez as the ideal person to scrutinise Manchester United’s indifferent start to the season, given that his high-profile defensive errors against Fulham and Tottenham are partly responsible for it. “We’re all trying to find the guy who did this,” Martínez burbles on Amazon Prime after United’s 2-0 defeat by Atlético Madrid on Tuesday night, dressed in a hot dog costume and throwing his arms out in mock innocence.

For those with no interest in engaging with what Martínez actually had to say, this alone will be sufficient grounds to ignore him. Already you can sense the squeamishness of former players for whom the violation of an omertà is the real cardinal sin here, a phantom limb that still tingles years after they last stepped into a dressing room. “Disrespectful,” reckoned Wayne Rooney. “I don’t feel like he’s got the right,” agreed Ashley Young. “These things don’t end well, man,” Rio Ferdinand observed on his podcast.

But traducing the messenger will always be easier than hearing the message, even if Martínez’s views are widely shared within the United dressing room. If the fallout from the Manchester City legal case has demonstrated anything, it is English football’s ingrained reluctance to scrutinise the owners and executives who hold more influence over the sport than anybody else. Men in boots, not men in suits. Hey, watch the ball. It’s going to move!

All the same, it’s worth parsing exactly what Martínez was complaining about, because it’s a good deal more complex than it may appear on first viewing. Player calls out board – and specifically “technical director” Jason Wilcox – for failing to invest sufficiently in the squad over the summer. This was a summer in which United brought in three midfielders (Andrey Santos, Youri Tielemans, Carlos Baleba), a reserve goalkeeper (Karl Darlow), nobody up front and nobody in defence. So far, so simple.

But let’s begin to unpick the threads. Why did United not buy a defender in the summer, despite pursuing deals for Lewis Hall and Myles Lewis-Skelly? Because they were unable to sell a defender. There was no interest in Diogo Dalot or Noussair Mazraoui, nor in Harry Maguire, nor in Luke Shaw or Martínez, whose contracts are up next summer in any case. This is the product of recruitment decisions made and deals signed years ago. But why do United, one of the world’s most lucrative clubs, need to sell to buy in the first place?

Perhaps for the same reason they baulked at the asking price for Elliot Anderson and decided to sign the inconsistent Santos and the adequate but overpromoted Tielemans instead: this is a club in austerity measures, whose part-owner Jim Ratcliffe has been furiously cutting costs and raising ticket prices, determined to curb the years of excess that preceded him, where mediocre players were signed on ridiculous money, where failing coaches were paid to leave. But why would United, one of the most commercially successful clubs in footballing history, ever need to cut costs?

Perhaps as a result of the more than £1bn drained out of the club by the Glazers in their 21 years of ownership, not just in dividends but share sales and interest payments on the debt they undertook to buy the club, while neglecting the maintenance of Old Trafford and the training ground at Carrington. Whether he knows it or not, when Martínez is criticising the lack of investment he is in effect holding the Glazers accountable for more than two decades of avarice and mismanagement.

All of which lends a certain significance to Martínez’s intervention that goes beyond the churn of the weekly news cycle. Has any serving United player spoken out so frankly about the ruins of the Glazer ownership? Ole Gunnar Solskjær made a minor stand in early 2005, before they had arrived at the club. Cristiano Ronaldo threw some hand grenades over his shoulder a few days before he left for the second time in 2022. Countless others waited for the safety of retirement, when their disgruntlement could be monetised for personal gain, rather than used to stigmatise them, as Martínez will doubtless be stigmatised in the coming days.

Does any of this help United to win games in the short term? Almost certainly not. Martínez attempted to clarify his comments in a social media post on Wednesday night. He may still be dispatched to the bomb squad anyway, for the crime of pointing out that the emperor secured his throne almost entirely through the use of payment-in-kind loans.

Kick off your evenings with the Guardian's take on the world of football

Inevitably Michael Carrick too will be gone before long, away to the great Overlap podcast in the sky. A few months ago I wrote hopefully that Carrick might be the ideal man to cut through the noise and nonsense. I was wrong. The noise has beaten him; the nonsense always wins; any accumulated credit from last season has long since evaporated.

Of course it’s not Carrick’s fault that United haven’t signed a new defender in almost two years, that Bruno Fernandes is so clearly playing through discomfort, that Joshua Zirkzee is still somehow hanging around the place, that the ownership decided to embark on a purge of season-ticket holders in the summer, generating a huge wave of ill-feeling and apathy that can be felt at Old Trafford on matchdays: an entire fanbase trying to work out whether they actually still care about this.

But accountability is for the little people, and far easier to replace a coach on a modest 20-month contract than to oust Ineos or the Glazers, to replace Wilcox or Omar Berrada, even if we now know they partly built their reputations on the success of a club that has been found to have serially cheated. Bumper returns for the owners, price hikes for the fans, corners cut on the pitch; this is the story of the modern Manchester United, which by no coincidence is also the story of modern football.

Manchester United reportedly view Aston Villa manager Unai Emery as a dream target to replace the struggling Michael Carrick.

The Spanish tactician has done a superb job during his time at Villa Park, and has also impressed at other clubs throughout his career.

Emery is notably the holder of one hugely impressive record, having won the Europa League five times in his career – more than any other manager ever.

A three-time winner with Sevilla, Emery also won it with Villarreal, and then with Aston Villa last season, while he was also a runner-up in the final during his time in charge at Arsenal.

According to iNews, he’s now viewed as a strong candidate for the Man Utd job, and it’s certainly easy to see why with his credentials and experience.

The tricky thing with Emery is that his success has come at clubs with an obvious ceiling – the fact that he’s conquered the Europa League so convincingly whilst never quite hacking it at a club with bigger expectations is telling.

The 54-year-old’s only league title win came as Paris Saint-Germain manager, when dominating Ligue 1 is pretty much a given.

Even if United are not the force they used to be, he would be expected to get them back to challenging for major honours again – finishing in the top four and winning competitions like the Europa League wouldn’t be enough.

Emery notably struggled in similar circumstances at Arsenal when they were trying to rebuild after the departure of Arsene Wenger, and it never quite looked the right fit.

Villa seems just right for him and while he deserves respect for the job he’s done there, it doesn’t necessarily qualify for him for the huge challenge that would be waiting for him at Old Trafford.

#83. Manchester United teammates widely share Martínez view that Wilcox is failing

Defender challenged by at least one player

Later insists on social media: ‘I believe in teammates’

Lisandro Martínez’s view that Jason Wilcox is failing as Manchester United’s director of football reflects wider dressing-room frustration over recruitment and squad depth, although the outburst caused at least one teammate to challenge the defender to identify who he believes is not good enough for the side.

Martínez may face internal disciplinary action over extensive criticism of the hierarchy made in two broadcast interviews after Tuesday’s 2-0 Champions League defeat at Atlético Madrid. The players had Wednesday off so any action would not be taken before their return to training on Thursday.

Martínez directed his anger at Wilcox and what he referred to as “the top” of the club, saying not enough players had been signed and questioning the scale of investment, and that “more ambition” was needed. He said: “We don’t have the best squad at the moment compared with other top teams.”

Martínez’s comments prompted a challenge from at least one teammate before the flight back to Manchester and caused a heightening of tension. The Argentinian did not appear to explicitly call out his teammates, saying merely that “as players we have to do more”, and claimed that last season the squad had “created the base to build an amazing team”.

Although the sway of dressing-room opinion is with Martínez, his remarks are also seen as the centre-back considering his future, because he is out of contract next summer.

Defeat at Atlético left United with two wins this season, having taken six points from their first six Premier League matches, the club’s poorest league start for 40 years. The run of form has put the head coach Michael Carrick under pressure, but Martínez took aim at Wilcox and the rest of the hierarchy. “Tonight it is easy to say the coach, easy to say the players, but also we have to see other areas,” he said, pointing upwards.

“I expect more [investment], 100%. I expect more from our technical director [Jason Wilcox]. I have to be honest, as players we have to do more, but it is also important when you make a strong squad to be ready for this kind of night.”

Wilcox, the executive in charge of transfers, oversaw £155m of spending in the last window on Youri Tielemans, Andrey Santos and Carlos Baleba. The decision to recruit three midfielders while not strengthening other areas has been met with puzzlement among the squad, particularly as Wilcox was looking for a left-back, with Newcastle’s Lewis Hall the prime target.Luke Shaw is United’s only senior player in that position and his 12-year career at the club has been plagued by injury. The 31-year-old missed the defeat by Atlético and owing to a muscular problem. The lack of depth at left-back reflects the prevailing view among players that Carrick has been left without the resources to challenge. United were eliminated from the Carabao Cup by Brighton after making eight changes.

Although Carrick led United to third last season, this came after he took over as caretaker in mid-January with only 17 league games to play with the team out of all the other competitions.

Kick off your evenings with the Guardian's take on the world of football

Martínez is understood to favour a move to an elite European club outside the Premier League.

On Wednesday night, Martínez released a statement on social media in an attempt to clarify his comments, writing: “I know that, first and foremost, it’s us, the players, who have to deliver, and I put myself at the front of that responsibility.”

He added: “I believe in my teammates, our manager and everyone working to help this club move forward.” Yet he still maintained his post-match comments “reflect how I feel and how much I want to see this club competing for the biggest honours”.

#84. Englands TV-Experten kontern die harsche Trainerkritik des United-Kapitäns

Manchester United ist erneut in der Krise. Nach der Blamage in der Champions League beim FC Como kritisierte Kapitän Bruno Fernandes den Trainer. Das sehen legendäre Fußball-Experten deutlich anders.

Klicken Sie hier, um sich Artikel von WELT in der Google-Suche bevorzugt anzeigen zu lassen.

Nach der Blamage von Como kritisiert Manchester Uniteds Kapitän Bruno Fernandes die Taktik des Trainers – und löst damit deutliche Reaktionen aus. „Dass Bruno Fernandes so etwas tut, ist wirklich eine Schande“, schimpfte Ex-Nationalspieler Jamie Carragher. Der Portugiese habe eine „furchtbare Einstellung“ und sei nicht das, „was ein Kapitän von Manchester United sein sollte“.

Nach dem 1:4 beim italienischen Emporkömmling sprach Fernandes die seiner Meinung nach taktischen Verfehlungen von Trainer Michael Carrick an. „Es war die Entscheidung des Trainerteams, nicht Mann gegen Mann zu pressen und dahinter etwas Raum zu lassen“, sagte der 32-Jährige: „Welches System und welche Taktik man auch immer spielt – wenn du nicht läufst, dann wirst du nicht gewinnen.“

Der starke Nico Paz (2. Minute/57.), Anastasios Douvikas (49.) und Jacobo Ramon (76.) hatten die Tore für die Italiener erzielt, bei denen der ehemalige Bundesliga-Profi Marc Oliver Kempf durchspielte. Dem Ex-Münchner Joshua Zirkzee (90.+1) gelang kurz vor Schluss der Ehrentreffer für die Engländer.

Der Druck auf Trainer Michael Carrick wird vor dem nächsten Premier-League-Spiel am Sonntag gegen Bournemouth damit größer. „Ich übernehme 100 Prozent Verantwortung für das gesamte Team und die Leistung – aber damit hört’s auf“, sagte der 45-Jährige bei TNT Sports. „Als langjähriger Fan dieses Fußballvereins muss ich sagen, dass es für mich inakzeptabel ist, eine solche Leistung mitanzusehen.“

Das große Thema aber ist nun vorerst die Schelte des eigenen Kapitäns, den Carrick in der 67. Minute ausgewechselt hatte. Auch ein weiterer TV-Experte sprang dem Trainer zur Seite. „Wenn du Kapitän von Manchester United bist, kannst du nicht herauskommen und die Taktik infrage stellen“, sagte Ex-Nationalspieler Joe Cole im Fernsehen.

Der frühere United-Spieler Carrick hatte den Klub in der vergangenen Saison zurück in die Champions League geführt und war vom Interims- zum Cheftrainer aufgestiegen. In dieser Spielzeit läuft es jedoch bislang nicht gut. In der Königsklasse verbucht United einen Sieg aus drei Spielen. In der Premier League liegt man mit nur einem Sieg aus sieben Spielen auf Platz 13.

#85. Martínez available to face Leeds after Carrick accepts apology

Michael Carrick says Lisandro Martínez’s apology after criticising Jason Wilcox is enough for Manchester United to move on, with the defender available for Sunday’s trip to Leeds. The United head coach made clear that the club is not revisiting the dispute before a fixture arriving amid mounting pressure over results.

Martínez criticised United’s director of football after the 2-0 Champions League defeat at Atlético Madrid on Tuesday, arguing that the squad needed more investment. In a statement on Thursday, he apologised for expressing his frustration at a time and place that may not have been appropriate.

The apology addressed the circumstances of his comments, but did not name Wilcox or withdraw the underlying call for a stronger squad. Carrick said the acknowledgment was sufficient and that the club had moved on, according to the Guardian’s report of his pre-match comments.

Carrick also confirmed that Martínez would be available for the visit to Leeds. The episode had prompted questions about possible discipline, but the manager’s response makes clear that he does not expect the matter to remain a live issue within the group.

United’s disciplinary questions after Martínez’s comments

The timing leaves Carrick with little room for distraction. United had collected six points from their first six Premier League games, had been eliminated from the Carabao Cup by Brighton the previous month, and had recorded one win and one defeat in the Champions League, according to the Guardian’s 16 October report.

Luke Shaw and Kobbie Mainoo were due to face fitness tests; Carrick gave no outcome in the report. Martínez’s criticism followed the defeat in Madrid and focused on squad investment, bringing the issue of United’s depth into sharper view alongside their results.

Carrick said he understood the emotion behind Martínez’s outburst, while stressing that the intensity and passion he wants from players must be channelled constructively. He pointed to United’s response after conceding inside the first minute against Atlético as evidence of belief and togetherness.

Wayne Rooney had warned that Martínez’s remarks could damage the dressing room. Carrick rejected the idea that outside criticism reflected the squad’s mood, saying the players were in a better place on Friday than he had seen them for some time; that remains his assessment, rather than proof that every concern has disappeared.

United’s next test comes at Elland Road on Sunday, with Shaw and Mainoo’s fitness checks the outstanding team-news details before kick-off.

Chelsea and Manchester United could both be readying January transfer window moves for the next big thing in Portuguese football, as first reported here by the Daily Briefing.

The name on everyone’s lips at the moment is Sporting Lisbon winger Flavio Goncalves, who has been scouted by a number of Premier League clubs while his current employers desperately look to tie him down to a new contract.

The 19-year-old, who can play on either flank or as a central attacking midfield player, is current under contract until 2030, and it’s understood that he is currently available for a release clause fee of just €60m.

Amid growing interest from Man United, Chelsea, and others, Sporting are keen to get Goncalves to agree a new contract with a release clause closer to €100m, but they’re also braced for offers this January.

There has not been concrete progress on a new contract just yet, so it might be that the youngster and his representatives are waiting to see just how much his suitors push for him this January before deciding.

“Sporting know they have a huge talent on their hands, but they hope to get him to agree to a new deal so they can sell him for more money later. There’s every expectation that he could be a €100m player, so they won’t want to lose him on the cheap earlier than they need to.”

The Blues have a good relationship with Sporting after raiding them for Geovany Quenda in recent times, and they’ll be confident of snapping up another of their best talents.

Scouts from Chelsea and United, as well as unnamed teams from La Liga and Serie A have all been wowed by Goncalves’ “explosive acceleration, one‑on‑one dribbling ability, agility in tight spaces, and direct goal contribution”, according to one source.

All the ingredients are there for this young wide-man to have a fantastic career, so it will be intriguing to see where he ends up for the next stage of his development.

#88. Debakel im Como – Manchester Uniteds Krise wird immer schlimmer

Hauptnavigation: Nutzen Sie die Tabulatortaste, um durch die Menüpunkte zu navigieren. Öffnen Sie Untermenüs mit der Leertaste. Schließen Sie Untermenüs mit der Escape-Taste.

Hauptnavigation: Nutzen Sie die Tabulatortaste, um durch die Menüpunkte zu navigieren. Öffnen Sie Untermenüs mit der Leertaste.

Manchester United blamiert sich in Como und kassiert die nächste Niederlage in der Champions League. Der Druck auf Trainer Michael Carrick wird immer größer, da es auch in der Premier League katastrophal läuft.

Klicken Sie hier, um sich Artikel von WELT in der Google-Suche bevorzugt anzeigen zu lassen.

Die sportliche Krise von Manchester United spitzt sich immer weiter zu. Die zuvor schon seit sechs Pflichtspielen sieglosen Red Devils verloren nach einer desolaten Leistung in der Champions League mit 1:4 (0:1) bei Como 1907.

Der starke Nico Paz (2. Minute/57.), Anastasios Douvikas (49.) und Jacobo Ramon (76.) erzielten die Tore für die Italiener, bei denen der ehemalige Bundesliga-Profi Marc Oliver Kempf durchspielte. Dem Ex-Münchner Joshua Zirkzee (90.+1) gelang kurz vor Schluss noch der Ehrentreffer für die Engländer, die Como in allen Belangen unterlegen waren.

Wegen sintflutartiger Regenfälle war der Anpfiff der Partie in der Lombardei gefährdet, doch dann wurde das Spiel auf dem tiefen Rasen pünktlich angepfiffen. Como dominierte ab der ersten Minute, erzielte vor der Pause aber nur einen Treffer. Manchester gab in der ersten Halbzeit nicht einen Torschuss ab.

Nach der Pause sorgte das Team von Trainer Cesc Fabregas für klare Verhältnisse und zog auf 4:0 davon. Für Manchester Uniteds Trainer Michael Carrick wird der Druck immer größer. Das Team hat in der Champions League nur eines von drei Spielen gewonnen, und in der Premier League sieht es noch düsterer aus. Dort liegt United nur zwei Punkte von einem Abstiegsrang entfernt – in sieben Spielen holte die Mannschaft nur einen Sieg.

„Ich übernehme 100 Prozent Verantwortung für das gesamte Team und die Leistung – aber damit hört's auf“, sagte Carrick bei TNT Sports. „Als langjähriger Fan dieses Fußballvereins muss ich sagen, dass es für mich inakzeptabel ist, eine solche Leistung mitanzusehen.“ Auf die Frage, ob er noch die Rückendeckung seiner Vorgesetzten spüre, sagte der Ex-Profi: „Es geht nicht um mich. Es geht darum, dieses Spiel zu verdauen.“

Leroy Sané und Ilkay Gündogan feierten mit Galatasaray Istanbul den ersten Saisonsieg in der Champions League. Bei OSC Lille gewann der amtierende türkische Meister mit 4:2 (2:2).

Sané wurde in der 76. Minute eingewechselt, der ehemalige Kapitän der deutschen Nationalmannschaft Gündogan kam neun Minuten später von der Bank. Yunus Akgün, Aleksey Batrakov, Lucas Torreira und Victor Osimhen erzielten die Tore für Galatasaray, dessen nächster Gegner am 3. November der VfB Stuttgart ist.

#89. ‘I expect more’: the key questions from Martínez’s Manchester United broadside | Jamie Jackson

From Jason Wilcox’s role to the views of the rest of the squad, we tackle the major talking points at Old Trafford after the defender’s outburst

After Manchester United’s 2-0 loss at Atlético Madrid, Lisandro Martínez teed off at the director of football, Jason Wilcox, and the rest of the executive. “We don’t have the best squad at the moment compared with other top teams,” said the defender. “I expect more [investment], 100%. I expect more from our technical director [Jason Wilcox] … Really poor [the Atlético defeat] – I have to control myself a lot but something is a reflection of the mirror of how our club is at the moment.” Here we consider the key questions emerging from Martínez’s explosive remarks.

Yes is the short answer. The Guardian has been told the decision to target three midfielders in Andrey Santos, Youri Tielemans and Carlos Baleba caused bemusement in the squad that no other areas were strengthened in the summer. Carrick led United to third last term after the side only had 17 league games remaining when he took over in January, having been eliminated from the other competitions. The need for defensive reinforcements is particularly acute because the back four of Diogo Dalot, Martínez, Harry Maguire and Luke Shaw has been together since 2022. Martínez is out of contract next summer and it is thought the 28-year-old may not be offered a new deal or have his one-year option triggered by the club, so teammates are conscious his outburst also came because he expects to leave.

As United’s director of football the 55-year-old wields near-unilateral power on football decisions, with only Sir Jim Ratcliffe, who controls sporting policy, above him. If the buck stops with the executive, the most recent transfer window was Wilcox’s responsibility. This was the latest in a line of missteps on his United record. One concerns when Dan Ashworth (Wilcox’s predecessor) was on gardening leave from Newcastle in the first half of 2024, Wilcox interviewed potential replacements for Erik ten Hag before, oddly, deeming that the head coach should stay. The Dutchman signed a fresh contract but was sacked that autumn. Another involves Wilcox’s major falling out with the man he hired as the Dutchman’s successor, Ruben Amorim, after which the Portuguese said the director of football had told him he should change his favoured 3-4-3 shape. “I came here to be the manager of Manchester United – not to be the coach of Manchester United,” said Amorim, after a 1-1 draw at Leeds on 4 January (his job title was head coach). He was sacked the next day. Wilcox worked with Omar Berrada, United’s chief executive, at Manchester City, where Berrada rose to become chief football operations officer, and Wilcox became academy director. Wilcox moved to Southampton and became director of football from summer 2023 until April 2024, when he joined United, having been identified by Berrada, so there is scrutiny of his experience at elite level.

The 45-year-old’s ability to prosper is related to the quality of Wilcox’s work, primarily in the market, so he cannot be solely responsible for a dire six points from six league games and two victories in all competitions all season. Yet by definition his role as head coach demands Carrick do the best with his resources, and that progress is made on the training ground. On matchdays there is scant excuse for United’s disjointedness, inferior running stats and inability, twice, to take advantage against 10 men: the 1-0 loss to City and Saturday’s 1-1 draw with Tottenham. How is Carrick viewed by his players? According to Santos they back him. “We believe in the manager, we believe in the whole staff, we believe in [ourselves as] players,” he said after the Atlético game. “We are in a bad moment but we have to work to change this result.”.

For further evidence see Amad Diallo’s Snapchat post after the defeat by Atlético: “Fucking disgrace; I have no words.” So United have a World Cup-winner, Martínez, aiming shots at Wilcox and by implication Ratcliffe and Berrada, and a fellow senior player, Diallo, cursing but in ambiguous fashion, adding to the sense of meltdown at the club because it is unclear whether the “disgrace” meant the performance, Martínez’s diatribe, the squad his teammate referred to as not good enough, or something else. All of this is mirrored on the pitch, where United’s 671.4km run in the Premier League is the fifth-lowest (Arsenal’s 716.2 is the highest), and their 581 sprints the fourth-lowest (Brentford’s 765 the most).

Kick off your evenings with the Guardian's take on the world of football

In mitigation Wilcox can point to the six American siblings, who remain majority owners and whose family has placed an eye-watering £1.1bn-plus debt on the club that severely restricts the transfer budget. Wilcox oversaw a £155m summer outlay and a net spend of about £100m in a summer when City’s corresponding figures were £457.5m and about £175m. City buying players such as Enzo Fernández (for a British record-equalling £125m), Elliot Anderson (£115m), Ayyoub Bouaddi (£81m) and Iliman Ndiaye (£65m) showed their greater financial muscle, helped by being much better sellers of talent. How they have reached this point has, though, been thrown into sharp focus by last month’s verdict that they have serially cheated.

Any straw should be clutched at so Carrick and his staff can view the first 97 minutes of Baleba’s United career as a rosy augury. After entering on 59 minutes against Spurs, a cute swivel and deft chip to Bruno Fernandes was a moment as impressive as the rest of his debut, and at Atlético the 22-year-old hunted down opponents and crunched into tackles, suggesting his game contains energy, muscularity and skill. It’s early days but perhaps if Baleba had been fit to start the campaign (he was recovering from an ankle problem) he might have affected one or more of the defeat at Hull, the 2-2 draw at Everton when Ainsley Maitland-Niles equalised late, the 1-0 loss to City when Phil Foden was sent off after 23 minutes or the Carabao Cup elimination by Brighton.

#90. Second Man Utd star blasts Michael Carrick tactics after Bruno Fernandes interview

Youri Tielemans appeared to join Bruno Fernandes in questioning Michael Carrick’s tactics following Manchester United’s humiliating Champions League defeat to Como.

The Serie A side, competing in Europe’s premier competition for the first time in their history this season, outclassed United on Wednesday night, cruising to a 4-1 victory.

United are now without a win in their last seven with club captain Fernandes lashing out in his post-match interview with TNT Sports, ruthlessly throwing his manager under the bus with comments that leave the club in more chaos.

‘It’s a decision from the staff to not press man to man and sometimes leave space behind and try to get the ball, but sometimes when you give too much the ball to these teams they get confidence,’ Fernandes said.

Asked directly whether Carrick got his tactics wrong, Fernandes added: ‘That’s on the manager and staff to see it, not me. Sometimes you have to be more brave under pressure, take the risk of pressing high and making them uncomfortable.’

Tielemans also spoke to the broadcaster after the game and attempted to find an explanation for the woeful performance and result.

While the Belgium international suggested a lack of effort from some players was an alarming issue, he also pointed out tactical problems as reason for the club’s latest disaster.

‘It’s a combination of everything: tactical, technical, effort,’ Tielemans said when asked what has specifically gone wrong at United.

‘It’s everything. You can be good tactically but if the effort isn’t there, or the other way round, you have to be good at everything, but tonight we were poor at everything.

‘It’s the same message as we’ve had the last few weeks. We can be here and talk about it, but we have to show it on the pitch.

‘We can only apologise to the fans who travelled, the fans who watched us from home and support us. They’re disappointed and we’re disappointed.

‘The only thing we can do is try to show something on the pitch, which we haven’t done over the last few weeks.’

Defeat in Como was United’s worst result in Europe since 1996 having already made their worst start to a Premier League season in 40 years.

Former Bayern Munich and Germany manager Julian Nagelsmann is reportedly a leading candidate for the Manchester United job and would be keen to take over at Old Trafford.

The 39-year-old has been out of work since Germany’s disappointing World Cup exit in the summer, though he’s long been very highly regarded in the game.

Nagelsmann really made a name for himself at Hoffenheim when he was one of the youngest head coaches in Europe, and he’s continued to impress in a number of big jobs since then.

Things didn’t quite go to plan for him with the German national team, but it seems that hasn’t stopped him being Man Utd’s top target amid growing uncertainty over Michael Carrick’s future, according to the Telegraph.

Nagelsmann has surely got what it takes to manage a top Premier League club, but it would be his first spell in English football, and perhaps at one of the most challenging teams right now.

It’s been a difficult period at Old Trafford, and the pressure on Nagelsmann would be huge straight away, while he wouldn’t have the benefits that come with having a full pre-season and transfer window to work with.

The German tactician may well prove to be an upgrade on Carrick due to his superior experience at the highest level, but things aren’t always that simple.

MUFC have gone through a number of different and varied styles of managers since Sir Alex Ferguson’s retirement in 2013, and none have really worked.

#92. Jason Wilcox: ‘best eyes’ at Manchester United or promoted too far and too fast?

Director of football is tarnished by recruitment record and Amorim stint but has received glowing review from above

Lisandro Martínez is not naive. At 28, he has experienced plenty in football and knows the causes of success and failure. He has a World Cup winner’s medal, whereas Jason Wilcox has overseen the latest period of mediocrity at Manchester United, during which the focus has fallen more on the team’s performances than the wider reasons behind it.

Martínez was not solely speaking for himself when, after United’s defeat at Atlético Madrid, he strongly criticised Wilcox. His views are shared by others at United frustrated by the output from above and who “expect” more from a director of football. Wilcox knows he is under pressure because the inadequate results cannot all be pinned on Michael Carrick, who is working with a squad unable to cope with the rigours of challenging at the top of the Premier League and facing Europe’s elite.

Clubs live and die by their recruitment, and United sitting 13th in the Premier League after one win from six matches does not suggest the squad is in rude health. A sporting director, like a referee, goes unnoticed when performing well but it has reached the point at United where players can take no more.

Three years at Ajax taught Martínez about football structure. The Dutch clubs mix homegrown players with imports from around the world, many of whom are sold on for large profits. Wilcox would struggle to make the case that any of his recruits, with the possible exception of Senne Lammens, have markedly increased in value. There are many sporting directors more adept at finding undervalued talent. Wilcox was offered the chance to sign Elliot Anderson from Nottingham Forest a year before the midfielder signed for Manchester City, but decided against moving for a player who continued to grow in value and was eventually out of United’s price range. If Sir Jim Ratcliffe is so eager to run United as a business, questioning why the outlay on players leads to so little return would be a sensible starting point.

Wilcox arrived at Old Trafford in April 2024 after less than a year as the director of football in the Championship with Southampton, who gave him his first executive role in senior football. Before that, the former Blackburn and Leeds winger had worked in youth development, eventually becoming Manchester City’s academy director, and was well respected, but whether this was sufficient grounding for leading United’s football operation was debatable. Wilcox oversaw at City the emergence of a glut of talent, including Phil Foden, Cole Palmer and Morgan Rogers. Some of those who have dealt with him feel a senior academy position is where his skills lie.

A key reason why he was brought back to his native north-west by United was Omar Berrada’s appointment as chief executive. The pair worked together at City and there was a high degree of mutual respect, supported by the – now tainted – achievements of the club. Wilcox was given the title technical director and was supposed to work under the experienced sporting director Dan Ashworth, who held similar roles at Brighton and Newcastle but lasted only five months under Ratcliffe, earning Wilcox a rapid promotion.

Some United staff in place when Wilcox arrived grew frustrated at the number of former City Football Group appointments being made. United had got plenty wrong over the years but continued to thrive in certain aspects, so trying to turn the club into a copy of one of their fiercest rivals grated.

Wilcox was influential in the recruitment of Ruben Amorim, who had a specific way of playing but not the players to carry out his demands. The experiment failed, accelerated by a breakdown in relationship between the head coach and Wilcox, who did not see eye-to-eye on tactics, among other things.

When it comes to players there is increasingly a focus on Premier League experience with Wilcox; this summer Youri Tielemans, Andrey Santos and Carlos Baleba arrived from top-flight English clubs for a combined £155m. The midfield did require an overhaul, with Casemiro leaving and Manuel Ugarte, currently injured, never reaching the requisite level. That outlay, however, meant there was no money for a left-back or backup striker, leaving fans eager for an explanation. In the defeat at Atlético, Carrick played the 20-year-old centre-back Ayden Heaven out of position to cover Luke Shaw’s latest absence, summing up the shallowness of the squad.

Kick off your evenings with the Guardian's take on the world of football

Ratcliffe, the minority owner, is on record as saying he has a lot of time for Wilcox, “When I listen to Jason speak, I get what he’s saying,” he told the Times in March 2025. “He’s a warm character, he’s got a sense of humour, he knows what he’s talking about.” At matches, the pair can be often found sitting next to one another in discussion. Ratcliffe has displayed a ruthless business approach since investing in United, but removing Wilcox would be one of the more difficult personal decisions. Their relationship is strong and making a change would be bold. With finances likely to be tight in January, the planning at this stage is for next summer and who is best to navigate it.

If results continue to be substandard, Ratcliffe and Berrada will be forced to consider their options. Wilcox’s bond with the pair is important but Carrick has a lot more credit in the bank with a fanbase that wields power and has strong reservations over the director of football. Removing Wilcox would be the more widely popular decision but also an acceptance of failure from the top brass. Ratcliffe once said Wilcox had the “best eyes in the club” but he cannot be blinded by the truth.

#93. Four wins later, Eva Olid explains United Women’s Chelsea reset

Eva Olid says candid conversations after Manchester United Women’s 5-0 defeat to Chelsea helped spark a change in the team, with Charlton Athletic next up at The Valley on Sunday, 18 October.

The United head coach identified those discussions as the point when players and staff began to understand each other better. The Peoples Person reported her comments from a pre-match press conference published on 17 October, ahead of the Reds’ return from the international break.

The improvement was reflected in the results that followed, including a third consecutive league win against Durham.

Olid acknowledged that taking charge midway through pre-season, amid coaching changes, made for a difficult start. United lost their first two listed matches, going down 2-1 to London City Lionesses and then 5-0 at home to Chelsea.

A 1-1 draw away to Arsenal followed, before four consecutive victories: 10-1 against Sheffield United in the WSL Cup, 2-1 against West Ham, 5-1 against Durham in the cup and 1-0 against Liverpool. That stretch includes three successive league wins, though the cup scorelines offer a reminder that not every result came against WSL opposition.

Olid said the international break was not simply time away from the pitch. She stayed in Manchester and used it to reorganise staff responsibilities and working methods, describing the pause as an opportunity to get things in place and begin again with renewed energy.

🔴 Follow Stretty News on Google as a preferred source and be first with every United update.

“After the Chelsea game, we had those honest conversations, and I think you could see the change against Arsenal and in the following matches. I feel that we are now more connected. That doesn’t mean we will always have good days, but we have a better understanding of each other,” Olid said, as reported by The Peoples Person.

That qualification matters. A response to a heavy defeat can lift a side in the short term, but Olid’s explanation is about more than momentum: she believes the players have a clearer understanding of one another. The sequence of results backs up the timing of the change she describes, without proving on its own that the cohesion will hold through every test.

Her comments on the coaching group point to another part of the reset. Olid said she values staff with different profiles and personalities, and described José Herrera as someone with a similar mentality and strong tactical knowledge. Olid’s attacking approach is another element she is working to establish, but the source does not suggest that a single tactical adjustment explains the turnaround.

The opening losses, in her account, became a learning experience for the team as well as for her. There is still little basis for firm conclusions about availability: the players had only returned to Carrington on Friday, and Olid said she did not yet know who had recovered or whether there were fresh injury concerns.

She also said captain Maya Le Tissier returned from international duty in a positive mood after scoring her first international goal. Olid had watched her games and spoke with her about enjoying the attacking side of her role, while making clear she preferred Le Tissier in her usual position.

Olid expects Charlton to return from the break with energy and to see the fixture as a chance to pursue their first win. She warned that United must be ready for a difficult match, adding that the sides probably have different styles of play.

The challenge now is to take the understanding Olid describes into a fresh block of games. She wants United to carry the positive energy from their recent results into Sunday’s contest, where Charlton will test whether the response to Chelsea has become something more durable.

#94. Lisandro Martínez hits out at Manchester United hierarchy: ‘I expect more’

2-0 defeat to Atlético Madrid ‘reflects how our club is’

Argentinian says club has not invested enough in players

Lisandro Martínez has hit out at the Manchester United hierarchy, blaming the lack of investment as their dismal start to the season continued. Goals from Pablo Barrios and Ademola Lookman pushed United to a 2-0 defeat at Atlético Madrid, and the Argentina defender described that as a “mirror” of the way the club are. “Tonight it is easy to say the coach, easy to say the players but also we have to see other areas,” he said, pointing upwards.

Asked for his appraisal of the night, Martínez said: “Really poor, really poor. I have to control myself a lot but sometimes is a reflection of the mirror of how is our club at the moment.

“It is annoying, disappointing coming here and I am not seeing my team trying to get an amazing result here.

“I expect more [investment], 100%. I expect more from our technical director [Jason Wilcox]. I have to be honest, as players we have to do more but it is also important when you make a strong squad to be ready for this kind of night. Last season we did everything, we created the base to create an amazing team.”

United won 12 of 17 games under Michael Carrick last season but during the summer they made only three major signings – Carlos Baleba, Andrey Santos and Youri Tielemans – plus the back-up goalkeeper Karl Darlow on a free transfer from Leeds, and have won only twice in all competitions so far this term. They have suffered the worst start to a league campaign in 40 years.

Asked if the manager had been backed sufficiently, Martínez replied: “I don’t think so. He does his best. Every season is different. You can’t stay with what you did last season. The teams knows how you play, they know every detail, if you don’t change next season [it] is a big problem.

“You can’t keep changing, changing, changing the manager. What about other areas?” Martínez added, looking up. “I can’t say more.”

When Martínez’s words were put to Carrick, he said: “I haven’t seen the interview. I have half-heard about it. I think it’s a little bit the emotions of the game [talking]. I have to find out a bit more about it to be honest to decide what and if anything is going to be done. I can’t comment further.”

United’s manager also insisted that his side had “four almost one vs ones” and that he did not understand Leny Yoro’s red card on a night when they could have gone back to Manchester with a different result. “It is easy to look and think there is something deeply wrong, I understand that, but the players have been great,” he said. “There’s willing, and [they are] disappointed that they haven’t come out of here with something. We think we could have, should have.”

#95. Manchester verliert in Como: „Schande“: Wirbel um United-Kapitän nach Kritik an Trainer

Nach dem 1:4 von Manchester United gegen Como kritisiert Bruno Fernandes die Taktik von Trainer Michael Carrick. Was dem Portugiesen nun von Ex-Profis vorgeworfen wird.

Nach der Blamage von Como kritisiert Manchester Uniteds Kapitän Bruno Fernandes die Taktik des Trainers - und löst damit deutliche Reaktionen aus. „Dass Bruno Fernandes so etwas tut, ist wirklich eine Schande“, schimpfte Ex-Nationalspieler Jamie Carragher. Der Portugiese habe eine „furchtbare Einstellung“ und sei nicht das, „was ein Kapitän von Manchester United sein sollte“.

Nach dem 1:4 beim italienischen Emporkömmling sprach Fernandes die seiner Meinung nach taktischen Verfehlungen von Trainer Michael Carrick an. „Es war die Entscheidung des Trainer-Teams, nicht Mann gegen Mann zu pressen und dahinter etwas Raum zu lassen“, sagte der 32-Jährige. „Welches System und welche Taktik man auch immer spielt - wenn du nicht läufst, dann wirst du nicht gewinnen.“

Fernandes selbst war in der 67. Minute ausgewechselt worden. Seine anschließende Schelte kritisierte auch Ex-Nationalspieler Joe Cole. „Wenn du Kapitän von Manchester United bist, kannst du nicht herauskommen und die Taktik infrage stellen“, sagte der TV-Experte.

#96. Limited training keeps Mazraoui’s Leeds role uncertain

Michael Carrick says Noussair Mazraoui could return to Manchester United’s matchday group for Sunday’s trip to Leeds after resuming some training, offering the Reds another defensive option without confirming the full-back will start.

The update, reported by DevilPage and Tribuna, follows Mazraoui’s absence from United’s 1-1 draw with Tottenham and 2-0 Champions League defeat to Atletico Madrid. He returned from Morocco duty with an injury, but Carrick indicated that the defender had begun training again and might be involved at Elland Road.

Leeds host United on Sunday, 18 October, with kick-off scheduled for 15:00. Carrick’s update leaves Mazraoui’s availability short of certainty: he has trained a little, but the reports do not confirm his selection or suggest he is ready to start.

Patrick Dorgu, Benjamin Sesko and Manuel Ugarte are reported to miss the fixture, while Luke Shaw and Kobbie Mainoo are doubts. Carrick has confirmed that Lisandro Martinez will be in the matchday squad, giving United a clearer picture of some defensive options even as decisions remain on other players.

The club’s wider injury situation has shaped preparations for the Leeds match, with Mazraoui’s possible return one part of a broader availability check. The prospect of having him back with the group matters, but it should not be mistaken for confirmation that he will feature on the pitch.

🔴 Follow Stretty News on Google as a preferred source and be first with every United update.

Manchester United’s recent injury picture

In the source report, Carrick described United as having a somewhat stronger squad and said he hoped Mazraoui would be involved. The Moroccan’s return would add another option in defence after he missed two matches, but the manager’s update points to a possible squad role rather than a settled place in the starting XI.

Matthijs de Ligt is training at full workload and has recently returned to the substitutes’ bench after a prolonged back injury. Carrick’s update places him closer to a return, but does not establish when he will play again or whether he is ready to start.

The manager said De Ligt still needs more work and patience before he is back to full match condition. For the Leeds trip, the immediate selection question is whether Mazraoui’s limited training is enough for a place in the group; De Ligt’s progress remains a separate step in United’s defensive recovery.

Eddie Howe would reportedly be ready to become Manchester United manager if the club decides to end Michael Carrick’s increasingly difficult spell in charge.

United’s embarrassing 4-1 Champions League defeat to Como has intensified the pressure on Carrick, with the team now without a victory in seven consecutive matches.

Attention is consequently beginning to turn towards possible replacements, and Howe has emerged as one of the strongest candidates.

According to journalist Alan Nixon on Patreon, as relayed by TEAMtalk, Howe is currently regarded as the “favourite” for the Man United position and “would take it” if the opportunity was offered.

The 48-year-old has been out of management since leaving Newcastle United at the end of July following almost five years at St James’ Park.

Howe’s Newcastle spell ultimately ended after a disappointing 12th-place finish in 2025/26, but his overall record remains impressive. He took the club into the Champions League twice and delivered their first major domestic trophy in 70 years by winning the 2025 League Cup.

Fabian Hürzeler and Antonio Conte are also being considered by United, with both featuring prominently among the possible candidates.

Howe would not be the glamorous appointment some supporters might expect, but that may actually work in his favour.

Man United have repeatedly chased big managerial personalities or fashionable tactical projects without creating lasting stability.

Howe offers something different: proven Premier League experience, a clear pressing style and evidence that he can improve players over a sustained period.

His work at Newcastle showed he can take over a struggling side, improve standards quickly and eventually compete for major honours.

There are legitimate doubts. Newcastle’s final season under Howe was poor, and managing United brings a completely different level of scrutiny.

However, compared with another experimental appointment, Howe would represent a relatively safe and logical choice.

If Carrick cannot reverse United’s alarming decline quickly, the former Newcastle boss could become considerably more than just another name on the shortlist.

#98. Michael Carrick fires back at Bruno Fernandes comments and blasts players

Michael Carrick hit back at pointed comments from Bruno Fernandes and heavily criticised his players as he was left ‘really angry’ by Manchester United’s 4-1 defeat at Como.

Managed by former Arsenal and Chelsea star Cesc Fabregas, Como are playing in the Champions League for the first time in their history but looked very much at ease against one of the biggest clubs in Europe.

Goals from Nico Paz(2), Tasos Douvikas and Jacobo Ramon put the Italians 4-0 up, with only a late Joshua Zirkzee consolation giving the traveling fans something to cheer.

It has been a terrible start to the season for the Red Devils and the cracks that have already appeared are getting bigger, with captain Fernandes taking shots at Carrick and his coaching staff.

Sign up to our daily football newsletter, pick your team and get the latest news sent directly to your inbox every morning.

Speaking to TNT Sports after the game, the Portuguese said: ‘It’s a decision from the staff to not press man-to-man and sometimes leave a bit of space in behind and then try to get the ball in the first moment to wait for them, to give them the ball.

‘But sometimes when you give too much the ball to these teams, they get confident, they have so many bodies behind the ball that make you run a lot which makes people get frustrated.

‘And then, when we recover the ball, the legs and the brain are much more difficult to make it work.’

To view this video please enable JavaScript, and consider upgrading to a web browser that supports HTML5 video

On whether Carrick got things wrong tactically, Fernandes said: ‘That’s on the manager and staff to see it, not me. Sometimes you have to be more brave under pressure, take the risk of pressing high and making them uncomfortable.’

The Manchester United boss, who has won just one of seven Premier League games this campaign, hit back at the criticism, blasting the efforts of his players on Wednesday night.

Use AI to go deeper into the stories you care about – powered by Metro and trusted publications.

Carrick has largely defended his players so far during an underwhelming campaign, but said after the loss in Lombardy: ‘I’m bitterly disappointed, I’m frustrated, more than frustrated, I’m really angry.’

He responded to Fernandes’ criticism by hammering the effort of his players: ‘We actually half-pressed man-to-man the second half and then conceded a goal from that.

‘And there’s a little bit on whatever system you play, whatever tactics you play, if you don’t run, and you don’t run forward, and you don’t run back, you ain’t going to win.

‘We might win, we might lose, but the way we went about it tonight, I wouldn’t say everyone, but the way we went about it in some ways tonight wasn’t acceptable. And I just wanted to let the fans know that I feel for them, for sure.

‘It wasn’t just the goals, if I’m totally honest. It was the manner and the way we played at times, in the way we went about it, that was the difficult thing to watch for me.’

Asked if he felt his players were still playing for him, he said: ‘It’s not about playing for me, it’s about playing for this football club. It’s the responsibility that comes with being in the position; I’m responsible for the football club and the supporters.

#99. Roy Keane links Martínez row to Manchester United unity problem

Roy Keane has described Manchester United as chaotic and like a circus after Lisandro Martínez publicly criticised the club’s hierarchy, turning the defender’s outburst into a wider indictment of the Reds’ leadership and dressing-room unity.

Martínez’s comments followed United’s 2-0 Champions League defeat by Atlético Madrid on Tuesday 13 October. The defender challenged the club’s ambition and summer recruitment; Keane’s reaction, reported by The Irish Times on Thursday 15 October, was that the episode reflected problems across Old Trafford rather than a single player speaking out.

Martínez’s criticism of United’s hierarchy placed director of football Jason Wilcox and the board under scrutiny. Keane’s intervention has shifted the focus from what the centre-back said to the conditions that made such a public dispute possible.

Martínez met manager Michael Carrick and the club’s leadership group after a training session on Thursday 15 October. In a statement that evening, he apologised for the timing and setting of his comments, acknowledged that the interview had not helped and said he remained focused on winning together.

The Irish Times reported that United considered the matter closed and did not intend to announce disciplinary action publicly, while also reporting that Martínez had been fined. That distinction matters: the club may regard the incident as settled internally, but the public criticism has already put recruitment, decision-making and player relationships in the spotlight.

Former United defender Gary Neville argued that Martínez’s call for more signings was a direct criticism of team-mates, and questioned whether the defender had the standing to make such a challenge. Both Neville and Keane also questioned Martínez’s individual performances, even as they acknowledged concerns about the club’s condition.

🔴 Follow Stretty News on Google as a preferred source and be first with every United update.

Keane’s assessment was broader than a rebuke of Martínez. He said United were not together and described the atmosphere and leadership structure as chaotic from top to bottom, also criticising the process that led to Carrick becoming manager and arguing that other options had been available.

He questioned the character in the dressing room and did not regard Martínez as a senior United player. That makes the criticism two-sided: Keane sees a club with a unity problem, but also believes Martínez’s status and form weaken his case for publicly challenging others.

The Irish Times also reported that some frustrated team-mates were said to share elements of Martínez’s concerns. Keane, however, did not expect a major falling-out. The available picture is therefore not of a confirmed dressing-room split, but of overlapping dissatisfaction that has become unusually visible.

United’s squad depth and internal divisions are now part of the same conversation. A player’s public challenge to recruitment, followed by a sharp response from former senior figures, is a poor advertisement for communication inside a club trying to restore stability.

The dispute has landed during what the Irish Times described as United’s worst start to a league season since 1986. Martínez’s remarks came after a damaging European defeat and questioned whether the summer recruitment matched the club’s ambitions.

Neville also drew a distinction between Wilcox’s role and responsibility for the transfer budget, arguing that the owners set the financial limits. That leaves the criticism reaching beyond one executive: even if a football director is the most visible target, recruitment decisions ultimately involve the club’s wider leadership.

United can treat the disciplinary question as closed, but that does not resolve the issue Keane identified. Martínez has said he is focused on winning together; the next test is whether the squad can show that unity on the pitch, while the questions about recruitment and leadership remain unanswered.

double quotation markSo much for a reaction, a rebellion: instead, there was a kind of resignation, a sense that this is just their reality: a game that began badly, barely got better, and finished even worse. When you fall behind so early, don’t take your chances and go down to 10 men, the chances are you will lose. And that’s what United did. There were some decent moments but they won’t be enough. The red card was harsh and Michael Carrick will doubtless talk of small margins in post-match but it was not nearly enough. They remain on two wins all season. The poor start is becoming a poor season.

90+3 min: Another United chance that goes begging. Zirkzee deliberates when the ball comes his way via an error, and then Cunha shoots on goal. Oblak makes another save. Again, better execution required from United. Their finishing all season has been poor, scratchy.

90 min: Five minutes added on for United to suffer through. Atletico are happy to pass the ball around. This one is done, stick a fork in it, referee.

88 min: Le Normand replaces Simeone Jnr and is almost immediately booked. Things happen quickly in this game.

86 min: The ball is in Atletico’s half most of the time at this point but United look incapable of getting through. They had the chances, but did not take them. Each of them had a high tariff of difficulty but a good team takes them. United are not a good team at present.

85 min: Robin Le Normand, the medieval knight, is set to come on for Atletico.

83 min: On to Leeds for United; it does not get any easier for Carrick. Leeds gave Arsenal quite the game on Saturday and United’s physical capabilities are still in question.

81 min: Maguire’s arrival is followed by a corner and an awful lot of pushing in the box. The corner comes to nothing and Mason Mount has to chase back, and win a foul from his chaser.

80 min: The game lost for United, off go Heaven and Bruno, and on come Cunhaa and Maguire.

And that caps it off. David’s shot is saved, and it comes to Lookman, who slides it in. The two subs have won this game for Atletico, and lost it for United with the red card and that clinching goal.

78 min: Joshua Keeling speaks for a few when he says: “I’m sorry but that is an absolute disgrace of a decision. That is not even a foul, never mind a red card. Goodness me, what a joke.”

77 min: That makes it tougher for United. Just when they had put out the fires of Alvarez and Baena, that happens.

75 min: Some confusion there. It looked harsh, a coming together. But, say the officials, it counts as a denial of a goalscoring opportunity, and so lands the first red of Leny Yoro’s career.

73 min: Some anger when David goes down heavy under a tackle from Yoro. The ball spins to Lookman who has a shot saved. A foul is given to United but out comes the curly finger of VAR. And, so as a clear and obvious error has been committed, off goes Yoro.

71 min: Two more Atleti changes: Baena and Alvarez off, Lookman and Hjulmand on. The latter was long linked with United when Ruben Amorim was manager. Sporting friends.

70 min: Fernandes strikes the crossbar, and from a short corner, the ball exchanged with Amad and then drilled over Oblak’s head and back out.

68 min: Fernandes has gone into the position that Tielemans vacated. And he gets a a chance from that central position. United continue to push forward. Mbeumo has gone out right, as Zirkzee goes to the centre.

66 min: United make changes: Zirkzee, Mount and Amad on, Tielemans, Baleba and Rashford off. Baleba was the superior performer of the departed trio.

65 min: Baena dances and cavorts in the box, and then digs out a vicious shot. Lammens just about holds on to the ball.

64 min: Nice pass from Baleba, and Bruno hits a cross first time, aimed at Mbeumo but Romero cuts it off. Here come the United changes.

63 min: Llorente, such a warrior, steps in stop Tielemans. What changes can Carrick make? Fans of Boro tell us that was often slow to make them there. An extra spark would help though that bench looks short of anything remotely electric.

61 min: Atleti subs: Lee and Barrios off, and on come Jonathan David and Marc Pubill.

60 min: Feels like a game where both teams are waiting for the chance to counter. Though Baena carries a threat even when there’s heavy traffic and fires a shot that will not beat Lammens.

58 min: Ooof. Tielemans, at last, finds space, and his pass is a belter. Bruno waits for the bounce, it’s there to be hit, but it’s stabbed wide. Bruno Fernandes at his best buries thar.

56 min: Chris Greenhough gets in touch: “I don’t know what’s more deflating: United’s sluggish performances so far this year, or the fact that the Wilcoxes, Brailsfords, and Berradas will coast through the whole shambles, unquestioned and untarnished. In any other job, etc. etc...”

So does Tim Stappard: “Of course Carrick will lose his job. They all do. The question is whether Wilcox and Berrada do. He also definitely doesn’t have a clue what his best side and formation is.

55 min: So close for Atleti. Great ball from Barrios, and Alvarez scuffs his volley off the post. United strike it lucky once more. There’s real quality, and they will get punished if they keep switching off.

53 min: Santos is sat deepest of the United midfield, holding while Baleba steps forward. United are back in the groove they all too briefly found in the first half. Llorente eventually robs Tielemans who has struggled for impact in his more advanced position.

52 min: Romero steps out to rob Baleba, and then Simeone wins a foul from a Rashford challenge. The United player gets the first on-field booking of the night.

#103. He's finished: Man United star who lost 80% of his duels vs Como should have played his last game

Correspondent AUTHORITY Football journalist with experience at Reach PLC and PA Media; University of Central Lancashire graduate. FOCUS Fan-engagement content and match analysis across the Premier League and Championship. THE INSIGHT Oliver utilises a network of club and industry contacts to deliver verified reporting and fan-first content. He provides the human stories behind the results to ensure fans get the full picture as it happens.

Man United defender Noussair Mazraoui is surely set to leave the club at the earliest opportunity after he was tormented in their Champions League loss to Como.

United's winless run was extended to seven games as they were thrashed 4-1 by Como on Wednesday night.

Their worst run without a victory since 2015 has only increased the pressure on Michael Carrick and his players as a managerial change is touted.

Carrick was forced to put out a depleted backline in Northern Italy due to injury issues, as Matthijs de Ligt featured after 11 months out and Mazraoui started at left-back.

The Moroccan produced a shocking individual display against Como's dynamic attacking forces to surely push him further down Carrick's pecking order at the back.

He has rarely shone for the Red Devils over the last 12 months and such a showing should be the final nail in the coffin for him possibly earning a new contract beyond 2028.

Mazraoui, 28, only played 20 times and started just 11 league games last season as he struggled with persistent injury issues.

He made his fourth start of the campaign against Como on Wednesday, but failed to prove that he should be utilised more often by Carrick.

His performance proved the opposite, if anything, as he only won one of five attempted ground duels, made just one tackle, was dribbled past once and made an error leading to a shot.

Como captain Lucas da Cunha waltzed past Mazraoui to set Nico Paz up for the hosts' opener inside three minutes, while he failed to stop Kayky's cross for Jacobo Ramon to make it 4-0 in the second half.

The 28-year-old only made one progressive carry from full-back and failed to create a chance, while he lost possession a whopping nine times.

It was the most disappointing performance of his season so far, and surely proved that he should not be kept around at Old Trafford for much longer as he approaches the final 18 months of his contract.

Luke Shaw is currently the only established senior left-back in the United squad after their failure to bolster the position in the summer.

They wanted to sign Lewis Hall, but Newcastle have now tied him down to a new contract and he will not be leaving Tyneside anytime soon.

Sources have exclusively revealed to Football Insider that United are extensively scouting Bournemouth's Adrien Truffert, and he is a top target for left-back for the coming transfer windows.

Barcelona's Alejandro Balde and Aston Villa's Ian Maatsen are also believed to be on their radar, with Mazraoui's future surely heading one way as a result of the ongoing search.

#105. Herrera backs Fernandes and urges patience with Carrick

Former Manchester United midfielder Ander Herrera has defended captain Bruno Fernandes amid a difficult spell for the Reds and backed Michael Carrick to build a team capable of competing for trophies. Fernandes has not matched the high standards he set last season, despite opening this campaign with a hat-trick against Ipswich Town in August. Speaking on Rio Ferdinand Presents, Herrera said the scrutiny should not erase the responsibility Fernandes has shouldered across several seasons.

“For me, he’s a leader. He has had Manchester United on his back a lot of years, a lot of seasons,” Herrera said on the podcast, in comments reported by The Mirror and The Peoples Person. He argued that Fernandes has taken responsibility in difficult moments: asking for the ball, scoring and assisting, and speaking to the media after defeats.

Herrera’s defence comes as the captain’s form and role remain under close scrutiny during United’s broader slump. The former midfielder said Fernandes could have left but stayed to face difficult periods, and believes the criticism risks overlooking what he has contributed.

Herrera said Fernandes has repeatedly been a solution for United rather than the problem. – Ander Herrera, speaking on Rio Ferdinand Presents

Fernandes’ reduced creative output has been one part of the conversation around United’s struggles, alongside wider questions about the team’s form. Herrera’s point is not that the captain has been beyond criticism; it is that his record of stepping forward in adversity deserves to be part of the assessment.

Herrera also made a clear case for patience with Carrick, whom he believes can lead United towards silverware. He said coaches need time to build something, while acknowledging that the Premier League has become more competitive than the one he encountered during his own United spell.

Herrera said Carrick is the right man to bring trophies to the club and should be given time to build something. His backing is emphatic, though it is a belief in Carrick’s capacity to compete rather than a guarantee of a title on a fixed schedule.

Herrera’s perspective comes from five years at Old Trafford. He joined from Athletic Club in 2014, made 189 appearances and scored 20 goals in all competitions before leaving as a free agent in 2019; he later played for Paris Saint-Germain before returning to Athletic Club and Real Zaragoza.

He said the club informed him by letter that it would automatically extend his fifth season, rather than calling to discuss keeping him. Herrera said he had been happy at United and would have liked to stay, but the way the message was delivered left him hurt.

Herrera said receiving the letter made him feel his time at United was over and left him heartbroken. – Ander Herrera, speaking on Rio Ferdinand Presents

United’s results will keep attention on both the captain’s influence and Carrick’s plans for the side. Herrera believes Fernandes has repeatedly carried responsibility and that Carrick can build a team that competes, but his comments do not remove the pressure for improvement.

On the prospect of a Premier League title in two or three years, Herrera said he could not guarantee one but believed Carrick’s side would be competing.

#108. Kritik an Fußball-Superstar: Ten Hag tritt gegen Ronaldo nach: „Konnte es einfach nicht“

Cristiano Ronaldo ist 41 und gilt als Mustersportler. Sein Ex-Trainer Erik ten Hag schildert Erlebnisse aus der gemeinsamen Zeit bei Manchester United, die nicht ins Bild passen.

Erik ten Hag hat mehrere Jahre nach der gemeinsamen Zeit bei Manchester United gegen Fußball-Superstar Cristiano Ronaldo verbal nachgetreten. Der Niederländer, der im Sommer 2025 nach drei Spielen als Chefcoach beim Fußball-Bundesligisten Bayer Leverkusen entlassen worden war, zeichnete im Gespräch mit dem Sportportal „The Athletic“ ein Bild von Ronaldo, das nicht zum sonstigen Image des mittlerweile 41 Jahre alten Portugiesen passt.

Ronaldo habe Trainingseinheiten versäumt, bei Übungen nicht die erforderliche Herzfrequenz erreicht und nach einer 0:4-Niederlage bei Brentford im August 2022 bei einem Lauf nur zwei der drei geplanten Runden absolviert. „Ich habe ihn damit konfrontiert“, sagte der 56-jährige ten Hag.

Er habe Ronaldo erklärt: „Ronnie, alle wollen für dich arbeiten, um dich in die richtige Position zu bringen. Aber wenn du dich nicht selbst in den Strafraum bringst, kannst du kein Tor schießen.“ Ronaldo sei dazu nicht in der Lage gewesen, fügte ten Hag hinzu.

An Ronaldos außergewöhnlichen Fähigkeiten ließ ten Hag keinen Zweifel. Der Portugiese gehöre zu den Größten der Fußballgeschichte. Gerade von einem Spieler dieses Kalibers habe er jedoch erwartet, mit gutem Beispiel voranzugehen und seinen Teil zum Erfolg der Mannschaft beizutragen. Ten Hag wollte Manchester United mit konsequentem Pressing gegen den Ball spielen lassen. Ronaldo habe dieses Konzept jedoch nicht mittragen können. „Er konnte es einfach nicht.“

Zudem habe Ronaldo anderen Spielern vermittelt, sich im Training zu schonen. „Man hat Trainingseinheiten, da kann man die Beine schonen. Aber zuerst muss man trainieren, um das richtige Fitnessniveau zu erreichen.“ Ten Hag zufolge habe Ronaldos Einstellung auch auf andere Spieler abgefärbt.

Ten Hags Urteil über die Zusammenarbeit mit Ronaldo fiel entsprechend deutlich aus: „Er hat die Mannschaft im Stich gelassen, und er hat mich im Stich gelassen.“

„The Athletic“ berichtet, Ronaldo habe auf eine Anfrage zu ten Hags Äußerungen bislang nicht reagiert. Ten Hag betreute den Portugiesen in der Saison 2022/23 rund ein halbes Jahr bei Manchester United. Im Januar 2023 verließ Ronaldo den englischen Traditionsclub und wechselte nach Saudi-Arabien. Auch ten Hags Zeit in Manchester endete vorzeitig: Im Oktober 2024 wurde der Niederländer entlassen.

Das ist eine Nachricht direkt aus dem dpa-Newskanal.
