#109. Man Utd fear £74m star is ruled out for rest of the year – what are their options?

Manchester United face being without Benjamin Sesko for the rest of the year.

Sesko has not started a Premier League game this season and has not featured in any capacity since the derby defeat to Manchester City midway through September.

The Slovenia international picked up a shin injury last month – thought to be an issue separate to the one that ended his 2025-26 season early and ruled him out for the entire pre-season campaign.

There were six appearances in all competitions this term, scoring in back-to-back games against Everton and Sabah before his latest issue struck. The 23-year-old stayed in Manchester during last month’s international break to begin undergoing tests to determine the severity of the issue and has not returned to training since. Michael Carrick hopes to offer a clearer picture on his status when he speaks to the media on Friday.

Sign up to our daily football newsletter, pick your team and get the latest news sent directly to your inbox every morning.

The Sun however report United are bracing themselves for bad news with fears the striker will be ruled out for four months after extensive tests and checks at Carrington.

Having scored a goal every 85 minutes under Carrick last season, his 2025-26 campaign came to a premature end in May after a heavy landing down Old Trafford’s famous slope around the edge of the pitch against Liverpool, aggravating a shin injury in the process. Sesko had initially suffered the injury in early spring, carrying the problem as United chased down third place.

Sesko’s injury is one that is difficult to judge without intimate knowledge of what’s going inside the United medical room. But this latest setback would suggest he would have perhaps benefited with more recovery time – even if his side have started the season in desperate need of a formidable presence up front.

Sesko has not started a Premier League game since 3 May with his rehabilitation stretching across the summer months and into September. United staff have already seen the patient approach fall short. There are different options going forward but it now appears there will be another extended layoff for the player.

‘The risk is always there with recurrence. With injuries like this, you are playing a game of whack-a-mole,’ Stephen Smith, CEO and founder of Kitman Labs which specialises in injury welfare and performance analytics, told Metro.

Use AI to go deeper into the stories you care about – powered by Metro and trusted publications.

‘They can be very challenging. The first thing you try isn’t always going to be the thing that works. They will have been assessing what they did the first time around and now looking at different options. I would be confident they won’t just be doing the same thing because that hasn’t fixed it. They will want to move on.

‘Caution is the right word here. Given this has flared up again they will be inclined to be more cautious, not less. In terms of timelines, unless he responds incredibly quickly and very differently than last time around, I wouldn’t be surprised if we saw a similar time frame and similar time lost as we saw last time.’

As Carrick alluded to in his press conference this week, there has been extensive testing on Sesko’s shin which may have uncovered something new. It will be then up to the club to decide which action to take.

‘One option is to continue the conservative management. That is the route they have been going down so far,’ Smith continued. ‘Separately, they may also look at some very specific treatments that are aimed at bone or soft tissue healing.

‘If it keeps resurfacing and imaging points to something more structural like a stress fracture that isn’t healing, more invasive options become part of the conversation.

‘Sometimes surgery can be considered. Looking at something like this initially, all you are doing is X-ray which gives you some picture of the bone but you are not going to get everything. So they may have done MRI or CT (scans) on the bone as well to see if they can see any further damage or trauma.

‘Sometimes you get microfractures that aren’t healing. They can go in and perform micro fracture treatment around it. They may get in there and put small breaks into the bone to create blood flow and try kickstart the healing process. But that’s a very invasive option and you are left dealing with a broken bone and all the time requirements from that to handle.

‘If it keeps recurring beyond this than a more definitive surgical option becomes a bigger part of the conversation. But that is generally a last resort in this scenario when the conservative approaches have had a proper chance and not been successful.’

A prolonged absence for the RB Leipzig star will heighten United’s concerns in attack having failed to sign another centre forward this summer despite Sesko’s shin problems.

And given how tight the purse strings were in the final weeks of the summer, a major arrival in January seems unlikely.

Carrick may be forced to continue playing with the hand he has been dealt. Bryan Mbeumo has led the line in United’s last three matches with four goals this season. He is United’s best available option with excellent finishes against Tottenham and Leeds demonstrating he is a reliable goal scorer.

But it stretches an already thin squad. Joshua Zirkzee, who almost left the club on deadline day to join Everton, is the only other alternative and has started just one match since Carrick took the reins in January.

Matheus Cunha was given the role through the middle in the opening weeks of the season but was unconvincing, more adept off the left.

Marcus Rashford has also been deployed up top at various stages in his career but it is an option Carrick has not turned to yet.

JJ Gabriel spent much of last season playing as a false nine for United’s Under-18s but any hopes of him being part of their future appear to have been extinguished. Chido Obi is out on loan with Dutch side Willem II with the cupboard looking worryingly bare for United.

Yoro is suspended for Wednesday’s Champions League clash with Como, but former Red Devils captain Rio Ferdinand is keen to see him back in the lineup for the weekend after his impressive performance against Leeds United.

‘I thought Leny Yoro, especially in the first half, defending the back post was top drawer,’ Ferdinand said on his YouTube channel.

‘He looks like he’s growing in confidence and he looks like he just needs a run in the team now. I don’t know what position he’ll make his own first but he will go I just have to be in this team.

‘If he just continues playing the way he is and continues to get games, I don’t care who gets back fit now, I think he needs to stay in the team, prove his worth.

‘I think every week he looks stronger, I thought in Madrid he looked solid, the game before for France he was outstanding at centre-back and he comes back with a new-found confidence and I think that’s good for Manchester United.

‘The way he defended those crosses at the back post and in those away games you’ve got to have people willing to put their bodies there and I thought today in that sense especially he was top class.’

#115. Where is it going wrong for Carrick's Man Utd?

Man Utd were thumped 4-1 by Como in the Champions League; defeat exposed defensive, attacking and physical issues which have led to dreadful start to the season under Michael Carrick, with team now winless in seven games across all competitions

Senior Football Journalist @psmithXI

Please use Chrome browser for a more accessible video player

Thursday marks five months exactly since Man Utd announced Michael Carrick would be staying on as head coach. There will be little mood for celebrating anniversaries the morning after his worst night in the role.

The 4-1 thumping in Como left Carrick, in his own words, "angry". It also ruptured open divisions in the camp, with club captain Bruno Fernandes criticising the tactical plan.

United have made their worst-ever start to a Premier League season and that European humbling means they are now seven without a win across all competitions.

It's a shocking collapse in form from the brilliant run at the back end of last season which convinced United to sign Carrick up until 2028. So what's gone wrong?

This week, it was Fernandes. Last week, it was Lisandro Martinez hitting out at the hierarchy. The centre-back had technical director Jason Wilcox in his sights when he criticised the club for not strengthening the squad further in the summer transfer window.

United spent £163m - less than promoted pair Hull City and Ipswich Town, as well as seven other Premier League clubs. Midfield was the focus but left-back and centre-forward areas weren't bolstered.

Reasons for the scale of United's summer outlay are multi-layered - stretching right back to the Glazer takeover - but crucially Carrick was not able to significantly upgrade this group to go from playing just 40 games last season to taking on Champions League football and trying to improve on a third-place Premier League finish this term.

Please use Chrome browser for a more accessible video player

The World Cup made it a draining summer for many players at elite clubs. But when concerns about this season's increased workload for United were combined with Youri Tielemans suggesting there had been a lighter load of pre-season running, alarm bells began to ring.

United's intensity - or lack of - has proven to be a standout problem.

They have been out-run in six of their seven Premier League games. The only exception was when Man City played over an hour with a man less. On Wednesday, their out-of-possession failings were again exposed.

"If you don't run back, you ain't going to win," was how Carrick put it.

Please use Chrome browser for a more accessible video player

Fernandes may have issues with the pressing plan deployed by Carrick and his coaching staff in Como but with just one goal involvement in his past nine games for United, the drop-off from the team's talisman has clearly been extremely damaging.

All four of his goal involvements in the Premier League came against Ipswich Town (three goals, one assist).

After breaking the assists record last season, his chance creation figures have halved this season.

Fernandes' figures reflect a wider problem to United's attack.

Since that Ipswich game, he has had 12 shots - but just two on target.

No team has had more shots than United this season - but they rank joint-worst for xG per shot.

Too often it's speculative rather than substantial from United's forwards.

They were the top scorers during the final 17 games of last season, when Carrick was in charge and harnessing the powers of his Portuguese playmaker.

But the continued absence of Benjamin Sesko due to injury and the lack of an alternative out-and-out striker has left United without a focal point to target. Fernandes took on the role of false-nine on Wednesday and it didn't work.

Please use Chrome browser for a more accessible video player

If you're struggling to score, you need security at the back to give you a chance - but United haven't kept a clean sheet this season.

In Italy, the full-backs Diogo Dalot and Noussair Mazraoui had games to forget, while Matthijs de Ligt was understandably, laboured on his first appearance in 11 months.

Injuries to Martinez and Luke Shaw, illness for Ayden Heaven, and suspension for Leny Yoro didn't help. But whether it's a personnel problem, an issue with United's structure without the ball, or simply their intensity and fitness to shutdown their opponents, they are being too often opened up.

Different midfield pairings have been tried in front of the backline but the right combination - which likely includes Carlos Baleba, who is working back to fitness - is still something Carrick is searching for.

Many problems mean there is no easy answer.

United could have four of the nine players unavailable on Wednesday back for Bournemouth on Sunday, which will help. Yoro, Martinez and Heaven would refresh the defence, Marcus Rashford could add a spark to the attack.

But Fernandes suggested United must win a mental battle as much as a physical one this weekend.

Please use Chrome browser for a more accessible video player

On the impact of Como's opener after two minutes, he said: "Conceding the goal very early makes us feeling, going into the game, already in a mood of a defeat.

"It's very difficult to get out of that at the moment, without being ourselves and trying to push and trying to be brave, trying to have the ball, trying to make something happen.

"If we're not able to do that it's going to be very difficult to get out of this situation."

At Old Trafford, United are going to have to step up and show that bravery on and off the ball to deliver a performance and result to shift the narrative and put what has gone wrong so far this season behind them.

But quickly recapturing the magic of that run of form under Carrick in the second half of last season from this position would be some trick.

#116. Man Utd to re-issue iconic 1992 League Cup final jacket

The 1992 League Cup final is remembered for two things from a Manchester United perspective.

Firstly, it marked the clubâs first-ever triumph in the competition, as the Red Devils edged Nottingham Forest 1-0 at the old Wembley Stadium. Secondly â and perhaps just as fondly â it gave the world that pre-match jacket, a technicolour time capsule of early â90s football fashion.

While the match itself canât be replayed, the jacket can be relived â and Manchester United, alongside adidas, are doing just that, restoring it in all its retro glory for a new generation of fans and fashion aficionados.

The design is pure vintage: a black base with vibrant turquoise sleeve panels, and a bold abstract pattern in black, white, and green across the lower half. Itâs finished with a classic Adidas text logo and a special commemorative crest reading: "League Cup Final, Wembley 1992."

More than three decades on, United are giving supporters the chance to own two timeless pieces of club history â garments that capture both a landmark trophy win and the fearless swagger of â90s football style.

#117. Jamie Carragher hails ‘quality’ Man Utd star after Michael Carrick changes his position

Jamie Carragher hailed the ‘quality’ of Manchester United midfielder Youri Tielemans after Michael Carrick tweaked his position in the draw with Leeds.

United left Elland Road with a point on Sunday after Dominic Calvert-Lewin’s second half goal wiped out Bryan Mbeumo’s opener.

The visitors started the game brightly with Tielemans occupying a move advanced role alongside Bruno Fernandes and Mbeumo in attack.

The Belgium international, signed from Aston Villa for £35million in the summer, has started all seven Premier League matches this term, five alongside Kobbie Mainoo in central midfield in a partnership that was heavily criticised by former United captain Gary Neville.

Sign up to our daily football newsletter, pick your team and get the latest news sent directly to your inbox every morning.

With Mainoo injured, Carlos Baleba and Andrey Santos started to provide more energy in central midfield against Leeds with Tielemans impressing in his role further up the pitch, providing the assist for Mbeumo’s goal with a precise pass in the final third.

‘This is vitally important for United,’ Carragher told Sky Sports. ‘The fact he scans and plays it on his backfoot means he can play it wide.

#118. “Absolute disgrace”: Bruno Fernandes heavily criticised and “embarrassed” by Michael Carrick after miserable Man Utd loss

By Oliver Thomas | 22 Oct 2026 07:35, Last updated: 22 Oct 2026 07:37

Jamie Carragher has launched a scathing attack on Bruno Fernandes after the Manchester United captain appeared to criticise head coach Michael Carrick following Wednesday’s heavy 4-1 Champions League defeat to Como.

The Red Devils set unwanted history in Italy and Fernandes endured a night to forget, with the out-of-sorts playmaker hooked off in the 67th minute by Carrick with the score at 3-0.

After the match, Fernandes seemingly questioned Carrick’s tactical approach, saying: "It was a decision from the staff to not press man-to-man and sometimes leave space behind."

Carrick responded to those comments at his post-match press conference, adding: "Whatever system you play, whatever tactics you play, if you don't run, and you don't run forward, and you don't run back, you aren't going to win."

Fernandes, 32, is the second Man Utd player within a week to make a bold post-match statement after Lisandro Martinez, who was critical of the club’s hierarchy following a 2-0 defeat to Atletico Madrid and has since issued a public apology.

Former Liverpool defender Jamie Carragher believes that Martinez was wrong to take aim at Man United’s hierarchy, while he has heavily criticised Fernandes for his post-match comments after being “embarrassed” by Carrick.

Speaking as a pundit for CBS Sports, Carragher said: "We said Lisandro Martinez was bang out of order last week for calling out the hierarchy above him. For Bruno Fernandes to do that is a disgrace really.

"When you think about last season, he got Player of the Year. That was on the back of Michael Carrick becoming the manager. He wouldn't have got that award without Michael Carrick, because Michael Carrick put him back in his rightful position and he then went on to have a fantastic season because he's a talented footballer.

“But when you think of him as a footballer, he's a talent, but in terms of attitude and the way he goes about things, it's not right at all.

"And he's reacted like that because he's been brought off and he's been embarrassed tonight by the manager as he brought him off, and when he talks about pressing, whether it's man to man or zonal, for years, Bruno Fernandes has meant Manchester United can't press because he does things for the crowd.

"I commentate on games watching Man United and he runs all over the place like a kid in the schoolyard for the supporters. They all think he's working hard, he's not. He's killing the rest of the team doing it."

Carragher added: "Years ago, there was a captain called Bryan Robson, one of the greatest players to ever play for Man United because he played for the club, he didn't throw the manager or the players under the bus.

“He kept fighting for Manchester United and watching him throw his arms up every week, constantly in a game is an absolute disgrace, calling the manager out considering how he's performed this season on the pitch. Awful attitude and not what a Manchester United captain should do."

Asked about Man United’s identity and what direction the club is heading in, Carragher said: "Isn't it funny? How many managers has Bruno Fernandes had? So every single manager at Manchester United doesn't have an identity?

“How many managers are we on now for Bruno Fernandes? Five? They change the manager every season... Manchester United is rotten from top to bottom.

"I said earlier on, it's not about the manager. It's everything about the club, and who has got the answers? We comment on it all the time, 'they might need to change the manager or do this, do that.' Are we ever right really? Because no one ever seems to get it right.

“There's no doubt the manager needs to be changed now. Right now this can't go on for a club of the stature of Manchester United.

"But I'm glad Michael Carrick come out and just said that about Bruno Fernandes, that he don't run, because they don't run, this team.

"And what he did last season, no-one's proclaiming Michael Carrick as one of the greatest managers in European football, but what he did was he come and helped his own football club last year when he was out of work himself. He's come in and he did amazing.

"And the reason Manchester United are in the Champions League is because of Carrick, he got them in there. The reason they got another £100m, £150m into the club is because he got them in.

“No-one thought he was going to get into the Champions League last season, so I feel for him a little bit. He loves the club, he's a legendary figure as a player, but obviously it's not working out for him as a manager and that needs to be changed.

"But I'm delighted he brought him off, Fernandes, and I'm delighted that he had a little pop back there in the press because that's what they need some of those players."

Seeking to end a seven-match winless run in all competitions, Man United return to Premier League action on Sunday when they welcome Bournemouth to Old Trafford.

#119. Next Man United manager: 39-year-old ‘sounded out’ to replace Michael Carrick as sack decision looms

By Oliver Thomas | 22 Oct 2026 07:15, Last updated: 22 Oct 2026 07:18

Julian Nagelsmann has reportedly been sounded out as a potential replacement for Michael Carrick as Manchester United manager.

Carrick finds himself under immense pressure following a disastrous 4-1 Champions League defeat to Como on Wednesday night, extending the club’s winless run in all competitions to seven matches.

The 44-year-old has taken full responsibility for Man United’s slump in form and apologised to supporters for the result in Italy, while his tactics used in the loss to Como appeared to be criticised by Red Devils captain Bruno Fernandes.

Carrick steered Man United back into the Champions League courtesy of a third-placed Premier League finish last season, but his team sit 13th in the table this term - already 14 points behind leaders Man City - and have posted just two wins in their opening 10 games across all tournaments.

Although Carrick is under contract at Old Trafford until June 2028, talkSPORT claims that Man United now appear to be preparing for life without him.

The report states that 39-year-old Nagelsmann leads the list of candidates to replace Carrick and has admirers within Man United’s hierarchy.

Nagelsmann is said to have been in the Red Devils’ thinking before they opted to appoint Carrick on a permanent basis in May.

However, talks ultimately never reached an advanced stage as Nagelsmann was taking charge of Germany at the 2026 World Cup.

The former Bayern Munich and RB Leipzig boss is now a free agent after he stepped down from his Germany post following a disappointing last-32 World Cup exit.

Known for his tactical flexibility and high-pressing style of play, Nagelsmann won 23 of his 37 games in charge of Germany (62.2%) while he liftedthe 2021-22 Bundesliga title during his time at Bayern Munich.

It has recently been reported that Brighton & Hove Albion boss Fabian Hurzeler is the 'standout Premier League candidate' for Man United in their hunt to replace Carrick.

Former Chelsea and Tottenham Hotspur head coach Antonio Conte is also believed to be open to taking the Red Devils job after leaving his role at Napoli in the summer.

talkSPORT claims that Eddie Howe, who left Newcastle United at the end of July, is a manager who could get the Man United job ‘easily’ as a free agent.

USA manager Mauricio Pochettino, former Ipswich Town boss and ex-Man Utd coach Kieran McKenna, and Al-Hilal’s Simone Inzaghi have also been listed as potential candidates to replace Carrick.

Updated - October 22, 2026 11:29 am IST - Paris, France

Como's Nico Paz scores against Manchester United in the Champions League on October 21, 2026 | Photo Credit: Reuters

Manchester United were hammered 4-1 by Como in the Champions League on Wednesday as the pressure increased on beleaguered manager Michael Carrick, while a late Declan Rice equaliser earned Arsenal a 2-2 draw away to Bayern Munich.

Elsewhere, Real Madrid thumped RB Leipzig 4-1 and there were also notable victories for Inter Milan, Aston Villa and Real Betis.

The most remarkable scoreline, though, came in driving rain on the banks of Lake Como, where United suffered their joint-heaviest loss in the Champions League in the last 30 years.

Mbappé, Paz, Rice, Diomande ⚽️ Best goal? 🤔 @Heineken | #UCLGOTD https://t.co/IR2oTZYWTU

Nico Paz scored twice for Cesc Fabregas's Como, with Tasos Douvikas and Jacobo Ramon getting their other goals. This was a second 4-1 home win in this season's competition for the tournament debutants.

Joshua Zirkzee pulled one back at the death for United, but they are now seven games without a win in all competitions.

The Red Devils have just one win in the Premier League this season and have now suffered back-to-back losses in Europe. Carrick's position risks becoming untenable.

"I have not given my own position any thought," Carrick said.

"For us to play in that way, it's not acceptable. It wasn't a good performance at all," he admitted.

Last season's beaten finalists Arsenal have seven points from a possible nine after Rice struck in the 82nd minute to earn a draw in Munich.

Bukayo Saka went off injured early on but his replacement Eberechi Eze fired Arsenal into the lead, only for Alphonso Davies to level before the interval.

Harry Kane set up that goal with a magnificent pass and then gave Bayern the lead from a second-half penalty, his 10th strike this season in all competitions.

Bayern's Harry Kane, left, and Arsenal's Declan Rice talk after the UEFA Champions League match | Photo Credit: AP

But Arsenal hit back as Bayern registered a second straight Champions League stalemate.

"We looked dangerous, we got the goal and we had a moment there where we could have killed off the game," Kane told broadcaster DAZN.

"Probably a draw was a fair result overall... There's a lot of games and we've got players with injuries, players missing tonight, so we take the point and move on."

Jose Mourinho's Real are one of five teams with three wins from three in this season's Champions League, as goals either side of half-time from Bernardo Silva and Arda Guler set them on their way at the Bernabeu.

Kylian Mbappe made it 12 goals in 12 games this season for Real before Tidiam Gomis pulled one back, but Yan Diomande completed Real's victory with a spectacular volley against his former club.

"We know every point is important to get into the top eight, we know we have to win as many games as possible -- we've won three and there are five to go," Mbappe told Movistar.

Real Madrid's Kylian Mbappe scores against RB Leipzig | Photo Credit: Reuters

Villa also have a perfect record after three games, as they won 3-1 at home to Viking Stavanger with Ross Barkley's opener being followed by Emiliano Buendia's penalty and Henrik Heggheim's own goal.

Peter Christiansen got one back for the Norwegian champions who are still looking for their first win.

Inter had Alessandro Bastoni sent off midway through the second half before Carlos Augusto's late goal secured a 1-0 win at home to Shakhtar Donetsk, as the Italian champions got their first points.

Betis have maximum points as an Isco penalty handed them a 1-0 win over Feyenoord in Seville.

Club Brugge defeated Lens 3-0 in Belgium as Hans Vanaken, Joaquin Seys and Carlos Forbs all found the net.

Victor Osimhen sealed a 4-2 victory for Galatasaray away to his old side Lille in France, after earlier efforts from Yunus Akgun and Aleksey Batrakov and a Nabil Bentaleb own goal.

Teenager Soriba Diaoune got both goals for Lille, who have lost three out of three.

LASK of Austria have their first points after a fine 3-2 victory over Sporting in Lisbon.

Luis Suarez scored twice for Sporting, including an early penalty, but Christoph Lang, Sasa Kalajdzic and Krystof Danek all netted in between for the visitors.

Reigning champions PSG are top of the 36-team standings after beating Barcelona 4-2 on Tuesday, but the French side confirmed Wednesday that star winger Khvicha Kvaratskhelia ruptured the anterior cruciate ligament in his right knee in that game.

He will undergo surgery in the coming days amid doubts as to whether he will play again this season.

#121. Man United head coach Michael Carrick dissects devastating Como defeat as 45-year-old faces the sack

By Matt Law | 21 Oct 2026 20:54, Last updated: 21 Oct 2026 20:57

Manchester United head coach Michael Carrick has apologised to the club's supporters following Wednesday's shocking defeat to Como in the Champions League.

The 20-time English champions were behind after two minutes of the league stage fixture and ultimately suffered a 4-1 loss to the Serie A outfit.

Man United are now without a victory in their last seven matches in all competitions and have only won two of their opening 10 games of the season.

Carrick said that "everybody has got to take responsibility" for what occurred in Italy, and the Englishman also apologised to the club's supporters.

"It was more than disappointing. I think everybody has got to take responsibility for that. I 100% take responsibility for the overall team and the performance - that's where it stops," Carrick told TNT Sports after the match.

"It stops with me and I'm absolutely fine with that. I accept that totally. But as a supporter of this football club for so long, to see a performance like that is unacceptable, I have to say.

"I care an unbelievable amount about the supporters. They are the heartbeat of this football club and they always will be. For them to travel, I had to apologise for the performance.

"To have to watch a performance like that, I totally feel for them. There are no excuses at all. It is not acceptable."

When asked whether he still has the support of the board, Carrick said: "It's not about me. It's about digesting the game and how it went. That is the most important thing right now."

Carrick's position has not been helped by comments from Man United captain Bruno Fernandes following the defeat to Como.

"It's a decision from the staff to not press man to man and sometimes leave space behind and try to get the ball, wait for them, but sometimes when you give space to these teams they get confidence," Fernandes told TNT Sports after the match.

When asked whether he was insinuating that Carrick got his tactics wrong, Fernandes said: "That's on the manager and staff to see, not me. Sometimes you have to be more brave under pressure, take the risk of pressing high and making them uncomfortable.

"As you saw with them [Como], the centre-back was coming to mark me in my own half. We need to do that as a team but we were unable to.

"A team that plays through the middle with small connections, if you give them too much time and space then they can hurt you out wide."

Man United will welcome Bournemouth to Old Trafford in the Premier League on Sunday, but it remains to be seen whether Carrick stays in charge.

Eddie Howe is currently the favourite to become the next Man United head coach, with the Englishman available for an immediate start after leaving Newcastle United at the end of July.

Champions Arsenal needed a late goal from Noni Madueke, their only shot on target, to scrape ‌a 1-0 win at Nottingham Forest on Sunday (October 18, 2026) and remain three points behind Premier League leaders ​Manchester City.

Brighton & Hove Albion's Diego Gomez struck a sensational goal from inside his own half in a ⁠5-0 rout of Crystal Palace on Sunday to leave his side third in the table with 16 points, five behind leaders Manchester City.

Manchester United and bitter rivals Leeds United shared the spoils in a 1-1 draw at Elland Road at the end of a turbulent ‌week for the Red Devils in which defender Lisandro Martinez had publicly challenged the club's hierarchy.

Arsenal's win at Nottingham Forest sees them move back above Brighton into second 🔴 https://t.co/4RG6ExcqiE

Leeds are seventh in the table and Manchester United, who have just one win from seven games, are 13th.

Bournemouth ‌beat Sunderland 2-0 for their first league victory of the season to climb to 15th place. Sunderland remained ‌in ⁠the drop zone in 19th place.

At the City Ground, a frustrated Arsenal finally sealed victory in the ⁠80th minute.

Manchester United's Bruno Fernandes and Andrey Santos at the end of the match against Leeds | Photo Credit: Reuters

Ibrahim Sangare hesitated when he could have cleared the ball, leaving Forest manager Oliver Glasner visibly fuming, and second-half substitute Madueke then danced past Daniel Munoz before hammering a shot that deflected off Jair Cunha and wrong-footed Matz Sels.

"We're really happy, we did it all together. We showed ​grit, determination, and maybe we showed a champion ‌spirit here for that, really pleased," Madueke told Sky Sports.

Murillo almost salvaged a point for Forest in injury time when he beat three players before unleashing a shot that sailed agonisingly wide.

The defeat left Forest, who have yet to win or even score at the City Ground this season, in the drop zone in 18th place with ‌five points.

At the Amex, Brighton got on the scoresheet in the fourth minute through a Jaydee Canvot ​own goal, before Gomez struck a goal of the season contender three minutes later, robbing Adam Wharton of the ball near the halfway line before taking a touch and firing the ball over ⁠keeper Walter Benitez and into the net.

"When I got the ball I knew the goalkeeper was a little bit ahead," Gomez told Sky Sports. "When I scored I didn't know where to run or what to do. I was very happy with ‌the goal."

Yasin Ayari padded Brighton's lead three minutes later, before Charalampos Kostoulas and Jack Hinshelwood completed the rout after the break.

Kostoulas scored just after halftime when Ayari's shot deflected into the Greece international's path, and Hinshelwood struck in the 84th when Luka Vuskovic's header was scooped off the line by Benitez and he fired home the rebound.

Manchester United looked on course for a much-needed victory when Bryan Mbeumo scored in the 35th minute after missing a couple of chances. Mbeumo held off James Justin before tapping in a Youri Tielemans cross from six yards out.

Leeds striker Dominic Calvert-Lewin equalised ‌in the 51st minute, chesting down the ball from a corner before poking home.

"We are slightly disappointed it is just a point," Leeds ​boss Daniel Farke told Sky. "Second half, we got the equaliser, we should have kept up the dominance, too many strikes went over the crossbar but it was overall a solid performance.

"We took it ⁠as a compliment that we have done many things right over the last month if we were seen as favourites against ⁠Manchester United."

Manchester defender Martinez retained his place in Michael Carrick's starting lineup despite criticising United's hierarchy following Tuesday's 2-0 Champions League defeat at Atletico Madrid, questioning the club’s summer investment and singling out director of football ‌Jason Wilcox before later apologising.

Bournemouth punished a shaky Sunderland start to take the lead after just six minutes, when Lewis Cook lofted a long ball forward that Evanilson ran on to and squared for Marcus Tavernier to head ​home from close range.

Evanilson scored himself in the 48th minute when the Brazilian rounded goalkeeper Robin Roefs before slotting home left-footed.

Updated - October 17, 2026 11:01 pm IST - Madrid

Atletico Madrid's Ademola Lookman is in action during the Champions League match against Manchester United in Madrid | Photo Credit: AP

Atletico Madrid eased to a 2-0 win over Manchester United in the Champions League on Tuesday (October 13, 2026) to pile pressure on Red Devils coach Michael Carrick.

Pablo Barrios pounced inside the first minute for Diego Simeone's side and after Leny Yoro's 74th minute red card set United back further, Ademola Lookman added another for the hosts in Madrid.

Man United have made their worst domestic start in 40 years and this league phase defeat in the Spanish capital was another blow, with the team now winless in five games across all competitions.

Carrick handed Carlos Baleba his first start for the club since arriving from Brighton this summer, as well as pushing captain Bruno Fernandes out to the right side.

Atletico were without their skipper Koke, after the death of his mother, while coach Simeone selected Julian Alvarez from the off for the first time this season at the Metropolitano stadium, after he tried to force a departure this summer.

"Defend what we love," Atletico fans ordered their supporters with a banner display before kick-off and the Rojiblancos were ahead after just 50 seconds.

Alex Baena found a way in behind United's defence and cut the ball across for Barrios, with Ayden Heaven unable to keep the midfielder's shot from crossing the line.

Bryan Mbuemo might have equalised for the visitors, nudging the ball through Cristian Romero's legs but firing just wide.

Alejandro Grimaldo came inches away from extending Atletico's lead with a free-kick which hit the angle between post and crossbar.

The hosts thought they had a second when Johnny Cardoso finished neatly from the edge of the box before half-time but the goal was disallowed after a VAR review showed the ball had struck Marcos Llorente's hand during the build-up.

Alvarez, yet to score this season for Atletico, hit the post early in the second half.

United rallied and Fernandes's lob drifted narrowly wide before the Portuguese playmaker hit the crossbar from the left of the box after a corner routine.

However the three-time European champions suffered a setback when defender Yoro was harshly dismissed after a VAR review for bringing down Canada striker Jonathan David as the last man.

It opened the door for Atletico to grab their second, with David foiled by goalkeeper Senne Lammens but London-born Nigeria international Lookman on hand to tuck home the rebound after 79 minutes.

Carrick withdrew playmaker Fernandes, looking ahead to the weekend visit to face rivals Leeds in the English top flight, where improvement is badly needed.

Atletico goalkeeper Jan Oblak saved from United substitute Matheus Cunha in the final stages but there was no way back for United.

Simeone's side were able to claim their first points after an opening defeat at Liverpool.

#124. Como 4-1 Man United: Red Devils captain Bruno Fernandes appears to question Michael Carrick after horrendous Champions League defeat

By Matt Law | 21 Oct 2026 20:30, Last updated: 21 Oct 2026 20:32

Manchester United captain Bruno Fernandes appeared to question the tactics of head coach Michael Carrick following Wednesday's 4-1 loss to Como.

The 20-time English champions are now without a victory in their last seven matches in all competitions, having been well-beaten by Cesc Fabregas side in the Champions League clash.

Fernandes struggled operating in a false nine position, and the Portugal international was substituted in the 67th minute of the match, with Joshua Zirkzee replacing him, and the latter scored a consolation for Man United late on.

The Red Devils skipper has said that it was "a decision from the staff to not press man to man", which caused issues for the English side throughout the contest.

Como outfought, outworked and outplayed a Man United team that have only been victorious in two of their first 10 matches of the 2026-27 campaign.

"It's not the first time, we started on the wrong foot, and they scored very early. Not much pressure on the ball, not many good movements with the ball, not many things going our way," Fernandes told TNT Sports.

"Conceding early makes us go into game with a mood of defeat, it's difficult to get out of that, trying to be brave, to have the ball and make something happen. If we are not able to do that, it is going to be very difficult to get out of this situation.

"It's a decision from the staff to not press man to man and sometimes leave space behind and try to get the ball, wait for them, but sometimes when you give space to these teams they get confidence."

7 - Manchester United are winless in seven consecutive games in all competitions for the first time since a run of eight matches between November and December 2015. Problems. https://t.co/2CCZsu6kLX

When asked whether he was insinuating that Carrick got his tactics wrong, Fernandes said: "That's on the manager and staff to see, not me. Sometimes you have to be more brave under pressure, take the risk of pressing high and making them uncomfortable.

"As you saw with them [Como], the centre-back was coming to mark me in my own half. We need to do that as a team but we were unable to.

"A team that plays through the middle with small connections, if you give them too much time and space then they can hurt you out wide."

The team's latest defeat will increase the pressure on Carrick, who apologised to the Man United supporters at the full-time whistle.

The Red Devils will continue their Premier League campaign at home to Bournemouth on Sunday afternoon, but it remains to be seen whether Carrick stays in charge.

#125. Como 4-1 Man United: Red Devils set unwanted history on horrific night for the club

By Matt Law | 21 Oct 2026 19:58, Last updated: 21 Oct 2026 20:32

Manchester United set unwanted history in their 4-1 defeat to Italian outfit Como in the Champions League.

The team's winless run in all competitions was stretched to seven matches on Wednesday evening when they were comfortably beaten by Cesc Fabregas' side.

Como outfought and outplayed Man United on an incredibly difficult night for the English team, who have only been victorious twice in their 10 games at the start of the 2026-27 campaign.

The Red Devils also created some unwanted history in the defeat to Como.

Man United became the first English team in history to concede in the first two minutes of successive major European matches - the first minute against Atletico Madrid last week, and the second minute against Como.

Man Utd are now on a run of seven games without winning across all competitions: ❌ 0-1 vs Man City ❌ 2-3 vs Brighton ? 1-1 vs Fulham ? 1-1 vs Tottenham ❌ 2-0 vs Atletico Madrid ? 1-1 vs Leeds ❌ 4-1 vs Como https://t.co/1R71XaoYPY

Nico Paz was the star of the show for Fabregas' side, scoring twice and providing an assist, with Man United finding it difficult to deal with the Argentina international.

Man United offered little throughout the match, but it might have been a different story had Diogo Dalot not passed up a glorious chance to score in the early exchanges.

Bruno Fernandes had a difficult night, with the Portugal international never looking comfortable in a false nine position, and he was substituted in the second period.

Matheus Cunha struggled in the first period and was replaced at the interval, while Youri Tielemans and Mason Mount also had tough nights.

Man United will continue their Premier League campaign against Bournemouth at Old Trafford on Sunday afternoon, but it remains to be seen if Michael Carrick is still in charge.

Carrick's position has come under serious threat due to the team's struggles, and he is the favourite to be the next Premier League manager to lose his job.

#128. Jurgen Klopp and Roberto Mancini battle for Arsenal, Man United target Nicolo Tresoldi

By Naim Beneddra | 21 Oct 2026 15:33

Former Liverpool manager Jurgen Klopp and Italy boss Roberto Mancini are competing to secure the international allegiance of Club Brugge striker Nicolo Tresoldi, whose Champions League hat-trick against Inter Milan has further enhanced his reputation amid interest from Arsenal and Manchester United.

The 22-year-old has emerged as one of Europe's most exciting young centre-forwards, scoring three times as Club Brugge stunned Italian champions Inter Milan with a remarkable 4-3 victory at San Siro on October 13.

Tresoldi's performance earned him the UEFA Player of the Match award and underlined why several leading European clubs have been monitoring his progress.

Arsenal and Manchester United have both been linked with the highly rated forward, who was reportedly valued at approximately €50m (£43m) during the summer transfer window.

However, his future is attracting attention beyond the transfer market, with Germany and Italy attempting to convince him to represent their senior national teams.

Klopp, who now manages Germany, and Mancini, who returned to take charge of Italy, have already held personal discussions with Tresoldi about his international ambitions.

Those conversations took place before his spectacular display against Inter, although the striker publicly revisited the subject after his Champions League heroics.

Despite interest from two of international football's most recognisable managers, Tresoldi has insisted that he is in no hurry to make a final decision.

Tresoldi's international situation is unusually complicated, with the Club Brugge forward eligible to represent three countries that have previously won the World Cup.

Born in Cagliari to an Italian father and Argentine mother, he moved to Germany at the age of 12 after his mother received a professional opportunity there.

He developed through Hannover 96's youth system and subsequently represented Germany at youth level, establishing himself as one of the country's most prolific Under-21 internationals.

Tresoldi has scored 16 goals in 27 appearances for Germany's Under-21 side, but his performances have also attracted the attention of Mancini.

The Italian coach is hoping to persuade the striker to represent the country of his birth, while Klopp wants him to continue his international development with Germany.

Speaking to ran earlier this month, Tresoldi revealed that Klopp had personally contacted him.

The former Liverpool manager initially sent him a message signed with his familiar nickname, Kloppo, before the pair spoke about his future.

Tresoldi admitted that he had been extremely nervous about the conversation, describing the exchange as a positive experience.

He subsequently confirmed that Mancini had also approached him.

Speaking after his hat-trick against Inter, Tresoldi told La Gazzetta dello Sport: "I've already spoken to Klopp and Mancini. I've explained how I see things."

However, the striker made clear that his immediate priority remained Germany's Under-21 team.

"For now, I'm enjoying myself with the youth teams, and then we'll see. We have a European Championship to win," he added.

Tresoldi had previously offered an indication of how difficult his decision could become.

"My heart is 50-50. Both countries mean a lot to me," he explained during a media appearance in September.

The forward intends to complete his involvement in the 2027 European Under-21 Championship, which takes place in Albania and Serbia, before deciding whether to represent Germany or Italy at senior level.

Argentina remains another possibility because of his family background, although his publicly acknowledged dilemma has primarily concerned Germany and Italy.

His decision could therefore remain unresolved until next summer, regardless of how quickly his club career progresses.

Tresoldi's growing reputation has been built on considerably more than one exceptional Champions League performance.

The striker joined Club Brugge from Hannover in 2025 for approximately €7.5m (£6.5m) and enjoyed an outstanding debut campaign in Belgium.

He scored 22 goals and registered six assists in 53 appearances across all competitions, helping Brugge win the Belgian league title and finishing as the competition's leading scorer.

His partnership with Christos Tzolis, who subsequently joined Arsenal, proved particularly effective during that successful campaign.

Tresoldi has maintained his goalscoring form this season, with nine goals in 11 appearances reported immediately after his hat-trick against Inter.

The forward's versatility is one reason for the widespread interest in his services.

Standing at 6ft 1in, he possesses the physical attributes to operate as a conventional centre-forward but prefers to move across the attacking third rather than remain permanently between opposition centre-backs.

His movement creates opportunities for teammates to attack central spaces, while his acceleration and willingness to press opponents make him an attractive option for clubs seeking a modern number nine.

The German youth international has also demonstrated an ability to deliver against high-profile opponents.

His three goals against Inter included a confidently converted penalty and two finishes from inside the penalty area, helping Brugge secure one of the most surprising results of the Champions League campaign.

The performance carried additional personal significance because Tresoldi grew up supporting Inter's city rivals AC Milan.

According to La Gazzetta dello Sport, his childhood admiration for Kaka, Filippo Inzaghi and Alexandre Pato helped establish his attachment to the Rossoneri.

His rise has been particularly remarkable considering that several Italian clubs, including Milan and Juventus, reportedly declined opportunities to recruit him during his youth.

Interest in the forward has since increased considerably.

Arsenal have reportedly considered a January approach, potentially offering Tresoldi the opportunity to reunite with Tzolis in North London, while Manchester United, Barcelona and Borussia Dortmund have also been credited with an interest.

Roma reportedly had a substantial summer proposal rejected, with Brugge reluctant to lose a player whose value has increased dramatically since his arrival.

Nevertheless, there is no indication that Tresoldi has agreed to join any of his reported suitors, and his international decision remains separate from any potential club transfer.

Klopp and Mancini will both hope to convince the striker that their respective national teams offer the best platform for his development.

For now, Tresoldi appears determined to keep his options open, with the European Under-21 Championship providing a natural deadline for one of international football's most intriguing eligibility decisions.

#130. Aston Villa transfer news: Alejandro Garnacho latest as Chelsea left with 'headache' over Argentine

By Darren Plant | 21 Oct 2026 15:09

Chelsea have reportedly been left increasingly concerned with Alejandro Garnacho's situation at Aston Villa.

The winger is currently hoping to be selected for the Champions League fixture against Viking FK on Wednesday night.

Unai Emery revealed at his pre-match press conference that one of his key attackers was among three players to suffer injury blows ahead of the contest in the West Midlands.

However, that will not necessarily mean that Garnacho moves up the Villa pecking order, a consequence of George Hemmings being in contention to return from a small layoff.

According to Football Insider, Chelsea are 'disappointed' with how Garnacho's time at Villa is unfolding.

Chelsea would have been encouraged last Wednesday when the Argentine was introduced as a late substitute in the 6-0 win over Fenerbahce.

Three days later, the 22-year-old was not part of the squad for the Premier League fixture at Newcastle United.

Garnacho will complete a permanent £43m transfer to Villa Park if he activates the contractually-agreed appearance threshold during his season-long loan.

With Villa facing Manchester City on Saturday, Garnacho will have accepted that he will realistically not be involved in that match.

However, as well as the fixture with Viking FK, Villa have a trip to Bournemouth in the EFL Cup fourth round over the next week.

If he does not feature in either or both matches, it is fair to assume that Emery has given up on the player when it comes to activating a long-term contract at Villa.

#131. Every left-back linked with Man Utd: How they are getting on and January transfer chances rated

By Lewis Blain | 21 Oct 2026 13:57

Manchester United's left-back problems are becoming increasingly difficult to ignore, with Luke Shaw and Patrick Dorgu both sidelined through injury.

Red Devils boss Michael Carrick is facing a defensive headache ahead of Wednesday's Champions League clash with Como, followed by a tricky Premier League meeting with AFC Bournemouth.

With Lisandro Martinez and Ayden Heaven also unavailable against Como, United's defensive depth is being severely tested, leaving the Englishman with precious few options.

A new left-back will surely be high on the agenda when the January transfer window opens, so with that in mind, Sports Mole assesses 12 players linked with a move to Old Trafford, looking at their current situations and rating their chances of joining in January 2027.

These ratings are subjective assessments of how realistic each transfer looks, rather than statistical probabilities. Factors such as current form, playing time, contract situations, reported interest and the selling clubs' willingness to negotiate have all been considered.

Ait-Nouri has struggled to establish himself at Man City this season, managing only two substitute appearances in the Champions League and zero minutes in the Premier League so far in 2026-27.

However, City spent around £31m on the Algerian in 2025 and tied him to a long-term contract, while selling to their Manchester arch-rivals would certainly be an obvious complication.

Davies has returned to Bayern's first-team picture and has already contributed three Bundesliga assists in the early stages of the campaign, despite his recent injury problems.

With the Canadian under contract until 2030, Bayern would have little incentive to lose an established defender midway through the season.

Lewis-Skelly remains part of Arsenal's first-team setup, although he has had a more rotational role in the Premier League this season, with 336 minutes across his first five top-flight appearances.

His ability to play at left-back and in midfield makes him invaluable to Mikel Arteta, and the Gunners would surely be reluctant to strengthen a direct rival by selling the England international.

Hall has continued to be a regular starter for Newcastle under Matthias Jaissle, featuring in all seven of their Premier League matches, playing every minute and contributing two goal contributions.

His new long-term contract, signed in September 2026, strengthens the Magpies' position further, making a January departure unlikely even with United's well-reported interest and desperate need for reinforcements.

Truffert has quickly established himself at Bournemouth, starting all seven of their Premier League matches and recording an important assist in their Europa League debut win over Real Sociedad.

The Frenchman has become an important part of the Cherries' defence since arriving from Rennes in 2025, and his contract until 2030 means United would probably need to offer a substantial fee, so he is a possible arrival but one that is still unlikely.

Robinson began the season as Fulham's first-choice left-back, starting their opening five Premier League matches before an ankle injury interrupted his progress. He returned to the starting XI for the 1-0 victory over Hull City on October 17th, so his fitness will be an important factor in any potential January interest.

His Premier League experience makes him an appealing option at Old Trafford, although the Cottagers would need to be convinced that selling a regular starter is worthwhile.

Antonee Robinson to Man Utd in January chances: 3/10.

Salinas has made an impressive breakthrough at Racing Santander, with the 19-year-old already making eight LaLiga starts and registering an assist.

His recent experience has included difficult results, including a heavy defeat against Celta Vigo, but regular senior football at such a young age makes him an intriguing long-term prospect for United rather than an obvious immediate solution.

Svensson has remained involved in Dortmund's first team, featuring in six Bundesliga matches and three Champions League outings during the opening stages of 2026-27.

His ability to operate at left-back while also providing flexibility in other defensive roles - as well as in midfield - is very attractive for a club like United, but Dortmund would have little reason to weaken their squad in January unless a compelling offer is made.

Seys is enjoying a productive start to the season, with four assists in eight Belgian league appearances and further involvement in two Champions League outings.

United have reportedly continued scouting the Club Brugge youngster after the summer window, but his development and importance to his current side mean Brugge may demand a sizeable fee to let him go.

Mitchell has started every one of Crystal Palace's seven Premier League matches this season and has scored twice - a brace in the 3-2 win over Fulham. The Eagles' recent 5-0 defeat to Brighton & Hove Albion was a setback, but Mitchell remains an established and proven first-team regular.

With his contract approaching its final months, United could have a credible opportunity to pounce in January if Palace cannot secure an extension.

Balde's situation at Barcelona has become one to monitor closely, considering he has only managed 12 minutes across the club's first eight LaLiga matches. Competition from Joao Cancelo and the emergence of La Masia wonderkid Xavi Espart have limited his opportunities, while Hansi Flick has acknowledged the difficulty of Balde's position.

If regular football remains elusive, a January move to Old Trafford, where he would undoubtedly start every week, could become more appealing.

Raum remains a regular starter and captain at RB Leipzig, having played the full 90 minutes in each of their opening six Bundesliga and two Champions League matches. Leipzig's form has fluctuated, including a 6-0 victory over Eintracht Frankfurt followed by a 6-1 defeat to Bayern Munich, but the Germany international's contract situation is the key factor.

His deal is due to expire in summer 2027, and unlike with Mitchell, United representatives are free to open talks over a summer free transfer at the turn of the year, leaving January as Leipzig's last opportunity to command any sort of fee if an extension cannot be agreed.

Every linked player rated - January window

Raum remains the most realistic candidate because United's reported interest coincides with a contract situation that could encourage Leipzig to negotiate.

Balde is one of the most intriguing alternatives, particularly if his lack of minutes at Barcelona continues, while Mitchell offers a proven Premier League option whose contract situation could make him attainable.

Seys and Svensson are also worth watching if United think more long-term, but given their immediate injury problems, it may push them towards a more established signing.

With January approaching, the key question is whether interest in Raum or Balde progresses into concrete negotiations.

#136. Man United head coach Michael Carrick addresses Red Devils future ahead of vital Champions League fixture

By Matt Law | 20 Oct 2026 19:32, Last updated: 20 Oct 2026 19:34

Manchester United head coach Michael Carrick has said that he 'feels backed' by the club during a difficult period.

The Red Devils have only won two of their first nine matches of the 2026-27 campaign, while seven points from seven matches in the Premier League has left them down in 13th spot in the table, five points behind fifth-placed Liverpool.

Carrick is under pressure to oversee a huge change in terms of results in the coming weeks, starting with Wednesday's Champions League clash with Como.

However, the Englishman has insisted that he 'feels backed' by the board.

"I don't need any reassurance. It's pretty clear. I know the role, I know my responsibilities, I don't need anyone to tell me when it's going well or when it needs to be better," Carrick told reporters during Tuesday's press conference.

"I know the realities and have no problem with that. I know we got to win football games for everything to flow and fit into place. I feel backed, yeah, why wouldn't I? I have a really good relationship with everyone at the football club. There are no issues on that side."

When asked about the changes from last season, which saw Carrick guide the Red Devils to a third-placed finish in the Premier League, he said: "It can be small things, you know? It can be small things. The games and the results, I'd say about fine lines, but ultimately that is football.

"So, as close as you can be, unless you don't get them fine lines in your favour, then you're further away. I think we were good at certain things of flipping them in our favour last year, and this time there's been things that's gone against us.

"Some of it we can do better with and we can help, but I don't think there's a major, major problem at all. I think we know what we need to get better at as a group and we're working on that, and fully believe that we can put that run together and we can get back to winning ways, and the group, the team, the squad is definitely capable of that."

Carrick also opened up on the team's struggles for clean sheets, producing only one shut-out this term - against Sabah in the Champions League.

"Yeah, you're dead right, I can't argue with that, you know? As a team in general play, not on play, I thought the other day we defended pretty well and didn't give much away," said Carrick.

"We know they were a good set-piece team and in the end that made a difference for them. So, there's definitely parts of our game that we have to improve to make things easier, fully aware of that."

Man United have a defensive crisis for Wednesday's game against Como, with Patrick Dorgu, Luke Shaw, Lisandro Martinez, Leny Yoro and Ayden Heaven all out of the contest.

#140. Cristiano Ronaldo ‘let Man United down’ as Erik ten Hag delivers critical verdict on legendary forward

By Oliver Thomas | 20 Oct 2026 13:06

Erik ten Hag believes that Cristiano Ronaldo let Manchester United down before his departure by mutual consent in November 2022.

Ronaldo made a sensational return to Old Trafford in August 2021 and ended his first season back at the club as their top scorer with 24 goals.

However, Man United’s failure to qualify for the Champions League led to the legendary forward wanting to leave in the summer of 2022 – a move which ultimately failed to materialise.

Ronaldo was then forced to play a role largely from the substitutes' bench under Ten Hag, who was appointed Red Devils boss in May 2022, and this led to tensions growing between the pair.

The relationship between Ronaldo and Ten Hag eventually broke down, and the former was handed a club suspension after refusing to come on as a substitute in a Premier League match against Tottenham – Ten Hag later told reporters: “I warned you (Ronaldo), now there are consequences”.

In an explosive interview with Piers Morgan just days before his Old Trafford contract was mutually terminated, Ronaldo said: "I don't have respect for [Ten Hag] because he doesn't show respect for me.

"If you don't have respect for me, I'm never going to have respect for you. Not only the manager, but the other two or three guys who are around the club.

"I felt betrayed. I don't care, people should listen to the truth. Yes, I felt betrayed. I feel that some people didn't want me here, not only this year but last season too."

Ten Hag, who was later sacked by Man Utd in October 2024, has since opened up on his time at Old Trafford and working with Ronaldo in an wide-ranging interview with The Athletic.

“He let the team down, and he let me down, because we planned with him,” said Ten Hag. “I flew out to Portugal to speak with him about what I was aiming for before I came to United.

“Obviously, he’s one of the best in football history. I respected him a lot, but at that time, as a manager, I had to make a rational decision and keep the players I admire in.”

Ten Hag claims that Ronaldo frequently withdrew from training sessions at Man United and failed to meet the required heart-rate thresholds during the practices he did attend.

Furthermore, following Man United's heavy 4-0 loss to Brentford in August 2022, Ronaldo allegedly completed only two of the three mandatory laps during the gruelling punishment run imposed by Ten Hag on the entire squad.

“I confronted him with this,” said Ten Hag. “Many times I say, ‘Ronnie, everyone wants to work for you to bring you in the right position, but if you can’t bring yourself in the box, you can’t score’.

“He was not capable to do this. It’s the wrong idea to put this to me that I expected him to do the pressing, that he’s exhausted, but he couldn’t. He ran neither in possession nor out of possession. The reason was because he didn’t prepare himself.

“Every top team has to embrace the top player, but the top player has to work for the team.

“He was telling the players, ‘Save your legs at the training’. I understand it when you are top fit; you have sessions, you can save the legs, but first you have to train yourself to get into the right fitness levels.”

Ten Hag believes that Ronaldo’s influence rubbed off on some of his Red Devils teammates, adding: “Players have to act, they have a big responsibility. Football is, for some, not the highest priority, and United for some wasn’t the highest priority.”

Ronaldo - who was recently punished by the Portuguese FA - left Man United to join Al-Nassr in January 2023 and has since scored 128 goals in 151 appearances for the Saudi Pro League side, winning his first title with the club last season.

Meanwhile, Ten Hag has also criticised Man United’s hierarchy, including Sir Jim Ratcliffe’s INEOS, for ‘breaking promises’ during his time at Old Trafford.

#141. “Embarrassed” Erik ten Hag accuses Man Utd, INEOS of ‘breaking promises’ before sacking

By Oliver Thomas | 20 Oct 2026 12:45

Erik ten Hag has hit out at his former club Manchester United for breaking “promises” made to him during his tenure at Old Trafford.

The 56-year-old won a EFL Cup and FA Cup during his two full seasons in charge of the Red Devils, but he was sacked just four months after lifting the latter trophy following a poor start to the 2024-25 season.

Ten Hag was left "completely devastated" and "ruined" by Man United’s decision to dismiss him according to his former teammate and close friend Hans Kraay Jr.

In a wide-ranging interview with The Athletic, Ten Hag detailed how Man United’s co-owners INEOS opted to trigger his one-year contract extension, just weeks after the Red Devils stunned rivals Man City in the FA Cup final.

“I wouldn’t go on with only one year left,” says the Dutchman. “So I said, ‘I need an extra year and we have to do it together’.

“They promised me loyalty, they promised me backing, they promised communication, that we get aligned. We spoke about the issues, but the promises, they didn’t keep.”

Man United were languishing in 14th place in the Premier League table on 11 points from nine matches when Ten Hag was sacked in October 2024, following a 2-1 defeat to West Ham.

“I think it was quite weird to separate ways in that moment,” Ten Hag said, pointing out Man United were still competing in every cup competition. “All the data was showing we are good. OK, two poor games to Liverpool and Spurs (both 3-0 home defeats). We were starting the process. Let it progress; the gameplay is getting better.”

“Maybe [the dressing room] can say differently, but I see on the pitch, and every game they fought. Also, my last game, that was probably one of our best games, we should have won against West Ham, but I’m sure the process was already ongoing; there were already people – maybe even in the team – who knew what would happen the day after.”

Ten Hag has said that he “can read situations” and sensed something was amiss behind the scenes before his sacking, while he has criticised Man United's hierarchy for their lack of communication and strategic continuity after Sir Jim Ratcliffe’s INEOS group took sporting control in December 2023.

Ten Hag noted that from their very first meeting at Carrington, the new ownership aimed to strip his power, saying: "[Ratcliffe] started talking about the structure in the football club, how the manager should be head coach... I said, 'OK, let’s discuss, but my contract is different.' I never heard anything.

“Then every (Man Utd) press conference, they ask me if the structure should be changed. I always want to cooperate in a club – you can’t do this on your own. You need to find a strategy about philosophy, game model, about team profile, individual profiles. That is what I was used to, but with them there was no communication.”

“The takeover process brought a lot of noise, uncertainty with the people, and disruptions,” he added. “So that was for the manager, for the coach, it was difficult to work, that’s clear. It was very complicated.”

Instead of seeking answers from the board shortly before his sacking, Ten Hag remained defiant, stating: "I was thinking I’m the best manager for United, so it doesn’t make sense to dive in. I was just focusing on my team.

"When you choose a manager, and he proves the last two seasons, winning trophies, getting into finals, then I don’t think it’s good club management to change.”

Another of those ‘promises’ broken by Man United, according to Ten Hag, was selling academy graduate Scott McTominay to Napoli for £25.7m in August 2024, having previously agreed that the club would not cash in on the midfielder.

“That’s why I got disappointed,” Ten Hag said. “In Ibiza, we agreed. I said, ‘No selling Scott McTominay’. We would go aligned in the transfer window. I make an argument, a reason: we don’t have scoring abilities.

“Scott brings scoring abilities and great mentality. He has United in all his veins. So when they bring up the subject, ‘No’. And in the end they sold him. I got, of course, very embarrassed and disappointed.”

Scotland international McTominay, 29, went on to become Serie A Player of the Year and a Scudetto champions with Napoli in his debut season at the club, while Man United opted to sign Manuel Ugarte as his replacement for around £50m from Paris Saint-Germain.

#142. Como vs. Man United: Red Devils head coach Michael Carrick has full-blown crisis for Champions League contest

By Matt Law | 20 Oct 2026 12:28, Last updated: 20 Oct 2026 12:33

Manchester United are facing a full-blown defensive crisis for Wednesday's crucial Champions League encounter with Serie A outfit Como.

The Red Devils trained on Tuesday morning and will fly to Italy on Tuesday afternoon ahead of the European fixture with Cesc Fabregas' side.

Leny Yoro will definitely miss the match through suspension, having been sent off in the team's 2-0 defeat to Atletico Madrid in the Champions League last week.

Meanwhile, Lisandro Martinez, who was forced off at the interval of Sunday's Premier League clash with Leeds United due to a thigh issue, is a major doubt.

Martinez did not train on Tuesday morning, while the same can also be said for Ayden Heaven, who was absent against Leeds last time out through illness.

Patrick Dorgu is again set to miss out with the hamstring injury that he suffered while representing Denmark during the September/October international break.

Luke Shaw was also missing from Tuesday's session, as the Englishman deals with his second injury problem of the campaign.

Matthijs de Ligt is now back in the fold, but the Netherlands international has not played for almost a year due to a chronic back injury.

As a result, Man United head coach Michael Carrick is facing a serious problem when it comes to selecting his defence for Wednesday's clash.

Harry Maguire is a certain starter, and he could be partnered by Noussair Mazraoui in the middle of the back four.

Mazraoui is a full-back by trade but can feature in the middle of the defence.

Diogo Dalot would then potentially operate at right-back, with Harry Amass or Jaydan Kamason in line to complete the defensive lineup.

Amass has already played in the Champions League this season, featuring against Sabah in the team's first match of the tournament, and the youngster could now be in line for a spectacular start against Como.

Marcus Rashford was also missing from training; the Englishman has been nursing a hip issue this season and could be absent alongside Kobbie Mainoo (unspecified).

Meanwhile, Benjamin Sesko continues to recover from a chronic shin issue, and it is unclear when the striker will be able to return to the field.

Man United head coach Carrick will answer questions on the injury situation during his press conference on Tuesday evening.
